Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Vinayapiṭaka

Vinayapiṭaka

Saṃgāyanassa pucchā vissajjanā

Questions and Answers concerning the Recitation

Okāsa dānakathā

Discourse on Granting Opportunity

Suṇātu [M 1.0001] me bhante saṅgho, mahatī ayaṃ parisā, evarūpāya parisāya na sukarā sabbaso kathaṃ sāvetuṃ, tasmā āyasmato ca javanattherassa pakhukkūnagaravāsino āyasmato ca vicittasārābhivaṃsassa tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikassa vinayapucchanabhārañca vinayavissajjana bhārañca āvahituṃ okāsaṃ dammi, karotha tumhe āvuso chaṭṭhasaṃgītipubbaṅgamāni vinayapucchana vinayavissajjana kiccāni yathādhammaṃ yathāvinayaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This assembly is large; it is not easy to make the entire matter heard by such an assembly. Therefore, I grant permission to the Venerable Javanatthera, resident of Pakhukkūnagara, and to the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the Tipiṭaka-holder, treasurer of the Dhamma, to undertake the burden of questioning on the Vinaya and the burden of replying on the Vinaya. Friends, perform the duties of questioning on the Vinaya and replying on the Vinaya, which are preliminary to the Sixth Council, in accordance with the Dhamma and the Vinaya.

Aggamahāpaṇḍita [M 1.0002] bhadantajavana mahātherena

By the Aggamahāpaṇḍita, the Venerable Javana Mahāthera

Ṭhapitā

Established

Pucchaka sammuti ñātti

Announcement of the Questioner’s Appointment

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ vinayaṃ puccheyyaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. If it is timely for the Saṅgha, I shall question the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the Tipiṭaka-holder, the treasurer of the Dhamma, on the Vinaya.

Tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārika

The Tipiṭaka-holder, Treasurer of the Dhamma

Bhadantavicittasārābhivaṃsena

By the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa

Ṭhapitā vissajjaka sammuti ñātti

Announcement of the Respondent’s Appointment

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ āyasmatā javanattherena saṅghanāyaka mahātherassa paṭinidhibhūtena vinayaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeyyaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. If it is timely for the Saṅgha, having been questioned on the Vinaya by the Venerable Javanatthera, the representative of the Saṅghanāyaka Mahāthera, I shall answer.

Paṭhama pārājika sikkhāpucchā

Question on the First Pārājika Training Rule

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0003] tena āvuso bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the first Pārājika laid down by that Blessed One, the knowing, the seeing, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Vesālī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, concerning whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sudinnaṃ bhante kalandaputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning Sudinna, the son of Kalandaka.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, in what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sudindo bhante kalandaputto purāṇadutiyikāya methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, Sudinna, the son of Kalandaka, engaged in sexual intercourse with his former wife. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – atthi āvuso tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti.

Question: Friend, is there in that case an original ruling, a supplementary ruling, and a ruling for a case that has not yet arisen?

Vissajjanā – ekā bhante paññatti dve anupaññattiyo, anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthi.

Answer: Venerable sir, there is one original ruling and two supplementary rulings; there is no ruling for a case that has not yet arisen in that case.

Pucchā – sabbatthapaññattinukho [M 1.0004] āvuso padesapaññattinukho.

Question: Friend, is it a ruling for all places or a ruling for a specific region?

Vissajjanā – sabbatthapaññatti bhante.

Answer: Venerable sir, it is a ruling for all places.

Pucchā – sādhāraṇapaññattinukho āvuso asādhāraṇapaññattinukho.

Question: Friend, is it a common ruling or an uncommon ruling?

Vissajjanā – sādhāraṇapaññatti bhante.

Answer: Venerable sir, it is a common ruling.

Pucchā – ekatopaññattinukho āvuso ubhatopaññattinukho.

Question: Friend, is it a ruling for one party or for both parties?

Vissajjanā – ubhatopaññatti bhante.

Answer: Venerable sir, it is a ruling for both parties.

Pucchā – pañcannaṃ āvuso pātimokkhuddesānaṃ katthogadhaṃ kattha pariyāpannaṃ.

Question: Friend, among the five recitations of the Pātimokkha, where is it grounded and where is it included?

Vissajjanā – nidānogadhaṃ [M 1.0005] bhante nidānapariyāpannaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it is grounded in the Introduction and included in the Introduction.

Pucchā – katamena āvuso uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Question: Friend, by which recitation does it come to recitation?

Vissajjanā – dutiyena bhante uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Reply: Venerable sir, it comes to recitation by the second recitation.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question: Friend, of the four downfalls, which downfall is it?

Vissajjanā – sīlavipatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is downfall in virtue.

Pucchā – kā āvuso vipatti.

Question: Friend, what is downfall?

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, lack of restraint is downfall.

Pucchā – kā āvuso sampatti.

Question: Friend, what is accomplishment?

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sammatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, restraint is accomplishment.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0006] āvuso paṭipatti.

Question: Friend, what is the practice?

Vissajjanā – na evarūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu, ayaṃ bhante paṭipatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, the practice is to undertake the training precepts for life, even at the cost of one's life, resolving: 'I will not do such a thing.'

Pucchā – kati āvuso atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, for how many reasons was the first Pārājika promulgated by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ, 1 - saṅghasuṭṭhutāya, 2 - saṅghaphāsutāya, 3 - dummaṅkūnaṃ puggalānaṃ niggahāya, 4 - pesalānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ phāsuvihārāya, 5 - diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ āsavānaṃ saṃvarāya, 6 - samparāyikānaṃ āsavānaṃ paṭighātāya, 7 - appasannānaṃ pasādāya, 8 - pasannānaṃ bhiyyobhāvāya, 9 - saddhammaṭṭhitiyā, 10 - vinayānuggahāya.

Reply: Venerable sir, the Blessed One promulgated the first Pārājika for ten reasons: 1. for the well-being of the Sangha, 2. for the comfort of the Sangha, 3. for the suppression of ill-behaved persons, 4. for the comfortable dwelling of conscientious monks, 5. for the restraint of defilements pertaining to this life, 6. for the warding off of defilements pertaining to future lives, 7. for the gaining of confidence by those without confidence, 8. for the increase of confidence in those who are confident, 9. for the establishment of the True Dhamma, 10. for the support of the Discipline.

Pucchā – ke [M 1.0007] āvuso sikkhanti.

Question: Friend, who trains?

Vissajjanā – sekkhāca bhante puthujjanakalyāṇakāca sikkhanti.

Reply: Venerable sir, trainees and virtuous ordinary people train.

Pucchā – ke āvuso sikkhitasikkhā.

Question: Friend, who are those who have completed the training?

Vissajjanā – arahanto bhante sikkhitasikkhā.

Reply: Venerable sir, Arahants are those who have completed the training.

Pucchā – kattha āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question: Friend, where is it established?

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is established in those who desire training.

Pucchā – ke āvuso dhārenti.

Question: Friend, who upholds it?

Vissajjanā – yesaṃ bhante vattati te dhārenti.

Reply: Venerable sir, those for whom it is applicable uphold it.

Pucchā – kassa [M 1.0008] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: Friend, whose words are these?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is the utterance of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question: Friend, by whom was it brought?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it has been handed down by tradition.

Saṃgāyanatthāya saṅghassa ñāpanaṃ

Announcement to the Sangha for the Purpose of the Recitation

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho ahaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ vicitthasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ paṭhamassa pārājikassa nidānampi pucchiṃ, puggalampi pucchiṃ, vatthumpi pucchiṃ, paññatti anupaññattiādīnipi pucchiṃ, puṭṭho puṭṭhoca so āyasmāvicittasārābhivaṃso vissajjesi, itihidaṃ bhante paṭhamaṃ pārājika sikkhāpadaṃ nimmalaṃ suparisuddhaṃ tasseva bhagavato vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa, tasmā yathāpure mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā brahmacariya saṅkhātassa buddhasāsanassa ciraṭṭhitiyā bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ dhammavinayaṃ saṅgāyiṃsuceva anusaṅgāyiṃsuca, evameva mayampi dāni sabbeva chaṭṭhasaṃgītimahādhammasabhāpariyāpannā idaṃ paṭhama pārājika sikkhāpadaṃ ekatosajjhāyanavasena saṃgāyeyyāma.

Venerable sirs, may the Sangha hear me. Venerable sir, I asked the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the Tipiṭaka-holder, treasurer of the Dhamma, about the origin of the first Pārājika rule, about the person, about the event, about the original promulgation, supplementary promulgations, and so on. And when asked, the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa explained. Thus, Venerable sir, this first Pārājika training rule is stainless and perfectly pure, being the very utterance of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. Therefore, just as formerly the great elder disciples headed by Mahākassapa, the ancient compilers of the Council, recited and re-recited the Dhamma and Vinaya for the long-lasting of the Buddha's Dispensation, which is called the holy life, for the welfare of many, for the happiness of many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans, so too, now, all of us gathered within this great Dhamma assembly of the Sixth Council should recite this first Pārājika training rule together in unison.

Revatābhi paṇḍitadhaja sāsanavaṃsa mahādhammarājaguruno

Of Revata, the Wise Banner-Bearer, the Sāsanavaṃsa, the Great Dhamma-King-Teacher.

Abhidhajamahāraṭṭhagurunoca saṅghanāyaka mahātherassa

and of the Abhidhajamahāraṭṭhaguru, the Sangha Leader, the Great Elder.

Okāsa dānakathā

Discourse on Granting Permission

Suṇātu [M 1.0010] me bhante saṅgho, mahatī ayaṃ parisā, evarūpāya parisāya na sukarā sabbaso kathaṃ sāvetuṃ, tasmā āyasmatoca sobhanassa sāsanari sārāmādhivāsino āyasmatoca vicittasārābhivaṃsassa tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikassa vinayapucchanabhārañca vinayavissajjanabhārañca āvahituṃ okāsaṃ dammi, karotha tumhe āvuso chaṭṭhasaṃgītipubbaṅgamāni vinayapucchana vinayavissajjana kiccāni yathādhammaṃ yathāvinayaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Sangha listen to me. This is a large assembly; in such an assembly, it is not easy to make the entire discourse heard. Therefore, I grant permission to the Venerable Sobhana, the resident of Sārāma, and to the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the Tipitakadhara and Dhamma-treasurer, to undertake the burden of questioning on the Vinaya and the burden of answering on the Vinaya. Friends, you should perform the duties of questioning on the Vinaya and answering on the Vinaya, as preliminaries to the Sixth Council, in accordance with the Dhamma and the Vinaya.

Aggamahāpaṇḍita bhadanta sobhanattherena

By the Venerable Aggamahāpaṇḍita Sobhana Thera.

Ṭhapitā pucchaka sammuti ñātti

The motion for the appointment of the questioner is established.

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ vinayaṃ puccheyyaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Sangha hear me. If it is the proper time for the Sangha, I would question the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the Tipiṭakadhara and Dhamma-treasurer, concerning the Vinaya.

Tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārika

The Tipiṭakadhara, the Dhamma-treasurer.

Bhadantavicittasārābhivaṃsena

By the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa.

Ṭhapitā vissajjaka sammuti ñātti

The motion for the appointment of the answerer is established.

Suṇātu [M 1.0011] me bhante saṅgho, yadisaṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ āyasmatā sobhanattherena saṅghanāyaka mahātherassa paṭinidhibhūtena vinayaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeyyaṃ.

Venerable sirs, may the Sangha hear me. If it is the proper time for the Sangha, I, having been questioned about the Vinaya by the Venerable Sobhana Thera, who is the representative of the Sanghanāyaka Mahāthera, will answer.

Dutiya pārājika sikkhāpucchā

Question on the Second Pārājika Training Rule.

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0012] tena āvuso bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dutiyaṃ pārājikaṃ kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, where was the second pārājika rule laid down by that Blessed One, the Knower, the Seer, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One?'

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante paññattaṃ.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, it was laid down in Rājagaha.'

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, concerning whom was it laid down?'

Vissajjanā – dhaniyaṃ bhante kumbhakāraputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning Dhaniya, the potter’s son.'

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, in what matter was it laid down?'

Vissajjanā – dhaniyo bhante kumbhakāraputto rañño dārūni adinnaṃ ādiyi, tasmiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, Dhaniya, the potter’s son, took timber belonging to the king that had not been given. It was laid down in that matter.'

Pucchā – atthi [M 1.0013] āvuso tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti.

Question: 'Friend, is there in that case a regulation, a supplementary regulation, and a regulation for a case not yet arisen?'

Vissajjanā – ekā bhante paññatti ekā anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthi.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, there is one regulation and one supplementary regulation; there is no regulation for a case not yet arisen in that case.'

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha mūlapaññatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is the root regulation in that case?'

Vissajjanā – yo pana bhikkhu adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyeyya, yathārūpe adinnādāne rājāno coraṃ gahetvā haneyyuṃvā bandheyyuṃvā pabbājeyyuṃvā corosi bālosi muḷhosi thenosīti tathārūpaṃ bhikkhu adinnaṃ ādiyamāno ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha mūlapaññatti.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, “Whatever bhikkhu should take what is not given, reckoned as theft—in such a kind of taking what is not given for which kings, having seized a thief, would execute, imprison, or banish him, saying, ‘You are a thief, you are a fool, you are deluded, you are a robber’—a bhikkhu taking what is not given in such a manner is also defeated and no longer in communion.” This, venerable sir, is the root regulation in that case.'

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0014] āvuso tattha anupaññatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is the supplementary regulation in that case?'

Vissajjanā – gāmāvā araññāvāti ayaṃ bhante tattha anupaññatti.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, “From a village or from a wilderness” is the supplementary regulation in that case.'

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is the complete regulation in that case?'

Vissajjanā – yo pana bhikkhu gāmāvā araññāvā adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyeyya, kathārūpe adinnādāne rājāno coraṃ gahetvā haneyyuṃvā bandheyyuṃvā pabbājeyyuṃvā corosi bālosi mūḷhosi thenosīti tathārūpaṃ bhikkhu adinnaṃ ādiyamāno ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, “Whatever bhikkhu, from a village or from a wilderness, should take what is not given, reckoned as theft—in such a kind of taking what is not given for which kings, having seized a thief, would execute, imprison, or banish him, saying, ‘You are a thief, you are a fool, you are deluded, you are a robber’—a bhikkhu taking what is not given in such a manner is also defeated and no longer in communion.” This, venerable sir, is the complete regulation in that case.'

Pucchā – sabbatthapaññatti [M 1.0015] nukho āvuso padesapaññattinukho.

Question: 'Friend, is it a regulation for all places or a regulation for a specific region?'

Vissajjanā – sabbatthapaññatti bhante.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, it is a regulation for all places.'

Pucchā – sādhāraṇapaññattinukho āvuso asādhāraṇapaññatti nukho.

Question: 'Friend, is it a common regulation or an uncommon regulation?'

Vissajjanā – sādhāraṇapaññatti bhante.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, it is a common regulation.'

Pucchā – ekatopaññattinukho āvuso ubhatopaññattinukho.

Question: 'Friend, is it a regulation for one community or for both communities?'

Vissajjanā – ubhatopaññatti bhante.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, it is a regulation for both communities.'

Pucchā – tasmiṃ [M 1.0016] āvuso dutiya pārājike adinnaṃ ādiyanto kati āpattiyo āpajjati vibhajitvā vissajjehi.

Question: 'Friend, in that second pārājika, when taking what is not given, how many offenses does one incur? Explain and answer in detail.'

Vissajjanā – adinnaṃ bhante ādiyanto tisso āpattiyo āpajjati, pañca māsakaṃvā atireka pañca māsakaṃvā agghanakaṃ adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyati āpatti pārājikassa, ūnapañcamāsakaṃvā atirekamāsakaṃvā adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyati āpatti thullaccayassa, māsakaṃvā ūnamāsakavā agghanakaṃ adinnaṃ theyyasaṅkhātaṃ ādiyati āpatti dukkaṭassa, adinnaṃ bhante ādiyanto imā tisso āpattiyo āpajjati.

Answer: 'Venerable sir, when taking what has not been given, one incurs three offenses. If one takes what has not been given, reckoned as theft, of a value of five māsakas or more, it is an offense of defeat. If one takes what has not been given, reckoned as theft, of a value less than five māsakas but more than one māsaka, it is a grave offense. If one takes what has not been given, reckoned as theft, of a value of one māsaka or less, it is an offense of wrong-doing. Venerable sir, when taking what has not been given, one incurs these three offenses.'

Pucchā – kesaṃ [M 1.0017] āvuso anāpatti.

Question: 'Friend, for whom is there no offense?'

Vissajjanā – dasannaṃ bhante puggalānaṃ anāpatti, sakasaññissa, vissāsaggāhena gaṇhantassa, tāvakālikaṃ gaṇhantassa, petapariggahaṃ gaṇhantassa, tiracchānagatapariggahaṃ gaṇhantassa, paṃsukūlasaññissa, ummattakassa, khittacittassa, vedanāṭṭassa, ādikammikassāti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, there is no offense for ten individuals: one who perceives it as his own, one who takes it with trust, one who takes it temporarily, one who takes it as belonging to the departed, one who takes it as belonging to an animal, one who perceives it as a refuse-rag, one who is insane, one whose mind is deranged, one afflicted by pain, and the first offender.'

Pucchā – pañcannaṃ āvuso pātimokkhuddesānaṃ katthogadhaṃ katthapariyāpannaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, of the five recitations of the Pātimokkha, in what are they based and in what are they included?'

Vissajjanā – nidānogadhaṃ bhante nidānapariyāpannaṃ.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, they are based in the introduction and included in the introduction.'

Pucchā – katamena [M 1.0018] āvuso uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Question: 'Friend, to which recitation does this belong?'

Vissajjanā – dutiyena bhante uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it belongs to the second recitation.'

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question: 'Friend, of the four failures, which failure is it?'

Vissajjanā – sīlavipatti bhante.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is a failure in virtue.'

Pucchā – sattannaṃ āvuso āpattikkhandhānaṃ katamo āpattikkhandho.

Question: 'Friend, of the seven classes of offenses, which class of offense is it?'

Vissajjanā – pārājikāpattikkhandho bhante.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is the class of offenses entailing expulsion.'

Pucchā – channaṃ āvuso āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti.

Question: 'Friend, of the six origins of offenses, from how many origins does it arise?'

Vissajjanā – tīhi bhante samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti, siyā kāyatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti, na vācāto, siyā vācātoca

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it arises from three origins. It may arise from body and mind, not from speech; or from speech and...

Cittatoca [M 1.0019] samuṭṭhāti, na kāyato, siyā kāyatoca vācātoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti.

...mind, not from body; or from body, speech, and mind.'

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso adhikaraṇānaṃ katamaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, of the four legal cases, which legal case is it?'

Vissajjanā – āpattādhikaraṇaṃ bhante.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is a legal case concerning an offense.'

Pucchā – sattannaṃ āvuso samathānaṃ katihi samathehi sammati.

Question: 'Friend, by how many of the seven procedures for settling legal cases is it settled?'

Vissajjanā – dvihi bhante samathehi sammati sammukhāvinayenaca paṭiññāta karaṇenaca.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is settled by two procedures: by a verdict in the presence and by acting on an admission.'

Pucchā – ko [M 1.0020] āvuso tattha vinayo, ko tattha abhivinayo.

Question: 'Friend, what is the discipline in this case, and what is the higher discipline?'

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante vinayo vibhatti abhivinayo.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, the regulation is the discipline; the analysis is the higher discipline.'

Pucchā – kiṃāvuso tattha pātimokkhaṃ kiṃ tattha adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, what is the Pātimokkha in this case, and what is the higher Pātimokkha?'

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante pātimokkhaṃ, vibhatti adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, the regulation is the Pātimokkha; the analysis is the higher Pātimokkha.'

Pucchā – kā āvuso vipatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is failure?'

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, lack of restraint is failure.'

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0021] āvuso sampatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is success?'

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sampatti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, restraint is success.'

Pucchā – kā āvuso paṭipatti.

Question: 'Friend, what is the practice?'

Vissajjanā – na evarūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu ayaṃ bhante tattha paṭipatti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, the practice in this case is this: one undertakes the training rules for life, until the end of one's days, thinking, "I will not do such a thing."

Pucchā – kati āvuso atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā dutiyaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, for how many reasons did the Blessed One lay down the second rule of expulsion?'

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā dutiyaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ. Saṅghasuṭṭhutāya saṅghaphāsutāya dummaṅkūnaṃ puggalānaṃ niggahāya pesalānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ phāsuvihārāya diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ āsavānaṃ saṃvarāya samparāyikānaṃ āsavānaṃ paṭighātāya appasannānaṃ pasādāya pasannānaṃ bhiyyobhāvāya saddhammaṭṭhitiyā vinayānuggahāya.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, the Blessed One laid down the second rule of expulsion for ten reasons: for the excellence of the Saṅgha, for the comfort of the Saṅgha, for the control of ill-behaved individuals, for the comfortable living of virtuous monks, for the restraint of the corruptions of the present life, for the prevention of the corruptions of a future life, for inspiring confidence in those who lack it, for increasing the confidence of those who have it, for the stability of the true Dhamma, and for the support of the discipline.'

Pucchā – ko [M 1.0022] āvuso sikkhanti.

Question: 'Friend, who trains?'

Vissajjanā – sekkhāca bhante puthujjanakalyāṇakāca sikkhanti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, both trainees and virtuous ordinary people train.'

Pucchā – ke āvuso sikkhitasikkhā.

Question: 'Friend, who are those whose training is complete?'

Vissajjanā – arahanto bhante sikkhitasikkhā.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, arahants are those whose training is complete.'

Pucchā – kattha [M 1.0023] āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, where is it established?'

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is established in those desiring training.'

Pucchā – ke āvuso dhārenti.

Question: 'Friend, who upholds it?'

Vissajjanā – yesaṃ bhante vattati te dhārenti.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, those for whom it is in effect uphold it.'

Pucchā – kassa āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: 'Friend, whose word is it?'

Vissajjanā – bhagavatā bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsammuddhassa.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it is the word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.'

Pucchā – kenāvuso [M 1.0024] ābhataṃ.

Question: 'Friend, by whom was it brought?'

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, it has been brought down by tradition.'

Pucchā – atthi nu kho āvuso tattha upanetabbaṃ vā apanetabbaṃ vā akkharapadapaccābhaṭṭhaṃ vā viraddhapadabyañjanaṃ vā.

Question: 'Friend, is there anything there to be added or removed, or with misplaced letters and words, or with incorrect phrasing and expression?'

Vissajjanā – natthi bhante.

Reply: 'Venerable sir, there is not.'

“Saṃgāyanatthāya saṅghassa ñāpanaṃ”

“Announcement to the Sangha for the purpose of recitation.”

Suṇātu [M 1.0025] me bhante saṅgho ahaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsa tipiṭakadharadhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ dutiyassa pārājikassa nidānampi pucchiṃ, puggalampi pucchiṃ, vatthumpi pucchiṃ, paññattimpi pucchiṃ, anupaññattimpi pucchiṃ, āpattimpi pucchiṃ, anāpattimpi pucchiṃ, aññānipi īdisāni pucchitabbaṭṭhānāni anekāni pucchiṃ, puṭṭho puṭṭhoca so āyasmā vicittasārābhivaṃso vissajjesi. Iti hidaṃ bhante idaṃ dutiyaṃ pārājika sikkhāpadaṃ nimmalaṃ, suparisuddhaṃ, tasseva bhagavato vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa, tasmā yathā pure mahākassa pādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā brahmacariyasaṅkhātassa buddhasāsanassa ciraṭṭhitiyā, bahujanahitāya, bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya, hitāya, sukhāya, devamanussānaṃ dhammavinayaṃ saṅgāyiṃsuceva anusaṅgāyiṃsuca, evameva mayampi dāni sabbeva chaṭṭhasaṃgītimahādhammasabhāpariyāpannā idaṃ dutiya pārājika sikkhāpadaṃ ekato gaṇasajjhāyaṃ katvā samaggā sammodamānā hutvā saṅgāyeyyāma.

Venerable sir, may the Saṅgha listen to me. Venerable sir, I asked the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the bearer of the Tipiṭaka and the treasurer of the Dhamma, about the origin of the second pārājika, the individual, the subject matter, the rule, the supplementary rule, the offense, the non-offense, and many other such points to be questioned. Being questioned thus, the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa answered. Thus, venerable sir, this second pārājika training rule is stainless, perfectly pure, the very word of that Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One. Therefore, just as in the past the great elder Mahākassapa and others, the foremost great elders, the compilers of the ancient recensions, recited and re-recited the Dhamma and Vinaya for the long duration of the Buddha’s dispensation called the holy life, for the welfare and happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans, so too may we, all now assembled in this great Dhamma assembly of the Sixth Council, having made a group recitation of this second pārājika training rule together, united and rejoicing, recite it.

Tatiya pārājika sikkhāpucchā

The Question on the Third Pārājika Training Rule

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0027] tena āvuso bhagavatā (pa) sammāsambuddhena tatiyaṃ pārājikaṃ kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the third pārājika promulgated by that Blessed One, the Perfectly Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was promulgated in Vesālī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, concerning whom was it promulgated?

Vissajjanā – sambahule bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was promulgated concerning a number of monks.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, in what matter was it promulgated?

Vissajjanā – sambahulā bhante bhikkhu aññamaññaṃ jīvitā voropesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, a number of monks deprived one another of life. In that matter, venerable sir, it was promulgated.

Pucchā – atthi [M 1.0028] nukho āvuso tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti.

Question: Friend, is there a rule, a supplementary rule, or an unarisen rule there?

Vissajjanā – ekā bhante paññatti, ekā anupaññatti, anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthi.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is one rule and one supplementary rule; there is no unarisen rule in this case.

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha mūlapaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the root rule there?

Vissajjanā – yopana bhikkhu sañcicca manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropeyya, satthahārakaṃvāssa pariyeseyya ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha mūlapaññatti.

Reply: 'Whatever monk should intentionally deprive a human being of life, or should look for a weapon-bearer for him, he is also defeated and no longer in communion.' This, venerable sir, is the root rule there.

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha anupaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the supplementary rule there?

Vissajjanā – maraṇavaṇṇaṃ vā saṃvaṇṇeyya maraṇāya vā samādapeyya ambhopurisa kiṃ tuyhiminā pāpakena dujjīvitena matante jīvitā

Reply: 'Or he might praise the beauty of death or incite someone to death, saying: “My good man, what is the use to you of this evil, miserable life? Death is better for you than life.”'

Seyyāti [M 1.0029] iti cittamano cittasaṅkappo anekapariyāyena maraṇavaṇṇaṃ vā saṃvaṇṇeyya maraṇāya vā samādapeyyāti ayaṃ bhante tattha anupaññatti.

‘“Better!”—thus with such a mind, such an intention, he might in various ways praise the beauty of death or incite someone to death.’ This, venerable sir, is the supplementary rule there.

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the complete rule there?

Vissajjanā – yo pana bhikkhu sañcicca manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropeyya, satthahārakaṃvāssa pariyeseyya, maraṇavaṇṇaṃ vā saṃvaṇṇeyya, maraṇāya vā samādapeyya, ambho purisa kiṃ tuyhiminā pāpakena dujjīvitena matante jīvitā seyyoti iti cittamano cittasaṅkappo anekapariyāyena maraṇavaṇṇaṃvā saṃvaṇṇeyya, maraṇāya vā samādapeyya, ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Reply: If any monk intentionally deprives a human being of life, or searches for an assassin for him, or speaks in praise of death, or incites him to die, saying, "My good man, what use is this wretched, miserable life to you? Death would be better for you than life," thinking thus with his mind and intention, in various ways he might speak in praise of death or incite him to die, he too is defeated, he is no longer in communion. This, venerable sir, is the complete rule there.

Pucchā – sabbatthapaññatti [M 1.0030] nukho āvusopadesapaññatti nukho.

Question: Friend, is it a rule for all cases, or a rule for a specific region?

Vissajjanā – sabbatthapaññatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a rule for all cases.

Pucchā – sādhāraṇapaññattinukho āvuso asādhāraṇapaññatti nukho.

Question: Friend, is it a common rule or an uncommon rule?

Vissajjanā – sādhāraṇapaññatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a common rule.

Pucchā – ekatopaññatti [M 1.0031] nukho āvuso ubhatopaññatti nukho.

Question: Friend, is it a rule for one side, or a rule for both sides?

Vissajjanā – ubhatopaññatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a rule for both sides.

Pucchā – tasmiṃ āvuso tatiya pārājike sañcicca manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropento kati āpattiyo āpajjati.

Question: Friend, in that third pārājika, how many offenses does one incur when intentionally depriving a human being of life?

Vissajjanā – sañcissa bhante manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropento tisso āpattiyo āpajjati, manussaṃ odissa papātaṃ khaṇati papatitvā marissatīti āpatti dukkaṭassa, papatite dukkhā vedanā uppajjati āpatti thullaccayassa, marati āpatti pārājikassa, sañcicca bhante manussaviggahaṃ jīvitā voropento imā tisso āpattiyo āpajjati.

Reply: Venerable sir, one who intentionally deprives a human being of life incurs three offenses: if, with a human being in mind, he digs a pit, thinking, "Having fallen, he will die," there is an offense of wrong-doing; when a painful feeling arises in the one who has fallen, there is a grave offense; when he dies, there is an offense entailing defeat. Venerable sir, one who intentionally deprives a human being of life incurs these three offenses.

Pucchā – kesaṃ [M 1.0032] āvuso anāpatti.

Question: Friend, for whom is there no offense?

Vissajjanā – sattannaṃ bhante puggalānaṃ anāpatti, asañcicca mārentassa, ajānantassa, na maraṇādhippāyassa, ummattakassa, khittacittassa, vedanāṭṭassa, ādikammikassa.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is no offense for seven kinds of persons: one who kills unintentionally, one who is unaware, one who does not intend to cause death, one who is insane, one whose mind is deranged, one who is afflicted by pain, and the first perpetrator.

Pucchā – pañcannaṃ āvuso pātimokkhuddesānaṃ katthogadhaṃ kattha pariyāpannaṃ.

Question: Friend, in which of the five recitations of the Pātimokkha is it grounded, in which is it encompassed?

Vissajjanā – nidānogadhaṃ bhante nidānapariyāpannaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is grounded in the introduction and encompassed by the introduction.

Pucchā – katamena [M 1.0033] āvuso uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Question: Friend, by which recitation does it come up for recitation?

Vissajjanā – dutiyena bhante uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Reply: Venerable sir, it comes up for recitation by the second recitation.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question: Friend, which of the four failures is it?

Vissajjanā – sīlavipatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a failure in virtue.

Pucchā – sattannaṃ āvuso āpattikkhandhānaṃ katamo āpattikkhandho.

Question: Friend, which of the seven classes of offenses is it?

Vissajjanā – pārājikāpattikkhandho bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is the class of offenses entailing defeat.

Pucchā – channaṃ āvuso āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti.

Question: Friend, of the six origins of offenses, by how many origins does it arise?

Vissajjanā – channaṃ bhante āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ tīhi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti, siyā kāyatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti, na vācato[M 1.0034], siyā vācatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti, na kāyato, siyā kāyatoca vācatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti.

Reply: Venerable sir, of the six origins of offenses, it arises from three origins: it might arise from body and mind, but not from speech; it might arise from speech and mind, but not from body; or it might arise from body, speech, and mind.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso adhikaraṇānaṃ katamaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ.

Question: Friend, which of the four legal issues is it?

Vissajjanā – āpattādhikaraṇaṃ bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a legal issue concerning an offense.

Pucchā – sattannaṃ [M 1.0035] āvuso samathānaṃ katihi samathehi sammati.

Question: Friend, of the seven means of settlement, by how many is it settled?

Vissajjanā – sattānaṃ bhante samathānaṃ dvīhi samathehi sammati, sammukhā vinayenaca paṭiññātakaraṇenaca.

Reply: Venerable sir, of the seven means of settlement, it is settled by two: by proceeding in the presence and by acting on an admission.

Pucchā – ko tattha āvuso vinayo ko tattha abhivinayo.

Question: Friend, what there is the discipline, and what is the higher discipline?

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante vinayo vibhatti abhivinayo.

Reply: Venerable sir, the rule is the discipline, the analysis is the higher discipline.

Pucchā – kiṃ āvuso tattha pātimokkhaṃ kiṃ adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Question: Friend, what there is the Pātimokkha? What is the higher Pātimokkha?

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante pātimokkhaṃ vibhatti adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, the rule is the Pātimokkha, the analysis is the higher Pātimokkha.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0036] āvuso vipatti.

Question: Friend, what is the failure?

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, lack of restraint is failure.

Pucchā – kā āvuso sampatti.

Question: Friend, what is accomplishment?

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sampatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, restraint is accomplishment.

Pucchā – kā āvuso tattha paṭipatti.

Question: Friend, what is the practice there?

Vissajjanā – na evarūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu ayaṃ bhante tattha paṭipatti.

Reply: “I will not do such a thing”—undertaking the training rules for as long as life lasts, even at the cost of one's life, this, venerable sir, is the practice there.

Pucchā – kati [M 1.0037] āvuso atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā tatiyaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, for how many reasons was the third pārājika rule laid down by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā tatiyaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ saṅghasuṭṭhutāya saṅghaphāsutāya (pa) vinayānuggahāya.

Reply: Venerable sir, for ten reasons was the third pārājika rule laid down by the Blessed One: for the well-being of the Saṅgha, for the comfort of the Saṅgha, (and so on), for the support of the Discipline.

Pucchā – ke [M 1.0038] āvuso sikkhanti.

Question: Friend, who are training?

Vissajjanā – sekkhāca bhante puthujjanakalyāṇakāca sikkhanti.

Reply: Venerable sir, both trainees and virtuous ordinary people train.

Pucchā – ke āvuso sikkhitasikkhā.

Question: Friend, who are those who have completed the training?

Vissajjanā – arahanto bhante sikkhitasikkhā.

Reply: Venerable sir, arahants are those who have completed the training.

Pucchā – kattha āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question: Friend, where is it established?

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is established in those who desire to train.

Pucchā – ke āvuso dhārenti.

Question: Friend, who upholds it?

Vissajjanā – yesaṃ bhante vattati te dhārenti.

Reply: Venerable sir, those for whom it is applicable uphold it.

Pucchā – kassa [M 1.0039] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: Friend, whose word is this?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is the word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question: Friend, by whom was it transmitted?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it has been transmitted through succession.

Pucchā – atthinukho āvuso tattha kiñci upanetabbaṃ vā apanetabbaṃ vā akkharapadapaccābhaṭṭhaṃ vā viraddhapadabyañjanaṃ vā.

Question: Friend, is there anything there to be added or removed, any misplaced letters or words, or any wrongly arranged words or syllables?

Vissajjanā – natthi bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is none.

Saṃgāyanatthāya saṅghassa ñāpanaṃ

An Announcement to the Saṅgha for the Purpose of Recitation

Suṇātu [M 1.0040] me bhante saṅgho, ahaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ tatiyassa pārājikassa nidānampi pucchiṃ, puggalampi pucchiṃ, vatthumpi pucchiṃ, paññattimpi pucchiṃ, anupaññattimpi pucchiṃ, āpattimpi pucchiṃ, anāpattimpi pucchiṃ, aññānipi evarūpāni pucchitabbaṭṭhānāni anekāni pucchiṃ, puṭṭho puṭṭhoca so āyasmā vicittasārābhivaṃso vissajjesi, itihidaṃ bhante tatiyapārājikasikkhāpadaṃ nimmalaṃ suparisuddhaṃ tasseva bhagavato vacanaṃ arahato sammāsammuddhassa, tasmā yathā pure mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā brahmacariyasaṅkhātassa buddhasāsanassa ciraṭṭhitiyā bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ dhammavinayaṃ saṅgāyiṃsuceva anusaṅgāyiṃsuca, evameva mayampi dāni sabbeva chaṭṭhasaṃgītimahādhammasabhāpariyāpannā idaṃ tatiyapārājikasikkhāpadaṃ ekato gaṇasajjhāyaṃ katvā samaggā sammodamānā hutvā saṃgāyeyyāma.

Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. I, venerable sirs, asked the venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the bearer of the Tipiṭaka, the treasurer of the Dhamma, about the origin of the third pārājika, the individual, the object, the enactment, the supplementary enactment, the offense, the non-offense, and many other such points to be questioned. Question by question, the venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa answered. Thus, venerable sirs, this third pārājika training rule is stainless, perfectly pure—the very word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. Therefore, just as the great elders, such as Mahākassapa and others, the ancient compilers, recited and reviewed the Dhamma and Vinaya for the long standing of the spiritual life known as the Buddha’s dispensation, for the welfare and happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans—so too, we, all gathered in this sixth great Dhamma council, having made a collective recitation of this third pārājika training rule, united and in harmony, should recite it.

Catuttha pārājika sikkhāpucchā

The Question about the Fourth Pārājika Rule

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0042] tena āvuso bhagavatā jānatā passatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena catutthaṃ pārājikaṃ kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the fourth pārājika rule laid down by that Blessed One, the knowing and seeing… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante.

Reply: At Vesālī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, concerning whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – vaggumudātīriye bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ.

Reply: It was laid down concerning the monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā River, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, in what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – vaggumudātīriyā bhante bhikkhū gihīnaṃ aññamaññassa uttarimanussadhammassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: The monks on the bank of the Vaggumudā River, venerable sir, praised one another's superhuman qualities to laypeople. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – atthi [M 1.0043] āvuso tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti.

Question: Friend, is there in this matter a rule, a supplementary rule, or an unarisen rule?

Vissajjanā – ekā bhante paññatti ekā anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthi.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is one rule, one supplementary rule, but no unarisen rule in that case.

Pucchā – katamā āvuso tattha mūlapaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the fundamental precept here?

Vissajjanā – yo pana bhikkhu anabhijānaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ attupanāyikaṃ alamariyañāṇadassanaṃ samudācareyya, itijānāmi itipassāmīti, tato aparena samayena samanuggāhīya mānovā asamanuggāhīya mānovā āpanno visuddhāpekkho evaṃ vadeyya ajānamevaṃ āvuso avacaṃ jānāmi apassaṃ passāmi tucchaṃ musāvilapinti ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha mūlapaññatti.

Answer: If a bhikkhu, without direct knowledge of a superhuman state, claiming for himself a distinction in knowledge and vision worthy of the noble ones, should declare, "Thus I know, thus I see," and later, whether questioned or not, desiring purification, should speak thus: "Friends, not knowing, I spoke thus, claiming to know, claiming to see what I did not see, speaking falsely, vainly," he too is defeated, he is no longer in association. This, venerable sir, is the original rule concerning that.

Pucchā – katamā [M 1.0044] āvuso tattha anupaññatti.

Question: What, friend, is the supplementary rule in that case?

Vissajjanā – aññatra adhimānāti ayaṃ bhante tattha anupaññatti.

Answer: "Except for conceit"—this, venerable sir, is the supplementary rule there.

Honti ye te ānanda bhikkhū adiṭṭhe diṭṭhasaññino apatte pattasaññino anadhigate adhigatasaññino asacchikate sacchikatasaññano adhimānena aññaṃ byākaronti, tañca kho etaṃ abbohārikaṃ.

There are, Ānanda, monks who, though they have not seen, think they have seen; though they have not attained, think they have attained; though they have not realized, think they have realized; though they have not directly experienced, think they have directly experienced. Out of conceit, they declare a higher knowledge—but this is worthless.

Pucchā – katamā [M 1.0045] āvuso tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the full rule in that case?

Vissajjanā – yopana bhikkhu anabhijānaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ attupanāyikaṃ alamariyañāṇadassanaṃ samudācareyya, iti jānāmi iti passāmīti, tato aparena samayena samanuggāhīyamānovā asamanuggāhīyamānovā āpanno visuddhāpekkho evaṃ vadeyya ajānamevaṃ āvuso avacaṃjānāmi apassaṃ passāmi tucchaṃ musā vilapinti, aññatra adhimānā ayampi pārājiko hoti asaṃvāsoti ayaṃ bhante tattha paripuṇṇa paññatti.

Answer: If a bhikkhu, without direct knowledge of a superhuman state, claiming for himself a distinction in knowledge and vision worthy of the noble ones, should declare, "Thus I know, thus I see," and later, whether questioned or not, desiring purification, should speak thus: "Friends, not knowing, I spoke thus, claiming to know, claiming to see what I did not see, speaking empty, false words," except for conceit, he too is defeated, he is no longer in association. This, venerable sir, is the full rule here.

Pucchā – sabbatthapaññatti [M 1.0046] nukho āvuso padesapaññatti nukho.

Question: Friend, is this a universally applicable rule or a locally applicable rule?

Vissajjanā – sabbatthapaññatti bhante.

Reply: It is a universally applicable rule, venerable sir.

Pucchā – sādhāraṇapaññatti nukho āvuso asādhāraṇapaññattinukho.

Question: Friend, is this a shared designation or an unshared designation?

Vissajjanā – sādhāraṇapaññatti bhante.

Reply: A common designation, venerable sir.

Pucchā – ekatopaññatti nukho āvuso ubhato paññatti nukho.

Question: Friend, is this a rule for one group or a rule for both groups?

Vissajjanā – ubhatopaññatti bhante.

Reply: It is a rule for both groups, venerable sir.

Pucchā – tasmiṃ [M 1.0047] āvuso catuttha pārājike asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ ullapanto kati āpattiyo āpajjati, vibhajitvā vissajjehi.

Question: In that fourth pārājika, friend, when one claims a non-existent, untrue superhuman quality, how many offenses does one incur? Explain and differentiate.

Vissajjanā – tasmiṃ bhante catutthapārājike asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ ullapanto tisso āpattiyo āpajjati, asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ ullapati āpattipārājikassa, yo te vihāre vasati so bhikkhu arahāti bhaṇati paṭivijānantassa āpatti thullaccayassa, appaṭivijānantassa āpatti dukkaṭassa, uttarimanussadhammaṃ bhante ullapanto imā tisso āpattiyo āpajjati.

Answer: In regard to that fourth pārājika, venerable sir, one incurs three offenses by falsely claiming a non-existent, untrue superhuman state. By falsely claiming a non-existent, untrue superhuman state, one incurs a pārājika offense. If one says, knowing, "The monk who lives in your monastery is an arahant," one incurs a thullaccaya offense; not knowing, one incurs a dukkaṭa offense. Venerable sir, by falsely claiming a superhuman state, one incurs these three offenses.

Pucchā – kesaṃ [M 1.0048] āvuso anāpatti.

Question: For whom, friend, is there no transgression?

Vissajjanā – channaṃ bhante puggalānaṃ anāpatti adhimānena bhaṇantassa anullapanādhippāyassa ummattakassa khittacittassa vedanāṭṭassa ādikammikassa.

Answer: For six types of individuals, venerable sir, there is no offense: one who speaks due to overestimation, one who has no intention of making such a declaration, one who is insane, one whose mind is deranged, one afflicted with pain, and one who is a beginner.

Pucchā – pañcannaṃ [M 1.0049] āvuso pātimokkhuddesānaṃ katthogadhaṃ kattha pariyāpannaṃ.

Question: Friend, where are the five recitations of the Pātimokkha rooted and encompassed?

Vissajjanā – nidānogadhaṃ bhante nidānapariyāpannaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is rooted in the origin and connected with the origin.

Pucchā – katamena āvuso uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Question: On what basis, friend, does the recitation proceed?

Vissajjanā – dutiyena bhante uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Reply: Venerable sir, it comes to the recitation by the second recitation.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question: Friend, which of the four failures is it?

Vissajjanā – sīlavipatti bhante.

Reply: It is failure in virtue, venerable sir.

Pucchā – sattannaṃ [M 1.0050] āvuso āpattikkhandhānaṃ katamo āpattikkhandho.

Question: Of the seven aggregates of offenses, friend, which aggregate of offenses is it?

Vissajjanā – pārājikāpattikkhandho bhante.

Reply: The category of offenses entailing expulsion, venerable sir.

Pucchā – channaṃ āvuso āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti.

Question: Friend, regarding the six sources of offenses, by how many sources is an offense produced?

Vissajjanā – channaṃ bhante āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ tīhi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti, siyā kāyatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti, na vācato, siyā vācatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti, na kāyato, siyā kāyatoca vācatoca cittatoca samuṭṭhāti.

Answer: Venerable sir, of the six origins of offenses, this offense arises from three: sometimes from body and mind, not from speech; sometimes from speech and mind, not from body; and sometimes from body, speech, and mind.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ [M 1.0051] āvuso adhikaraṇānaṃ katamaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ.

Question: Among the four kinds of disputes, friend, which dispute is it?

Vissajjanā – āpattādhikaraṇaṃ bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is a legal issue concerning an offense.

Pucchā – sattannaṃ āvuso samathānaṃ katihi samathehi sammati.

Question: Friend, by how many settlements are the seven kinds of settlements resolved?

Vissajjanā – sattannaṃ bhante samathānaṃ dvīhi samathehi sammati, sammukhāvinayenaca paṭiññātakaraṇenaca.

Answer: Venerable sir, the seven kinds of settlements are resolved by two settlements: by a verdict in the presence and by a procedure based on an admission.

Pucchā – ko tattha āvuso vinayo, ko tattha abhivinayo.

Question: What, friend, is the discipline in this case? And what is the higher discipline?

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante vinayo, vibhatti abhivinayo.

Answer: The formulation is the discipline, venerable sir, and the exposition is the higher discipline.

Pucchā – kiṃ [M 1.0052] āvuso tattha pātimokkhaṃ, kiṃ adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Question: What, friend, is the Pātimokkha in this case, and what is the higher Pātimokkha?

Vissajjanā – paññatti bhante pātimokkhaṃ, vibhatti adhipātimokkhaṃ.

Reply: The formulation, venerable sir, is the Pātimokkha; the analysis is the higher Pātimokkha.

Pucchā – kā āvuso vipatti.

Question: What, friend, is failure?

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Reply: Venerable sir, lack of restraint is failure.

Pucchā – kā āvuso sampatti.

Question: What, friend, is success?

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sampatti.

Reply: Restraint, venerable sir, is success.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0053] āvuso tattha paṭipatti.

Question: What, friend, is the practice in this case?

Vissajjanā – na evarūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu ayaṃ bhante tattha paṭipatti.

Reply: One trains in the training rules, having undertaken them with the thought, 'I will not do such a thing,' for as long as life lasts, even unto death. This, venerable sir, is the practice in this case.

Pucchā – kati āvuso atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā catutthaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, for how many reasons was the fourth rule of defeat laid down by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā catutthaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ, saṅghasuṭṭhutāya saṅghaphāsutāya…pe… vinayānuggahāya.

Reply: Venerable sir, the fourth rule of defeat was laid down by the Blessed One for ten reasons: for the excellence of the Saṅgha, for the comfort of the Saṅgha… for the support of the discipline.

Pucchā – ke [M 1.0055] āvuso sikkhanti.

Question: Friend, who are the ones who train?

Vissajjanā – sekkhāca bhante puthujjanakalyāṇakāca sikkhanti.

Reply: Venerable sir, both trainees and virtuous ordinary persons are the ones who train.

Pucchā – ke āvuso sikkhitasikkhā.

Question: Friend, who are those who have completed the training?

Vissajjanā – arahanto bhante sikkhitasikkhā.

Reply: Arahants, venerable sir, are those who have completed the training.

Pucchā – kattha āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, is it established?

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is established in those who desire the training.

Pucchā – ke āvuso dhārenti.

Question: Friend, who upholds it?

Vissajjanā – yesaṃ bhante vattati te dhārenti.

Reply: Venerable sir, those for whom it is current uphold it.

Pucchā – kassa [M 1.0056] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: Whose utterance is this, friend?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Reply: Venerable sir, it is the utterance of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question: Friend, by whom was it handed down?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it has been handed down by tradition.

Pucchā – atthi nukho āvuso tattha kiñci upanetabbaṃ vā apanetabbaṃ vā akkharapadapaccābhaṭṭhaṃ vā viraddhapadabyañjanaṃ vā.

Question: Friend, is there anything in it to be added or removed, or any letter or word that is misplaced, or any word or phrase that is defective?

Vissajjanā – natthi bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is not.

Saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

Questions on the Saṅghādisesa Training

1. Sukkavissaṭṭhisaṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

1. Questions on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule concerning the Emission of Semen

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0057] tena āvuso bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena upakkamitvā asuciṃ mocentassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa concerning one who intentionally emits semen laid down by the Blessed One, the Knower, the Seer, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paññatto.

Response: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante seyyasakaṃ ārabbha paññatto.

Response: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the Venerable Seyyasaka.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning what incident was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante seyyasako hatthena upakkamitvā asuciṃ mocesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Response: Venerable sir, the Venerable Seyyasaka intentionally emitted semen with his hand. It was laid down concerning that incident.

Pucchā – atthi [M 1.0059] āvuso tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññatti.

Question: Friend, is there in this case an original rule, a supplementary rule, or an unarisen rule?

Vissajjanā – ekā bhante paññatti, ekā anupaññatti, anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthi.

Response: Venerable sir, there is one original rule, one supplementary rule, and no unarisen rule in this case.

Pucchā – katamā āvuso tattha mūlapaññatti.

Question: Friend, what is the original rule in this case?

Vissajjanā – sañcetanikā sukkavissaṭṭhi saṅghādisesoti ayaṃ bhante tattha mūlapaññatti.

Response: Intentional emission of semen is a Saṅghādisesa. This, venerable sir, is the original rule in this case.

Pucchā – katamā panāvuso tattha anupaññatti.

Question: But what, friend, is the supplementary rule in this case?

Vissajjanā – aññatra supinantāti ayaṃ bhante tattha anupaññatti.

Response: Except in a dream. This, venerable sir, is the supplementary rule in this case.

Pucchā – katamā [M 1.0060] panāvuso tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Question: But what, friend, is the complete rule in this case?

Vissajjanā – sañcetanikā sukkavissaṭṭhi aññatrasupinantā saṅghādisoti ayaṃ bhante tattha paripuṇṇapaññatti.

Response: Intentional emission of semen, except in a dream, is a Saṅghādisesa. This, venerable sir, is the complete rule in this case.

Pucchā – sādhāraṇa paññattinukho āvuso asādhāraṇa paññattinukho.

Question: Friend, is it a common rule or an uncommon rule?

Vissajjanā – asādhāraṇapaññatti bhante.

Response: It is an uncommon rule, venerable sir.

Pucchā – tattha āvuso upakkamitvā asuciṃ mocento kati āpattiyo āpajjati.

Question: Friend, in this case, when one intentionally emits semen, how many offenses does one incur?

Vissajjanā – upakkamitvā bhante asuciṃ mocento tisso āpattiyo āpajjati, ceteti upakkamati muccati āpatti

Response: Venerable sir, when one intentionally emits semen, one incurs three offenses: if one intends, makes an effort, and emits, it is an offense

Saṅghādisesassa[M 1.0061], ceteti upakkamati na muccati āpatti thullaccayassa, payoge dukkaṭaṃ.

of Saṅghādisesa. If one intends and makes an effort but does not emit, it is a grave offense. For the attempt, it is an offense of wrong-doing.

Pucchā – kesaṃ āvuso anāpatti.

Question: Friend, for whom is there no offense?

Vissajjanā – channaṃ bhante anāpatti supinantena mocentassa na mocanādhippāyassa ummattakassa khittacittassa vedanāṭṭassa ādikammikassa imesaṃ kho bhante channaṃ anāpatti.

Response: Venerable sir, there is no offense for these six: one who emits in a dream, one who has no intention to emit, one who is insane, one whose mind is deranged, one who is afflicted with pain, and the first offender. Venerable sir, there is no offense for these six.

Pucchā – pañcannaṃ [M 1.0062] āvuso pātimokkhuddesānaṃ katamena uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Question: Friend, with which of the five Pātimokkha recitations does it come to be recited?

Vissajjanā – pañcannaṃ bhante pātimokkhuddesānaṃ tatiyena uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati.

Response: Venerable sir, it comes to be recited with the third recitation of the five Pātimokkha recitations.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question: Friend, which of the four failures is it?

Vissajjanā – catunnaṃ bhante vipattīnaṃ sīlavipatti.

Response: Venerable sir, it is the failure of virtue among the four failures.

Pucchā – kā āvuso vipatti.

Question: Friend, what is failure?

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Response: Venerable sir, lack of restraint is failure.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0063] āvuso sampatti.

Question: Friend, what is accomplishment?

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sampatti.

Response: Restraint, venerable sir, is accomplishment.

Pucchā – kā āvuso paṭipatti.

Question: Friend, what is the practice?

Vissajjanā – na eva rūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu ayaṃ bhante paṭipatti.

Answer: 'I will not do such a thing'—thus for life, until the end of breath, one undertakes and trains in the training rules. This, venerable sir, is the practice.

Pucchā – kati āvuso atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā upakkamitvā asuciṃ mocentassa saṅghādiseso paññatto.

Question: Friend, for how many reasons did the Blessed One lay down the Saṅghādisesa concerning one who intentionally emits semen?

Vissajjanā – dasa [M 1.0064] bhante atthavase paṭicca bhagavatā upakkamitvā asuciṃ mocentassa saṅghādiseso paññatto saṅghasuṭṭhutāya…pe… vinayānuggahāya.

Answer: Venerable sir, the Blessed One laid down the Saṅghādisesa concerning one who intentionally emits semen for ten reasons: for the well-being of the Sangha… for the maintenance of the Vinaya.

Pucchā – kattha āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question: Friend, where is it established?

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it is established among those who desire training.

Pucchā – ke āvuso dhārenti.

Question: Friend, who upholds it?

Vissajjanā – yesaṃ bhante vattati te dhārenti.

Answer: Venerable sir, those for whom it is applicable uphold it.

Pucchā – kassa [M 1.0065] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: Friend, whose word is this?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Answer: This, venerable sir, is the word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question: Friend, by whom was it brought?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it has been handed down through succession.

Pucchā – atthi nukho āvuso tattha sanidāne sapadabhājanīye savinītavatthuke upanetabbaṃ vā apanetabbaṃ vā akkharapadapaccābhaṭṭhaṃ vā viraddhapadabyañjanaṃ vā.

Question: Friend, is there in this case, with its origin, with its word-by-word analysis, with its incident of discipline, anything to be added or removed, or any mispronounced letter or word, or any faulty word or phrase?

Saṃgāyanatthāya saṅghassa ñāpanaṃ

Announcement to the Sangha for the purpose of recitation

Suṇātu [M 1.0066] me bhante saṅgho āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ paṭhamassa saṅghādisesa sikkhāpadassa nidānampi pucchiṃ, puggalampi pucchiṃ, vatthumpi pucchiṃ, paññattimpi pucchiṃ, anupaññattimpi pucchi, āpattimpi pucchiṃ, anāpattimpi pucchiṃ, aññānipi evarūpāni anekāni pucchitabbaṭṭhānāni pucchiṃ, puṭṭho puṭṭho ca so āyasmā vicittasārābhivaṃso vissajjesi, itihidaṃ bhante paṭhama saṅghādisesa sikkhāpadaṃ saparivāraṃ nimmalaṃ suparisuddhaṃ tasseva bhagavato vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa. Tasmā yathāpure mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā brahmacariyassa ciraṭṭhitiyā bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ [M 1.0067] dhammavinayaṃ saṃgāyiṃsuceva anusaṃgāyiṃsuca, evameva mayampi sabbeva chaṭṭhasaṃgītimahādhammasabhā pariyāpannā idaṃ paṭhamaṃ saṅghādisesa sikkhāpadaṃ nidānato paṭṭhāya ekaccānaṃ vacasā sajjhāyavasena ekaccānaṃ manasā manasikaraṇavasenaca ekato gaṇasajjhāyaṃ katvā saṃgāyeyyāma.

Venerable sirs, may the Sangha listen to me. I have questioned the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the bearer of the Tipiṭaka, the keeper of the Dhamma treasury, regarding the origin of the first Saṅghādisesa training-rule, the person involved, the incident, the original rule, its supplementary rule, the offense, the non-offense, and many other such points to be questioned. And the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa answered each question as it was asked. Thus, venerable sirs, this first Saṅghādisesa training-rule, with its retinue, is spotless and thoroughly purified—the very word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. Therefore, just as the great elders like Mahākassapa and other ancient council reciters recited and re-recited the Dhamma and Vinaya for the long-lasting continuance of the holy life, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of many people, out of compassion for the world, for the good, benefit, and happiness of devas and humans, so too should we all, gathered in this Sixth Great Dhamma Council, recite this first Saṅghādisesa training-rule together, starting from its origin—some by way of vocal recitation, and some by way of mental reflection—and having performed a group recitation, let us recite it together.

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0068] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena mātukāmena saddhiṃ kāyasaṃsaggaṃ samāpajjantassa ca, mātugāmaṃ duṭṭhullāhi vācāhi obhāsantassa ca, mātugāmassa santike attakāma pāricariyāya vaṇṇaṃ bhāsantassa ca, sañcarittaṃ samāpajjantassa ca saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, concerning one who engages in bodily contact with a woman, or who addresses a woman with lewd words, or who speaks in praise of attending on a woman for his own carnal pleasure in her presence, or who acts as a go-between?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paññatto.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī.

Pucchā – kaṃ [M 1.0069] āvuso ārabbha paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante udāyittheraṃ ārabbha paññatto.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the Venerable Elder Udāyī.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning what incident was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante udāyitthero mātugāmena saddhiṃ kāyasaṃsaggaṃ samāpajji, āyasmāyeva bhante udāyitthero mātugāmaṃ duṭṭhullāhi vācāhi obhāsi, āyasmāyeva bhante udāyitthero mātugāmassa santike attakāma pāricariyāya vaṇṇaṃ abhāsi, āyasmāyeva bhante udāyitthero sañcarittaṃ samāpajjiṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Answer: Venerable sir, the Venerable Elder Udāyī engaged in bodily contact with a woman; the Venerable Elder Udāyī addressed a woman with lewd words; the Venerable Elder Udāyī spoke in praise of attending on a woman for his own carnal pleasure in her presence; the Venerable Elder Udāyī acted as a go-between. It was laid down concerning that incident, venerable sir.

6. Kuṭikāra saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

6. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning the Building of a Hut

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0070] tena āvuso…pe… sammāsambuddhena saññācikāya kuṭiṃ kārāpentassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for one who has a hut built by solicitation?

Vissajjanā – āḷaviyaṃ bhante paññattho.

Answer: It was laid down at Āḷavī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āḷavike bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññatto.

Answer: It was laid down with reference to the monks of Āḷavī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kismiṃ [M 1.0071] āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āḷavikā bhante bhikkhū saññācikāyo kuṭiyo kārāpesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Answer: The monks of Āḷavī, venerable sir, had huts built by solicitation. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

7. Vihārakāra saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

7. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning the Construction of a Dwelling

Pucchā – yaṃ tena āvuso…pe… sammāsambuddhena mahallakaṃ vihāraṃ kārāpentassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for one who has a large dwelling built?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante.

Reply: At Kosambī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ [M 1.0072] āvuso ārabbha paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante channattheraṃ ārabbha paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down with reference to the Venerable Elder Channa, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kismiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmā bhante channo vihāravatthuṃ sodhento aññataraṃ cetiyarukkhaṃ chedāpesi gāmapūjitaṃ nigamapūjitaṃ nagara pūjitaṃ janapadapūjitaṃ raṭṭhapūjitaṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Reply: The Venerable Channa, venerable sir, while clearing a dwelling site, had a certain shrine tree cut down—one honored by villages, towns, cities, districts, and the realm. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

8-9. Duṭṭhadosa saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

8-9. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning Malicious Accusation

Pucchā – yaṃ tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena bhikkhuṃ amūlakena pārājikena dhammena anuddhaṃsentassa ca, bhikkhuṃ aññabhāgiyassa adhikaraṇassa kiñcidesaṃ lesamattaṃ upādāya pārājikena dhammena anuddhaṃsentassa ca saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for a monk who groundlessly accuses another monk of a Pārājika offense, and for a monk who, by seizing upon some trifling detail of another matter, accuses another monk of a Pārājika offense?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe [M 1.0073] bhante paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down at Rājagaha, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom and concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – mettiyabhūmajakā bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññatto, mettiyabhūmajakā bhante bhikkhū āyasmantaṃ dabbaṃ mallaputtaṃ amūlakena pārājikena dhammena anuddhaṃsesuṃ, teyeva bhante mettiyabhūmajakā bhikkhū āyasmantaṃ dabbaṃ mallaputtaṃ aññabhāgiyassa adhikaraṇassa kiñcidesaṃ lesamattaṃ upādāya pārājikena dhammena anuddhaṃsesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ, paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down with reference to the Mettiya-Bhūmajaka monks, venerable sir. The Mettiya-Bhūmajaka monks, venerable sir, groundlessly accused the Venerable Dabba the Mallian of a Pārājika offense. And those same Mettiya-Bhūmajaka monks, venerable sir, by seizing upon some trifling detail of another matter, accused the Venerable Dabba the Mallian of a Pārājika offense. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

10. Saṅghabhedaka saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

10. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning Causing Schism in the Saṅgha

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0074] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena saṅghabhedakassa bhikkhuno yāvatatiyaṃ samanubhāsanāya appaṭinissajjantassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for a monk who causes schism in the Saṅgha and does not relinquish it despite being admonished up to the third time?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down at Rājagaha, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ [M 1.0075] āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattho.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom and concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – devadattaṃ bhante ārabbha paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down with reference to Devadatta, venerable sir.

Devadatto bhante samaggassa saṅghassa bhedāya parakkami, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Devadatta, venerable sir, strove to create a schism in the united Saṅgha. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

11. Bhedānuvattaka sikkhāpucchā

11. Questioning on the Training Rule Concerning Following a Schismatic

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0077] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena bhedānuvattakānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ yāvatatiyaṃ samanubhāsanāya appaṭinissajjantānaṃ saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for monks who follow a schismatic, if they do not relinquish it despite being admonished up to the third time?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down at Rājagaha, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom and concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sambahule bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññatto, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū devadattassa saṅghābhedāya parakkamantassa anuvattakā ahesuṃ vaggavādakā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down with reference to many monks, venerable sir. Many monks, venerable sir, were followers and proponents of a faction, supporting Devadatta as he strove to cause a schism in the Saṅgha. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

12. Dubbaca, saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

12. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning Being Difficult to Admonish

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0078] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena dubbacassa bhikkhuno yāvatatiyaṃ samanubhāsanāya appaṭinissajjantassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for a monk who is difficult to admonish and does not relinquish it despite being admonished up to the third time?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down at Kosambī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ [M 1.0079] āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom and concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante channaṃ ārabbha paññatto, āyasmā bhante channo bhikkhūhi sahadhammikaṃ vuccamāno attānaṃ avacanīyaṃ akāsi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down with reference to the Venerable Channa, venerable sir. The Venerable Channa, venerable sir, when being spoken to by the monks in accordance with the Dhamma, made himself unadmonishable. It was laid down, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

13. Kuladūsaka saṅghādisesa sikkhāpucchā

13. Questioning on the Saṅghādisesa Training Rule Concerning Corrupting Families

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0080] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena kuladūsakassa bhikkhuno yāvatatiyaṃ samanubhāsanāya appaṭinissajjantassa saṅghādiseso kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where was the Saṅghādisesa laid down by that Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for a monk who is a corrupter of families and does not relinquish it despite being admonished up to the third time?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 1.0081] bhante paññatto.

Reply: It was laid down at Sāvatthī, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kaṃ ārabbha āvuso kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, with reference to whom and concerning what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – assaji punabbasuke bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññatto, assajipunabbasukā bhante bhikkhū saṅghena pabbājanīyakammakatā chandagāmitā dosagāmitā mohagāmitā bhayagāmitā pāpesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the monks Assaji and Punabbasuka. Venerable sir, the monks Assaji and Punabbasuka, upon whom an act of banishment had been carried out by the Saṅgha, went astray through desire, hatred, delusion, and fear, committing evil deeds. Concerning that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Aniyata sikkhāpucchā

Questioning on the Undetermined Training Rules

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0085] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena paṭhamo ca aniyato dutiyo ca aniyato kattha paññatto.

Question: Friend, where were the first undetermined rule and the second undetermined rule laid down by the Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paññatto.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Question: Friend, concerning whom and regarding what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āyasmantaṃ bhante udāyiṃ ārabbha paññatto, āyasmā bhante udāyī mātugāmena saddhiṃ ekoekāya raho paṭicchanne āsane alaṃkammaniye nisajjaṃ kappesi, tasmiñca vatthusmiṃ, āyasmāyeva bhante udāyī mātugāmena saddhiṃ raho nisajjaṃ akāsi, tasmiñca bhante vatthusmiṃ paññatto.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the Venerable Udāyī. Venerable sir, the Venerable Udāyī arranged to sit alone with a woman in a private, secluded seat suitable for misconduct, and concerning that matter; and also, venerable sir, the Venerable Udāyī himself sat alone in private with a woman, and concerning that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – kassa [M 1.0086] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question: Friend, whose utterance is this?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Reply: Venerable sir, this is the utterance of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question: Friend, by whom was it handed down?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it has been handed down through tradition.

Pucchā – atthi nukho āvuso ettha kocipi viraddhadoso.

Question: Friend, is there anyone here at fault for transgression?

Vissajjanā – natthi bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, there is none.

Saṃgāyanatthāya saṅghassañāpanaṃ

Announcement to the Saṅgha for the Purpose of Recitation

Suṇātu [M 1.0087] me bhante saṅgho, ahaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ vicittasārābhivaṃsaṃ tipiṭakadhara dhammabhaṇḍāgārikaṃ paṭhama duṭṭhadosa sikkhāpadato paṭṭhāya pucchitabbāni anekāni ṭhānāni pucchiṃ, so ca āyasmā puṭṭho puṭṭho vissajjeti, itihidaṃ bhante sabbaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ nimmalaṃ suparisuddhaṃ tasseva bhagavato vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa, tasmā yathāpure mahākassapādayo mahātheravarā porāṇasaṃgītikārā brahmacariyassa ciraṭṭhitiyā bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ saṃgāyiṃsuceva anusaṃgāyiṃsuca, evameva mayampi dāni sabbeva chaṭṭhasaṃgīti mahādhammasabhāpariyāpannā imāni sikkhāpadāni ekato gaṇasajjhāyaṃ katvā saṃgāyeyyāma.

May the Saṅgha, venerable sirs, hear me. Venerable sirs, I have questioned the Venerable Vicittasārābhivaṃsa, the bearer of the Tipiṭaka, the keeper of the Dhamma treasury, on many points to be questioned, starting from the first training rule concerning grave offenses. And that venerable one, when questioned again and again, answered. Thus, venerable sirs, this entire set of training rules is stainless and perfectly pure—the very words of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. Therefore, just as the great elder Mahākassapa and others, the eminent ancient compilers of the recensions, recited and reviewed for the long duration of the holy life, for the welfare and happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of devas and humans—so too, now, all of us assembled at this Sixth Great Dhamma Council, having collectively rehearsed these training rules, should recite them together.

Nissaggipācittiya

Rules of Forfeiture and Confession

1. Cīvaravagga 1. paṭhamakathina sikkhāpucchā

1. Chapter on Robes 1. Questioning on the First Training Rule

Pucchā – yaṃ [M 1.0089] tena āvuso bhagavatā…pe… sammāsambuddhena atirekacīvaraṃ dasāhaṃ atikkāmentassa nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the rule of forfeiture and confession laid down by the Blessed One… the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, for one who keeps an extra robe for more than ten days?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Vesālī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, concerning whom and regarding what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – chabbaggiye bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū atirekacīvaraṃ dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks kept extra robes. Concerning that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

1. Cīvaravagga 2. udosita sikkhāpucchā

1. Chapter on Robes 2. Questioning on the Training Rule concerning Staying Apart

Pucchā – ekarattaṃ [M 1.0090] āvuso ticivarena vippavasantassa nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the rule of forfeiture and confession laid down by the Blessed One for one who stays apart from the three robes for a single night?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī.

Pucchā – kaṃ āvuso ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, concerning whom and regarding what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sambahule bhante bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū bhikkhūnaṃ hatthe cīvaraṃ nikkhipitvā santaruttarena janapadacārikaṃ pakkamiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several monks. Venerable sir, several monks, having left robes in the hands of other monks, departed on wanderings in the countryside with only an under-robe and an upper-robe. Concerning that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

1. Cīvaravagga 3. tatiyakathina sikkhāpucchā

1. Chapter on Robes 3. Questioning on the Third Training Rule

Pucchā – akālacīvaraṃ [M 1.0091] āvuso paṭiggahetvā māsaṃ atikkāmentassa nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the rule of forfeiture and confession laid down by the Blessed One concerning one who, having received an out-of-season robe, keeps it for more than a month?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī.
