Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning many bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, many bhikkhunīs entered a monastery without asking permission. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 2. dutiya sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 2. The Second Training Question.

Pucchā – bhikkhuṃ [M 1.0226] āvuso akkosantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who abuses a bhikkhu? Concerning whom and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo āyasmantaṃ upāliṃ akkosiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Vesālī concerning the group of six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group of six bhikkhunīs abused Venerable Upāli. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 3. tatiya sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 3. The Third Training Question.

Pucchā – caṇḍikatāya [M 1.0227] āvuso gaṇaṃ paribhāsantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who abuses a group out of anger? Concerning whom and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante thullanandaṃ bhikkhuniṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, thullanandā bhante bhikkhunī caṇḍikatāya gaṇaṃ paribhāsi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the bhikkhunī Thullanandā. Venerable sir, the bhikkhunī Thullanandā abused a group out of anger. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 5. pañcama sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 5. The Fifth Training Question.

Pucchā – kulaṃ [M 1.0228] āvuso maccharāyantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who is stingy towards a family? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuniṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññatarā bhante bhikkhunī kulaṃ maccharāyi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning a certain bhikkhunī. Venerable sir, a certain bhikkhunī was stingy towards a family. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 6. chaṭṭha sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 6. The Sixth Training Question.

Pucchā – abhikkhuke [M 1.0229] āvuso āvāse vassaṃ vasantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who spends the rains in a residence without bhikkhus? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo abhikkhuke āvāse vassaṃ vasiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning several bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, several bhikkhunīs spent the rains in a residence without bhikkhus. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 7. sattama sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 7. The Seventh Training Question.

Pucchā – vassaṃ [M 1.0230] vuṭṭhāya āvuso bhikkhuniyā ubhatosaṅghe tīhi ṭhānehi na pavārentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who, after the rains retreat, does not invite criticism in both communities on three grounds? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo vassaṃ vuṭṭhā bhikkhusaṅghaṃ na pavāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning several bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, several bhikkhunīs, after completing the rains retreat, did not invite the community of bhikkhus. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 8. aṭṭhama sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 8. The Eighth Training Question.

Pucchā – ovādāya vā āvuso saṃvāsāya vā nagacchantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who does not go for instruction or for communion? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sakkesu bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo ovādaṃ nagacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established among the Sakyans concerning the group of six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group of six bhikkhunīs did not go for instruction. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

6. Ārāmavagga, 9. navama sikkhāpucchā

6. The Chapter on Monasteries, 9. The Ninth Training Question.

Pucchā – uposathampi [M 1.0231] āvuso na pucchantiyā ovādampi na yācantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who does not ask about the Uposatha and does not request instruction? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo uposathaṃ na pucchiṃsu, ovādampi na yāciṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning several bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, several bhikkhunīs did not ask about the Uposatha, nor did they request instruction. It was established, venerable sir, concerning that matter.

7. Gabbhinīvagga, 1-2. paṭhama, dutiya sikkhāpucchā

7. The Chapter on Pregnant Women, 1-2. The First and Second Training Questions.

Pucchā – gabbhiniṃ vā āvuso pāyantiṃ vā vuṭṭhāpentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the training rule entailing expiation established for a bhikkhunī who ordains a pregnant woman or a nursing woman? Concerning whom, and in what matter was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 1.0232] bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo gabbhiniṃ vuṭṭhāpesuṃ, pāyantiṃ vuṭṭhāpesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning several bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, several bhikkhunīs ordained a pregnant woman and ordained a nursing woman. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

7. Gabbhinīvagga, 3. tatiya sikkhāpucchā

7. The Chapter on Pregnant Women, 3. The Third Training Question

Pucchā – dve vassāni āvuso chasu dhammesu asikkhitasikkhaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who ordains a probationer who has not trained in the six rules for two years established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo dve vassāni chasu dhammesu asikkhitasikkhaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning several bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, several bhikkhunīs ordained a probationer who had not trained in the six rules for two years. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

8. Kumārībhūtavagga, 9. navama sikkhāpucchā

8. The Chapter on Young Women, 9. The Ninth Training Question

Pucchā – purisasaṃsaṭṭhaṃ [M 1.0233] āvuso kumārakasaṃsaṭṭhaṃ caṇḍiṃ sokāvāsaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who ordains a probationer who associates with men, associates with boys, is fierce, and prone to sorrow established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante thullanandaṃ bhikkhuniṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, thullanandā bhante bhikkhunī purisasaṃsaṭṭhaṃ kumārakasaṃsaṭṭhaṃ caṇḍiṃ sokāvāsaṃ caṇḍakāḷiṃ sekkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the bhikkhunī Thullanandā. Venerable sir, the bhikkhunī Thullanandā ordained the probationer Caṇḍakāḷī, who associated with men, associated with boys, was fierce, and prone to sorrow. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

8. Kumārībhūtavagga, 10. dasama sikkhāpucchā

8. The Chapter on Young Women, 10. The Tenth Training Question

Pucchā – mātāpitūhi [M 1.0234] vā āvuso sāmikena vā ananuññātaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who ordains a probationer not permitted by her parents or husband established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante thullanandaṃ bhikkhuniṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, thullanandā bhante bhikkhunī mātāpitūhipi sāmikenapi ananuññātaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the bhikkhunī Thullanandā. Venerable sir, the bhikkhunī Thullanandā ordained a probationer who had not been permitted by her parents and husband. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

9. Chattupāhanavagga, 1-2. paṭhama, dutiya sikkhāpucchā

9. The Chapter on Umbrellas and Sandals, 1-2. The First and Second Training Questions

Pucchā – chattupāhanaṃ āvuso dhārentiyā ca yānena yāyantiyā ca pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning one who uses an umbrella and sandals and one who travels by vehicle established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo chattupāhanaṃ dhāresuṃ, yānena yāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group-of-six bhikkhunīs used umbrellas and sandals and traveled by vehicle. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

9. Chattupāhanavagga, 4. catuttha sikkhāpucchā

9. The Chapter on Umbrellas and Sandals, 4. The Fourth Training Question

Pucchā – itthālaṅkāraṃ [M 1.0235] āvuso dhārentiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning one who wears women's ornaments established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo itthālaṅkāraṃ dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group-of-six bhikkhunīs wore women's ornaments. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

Pucchā – bhikkhu pana āvuso purisālaṅkāraṃ dhārento kiṃ nāma āpattiṃ āpajjeyya.

Question – But friend, what offense would a bhikkhu incur if he were to wear men's ornaments?

Vissajjanā – dukkaṭaṃ bhante āpattiṃ āpajjeyya.

Reply – He would incur a dukkaṭa offense, venerable sir.

9. Chattupāhanavagga, 5. pañcama sikkhāpucchā

9. The Chapter on Umbrellas and Sandals, 5. The Fifth Training Question

Pucchā – gandhavaṇṇakena [M 1.0236] āvuso nahāyantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning one who bathes with perfumed powder established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo gandhavaṇṇakena nahāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group-of-six bhikkhunīs bathed with perfumed powder. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

9. Chattupāhanavagga, 11. ekādasama sikkhāpucchā

9. The Chapter on Umbrellas and Sandals, 11. The Eleventh Training Question

Pucchā – bhikkhussa āvuso purato anāpucchā āsane nisīdantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who sits on a seat in front of a bhikkhu without asking permission established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo bhikkhussa purato anāpucchā āsane nisīdiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning many bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, many bhikkhunīs sat on seats in front of a bhikkhu without asking permission. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

9. Chattupāhanavagga, 12. dvādasama sikkhāpucchā

9. The Chapter on Umbrellas and Sandals, 12. The Twelfth Training Question

Pucchā – anokāsakataṃ [M 1.0237] āvuso bhikkhuṃ pañhaṃ pucchantiyā pācittiyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pācittiya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who asks a bhikkhu a question without having been given an opportunity established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahulā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhuniyo anokāsakataṃ bhikkhuṃ pañhaṃ pucchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning many bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, many bhikkhunīs asked a bhikkhu a question without having been given an opportunity. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

Pucchā – sappiṃ vā āvuso telaṃ vā madhuṃ vā phāṇitaṃ vā macchaṃ vā maṃsaṃ vā khīraṃ vā dadhiṃ vā viññāpetvā bhuñjantiyā pāṭidesanīyaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the pāṭidesanīya rule concerning a bhikkhunī who, having requested them, consumes ghee, oil, honey, molasses, fish, meat, milk, or curds established? Concerning whom, and in what connection was it established?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyā bhikkhuniyo ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhuniyo sappimpi telampi madhumpi phāṇitampi macchampi maṃsampi khīrampi dadhimpi viññāpetvā bhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was established at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six bhikkhunīs. Venerable sir, the group-of-six bhikkhunīs, having requested ghee, oil, honey, molasses, fish, meat, milk, and curds, consumed them. It was established in that connection, venerable sir.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ [M 1.0238] āvuso vipattīnaṃ katamā vipatti.

Question – Friend, of the four failures, which is the failure?

Vissajjanā – ācāravipatti bhante.

Reply – Venerable sir, it is a failure in conduct.

Pucchā – channaṃ āvuso āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti.

Question – Friend, of the six origins of offenses, by how many origins does an offense arise?

Vissajjanā – catūhi bhante samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti, siyā kāyato samuṭṭhāti, navācato nacittato, siyā kāyato ca vācato ca samuṭṭhāti, nacittato, siyā kāyato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhāti, navācato, siyā kāyato ca vācato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhāti, imehi bhante catūhi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhāti.

Reply – Venerable sir, it arises from four origins: sometimes from the body, not from speech or mind; sometimes from the body and speech, not from the mind; sometimes from the body and mind, not from speech; sometimes from the body, speech, and mind. Venerable sir, it arises from these four origins.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0239] āvuso vipatti.

Question – What, friend, is failure?

Vissajjanā – asaṃvaro bhante vipatti.

Reply – Venerable sir, lack of restraint is failure.

Pucchā – kā āvuso sampatti.

Question – What, friend, is success?

Vissajjanā – saṃvaro bhante sampatti.

Reply – Venerable sir, restraint is accomplishment.

Pucchā – kā [M 1.0240] āvuso paṭipatti.

Question – What, friend, is the practice?

Vissajjanā – na evarūpaṃ karissāmīti yāvajīvaṃ āpāṇakoṭikaṃ samādāya sikkhati sikkhāpadesu, ayaṃ bhante paṭipatti.

Reply – Venerable sir, one trains in the training rules, having undertaken them for life, until the end of one's breath, with the resolve, 'I will not do such a thing.' This, venerable sir, is the practice.

Pucchā – kassa āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question – Whose word is it, friend?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Reply – Venerable sir, it is the word of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question – By what was it brought, friend?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it has been handed down through succession.

Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho…pe… saṃgāyeyyāma.

Venerable sir, may the Saṅgha hear me… and so on… let us recite.

Mahāvaggapāḷi

The Text of the Great Division

Saṃgāyanassa pucchā vissajjanā

Questions and Answers on the Recitation

Pucchā – ubhato [M 2.0001] vibhaṅgānantaraṃ āvuso porāṇakehi saṃgītikāramahātherehi kaṃ nāma pāvacanaṃ saṃgītaṃ.

Question – Friends, after the two Vibhaṅgas, what teaching was recited by the great elders of old who were the reciters?

Vissajjanā – ubhato vibhaṅgānantaraṃ bhante porāṇakehi saṃgītikāramahātherehi khandhakā saṃgītā.

Reply – Venerable sirs, after the two Vibhaṅgas, the Khandhakas were recited by the great elders of old who were the reciters.

Pucchā – te pana āvuso khandhakā vaggabhedena katividhā.

Question – But friends, of how many kinds are those Khandhakas, by way of division into sections?

Vissajjanā – te [M 2.0002] pana bhante khandhakā duvidhā vaggabhedena mahāvaggo cūḷavaggoti.

Reply – But venerable sirs, those Khandhakas are of two kinds by way of division into sections: the Mahāvagga and the Cūḷavagga.

Pucchā – mahāvagge āvuso kati khandhakā pariyāpannā.

Question – Friends, how many Khandhakas are included in the Mahāvagga?

Vissajjanā – mahāvagge bhante dasakhandhakā pariyāpannā, seyyathidaṃ, mahākhandhako uposathakkhandhako vassūpanāyikakkhandhako pavāraṇākkhandhako cammakkhandhako bhesajjakkhandhako kathinakkhandhako cīvarakkhandhako campeyyakkhandhako kosambakakkhandhakoti.

Reply – Venerable sirs, ten Khandhakas are included in the Mahāvagga, namely: the Mahākhandhaka, the Uposathakkhandhaka, the Vassūpanāyikakkhandhaka, the Pavāraṇākkhandhaka, the Cammakkhandhaka, the Bhesajjakkhandhaka, the Kathinakkhandhaka, the Cīvarakkhandhaka, the Campeyyakkhandhaka, and the Kosambakakkhandhaka.

Pucchā – mahākhandhake āvuso bahū kathāyo, katamā tāsaṃ ādikathā.

Question – Friends, there are many narratives in the Mahākhandhaka. Which is the first narrative among them?

Vissajjanā – mahākhandhake bhante bodhikathā ādi.

Reply – Venerable sirs, in the Mahākhandhaka, the Bodhi narrative is the first.

Mahākhandhaka

The Great Chapter

Mahābodhipucchā

Questions on the Great Enlightenment

Pucchā – tasmātiha [M 2.0003] āvuso tato paṭṭhāya taṃ pucchissāmi, bhagavā āvuso paṭhamābhisambuddhakāle kattha kīvacīraṃ kenākārena vihāsi.

Question – Therefore, friends, from that point on I will ask you this: at the time of his first supreme enlightenment, where, for how long, and in what manner did the Blessed One dwell?

Vissajjanā – bhagavā bhante paṭhamābhisambuddho bodhirukkhamūle sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena vihāsi vimuttisukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedī.

Reply – Venerable sirs, the Blessed One, having first become fully enlightened, dwelt for seven days at the foot of the Bodhi tree in a single cross-legged posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation.

Pucchā – tadā āvuso bhagavā kīdisaṃ dhammaṃ manasikatvā kīdisaṃ udānaṃ udānesi.

Question – Then, friends, having attended in his mind to what teaching, what inspired utterance did the Blessed One utter?

Vissajjanā – tadā bhante bhagavā rattiyā paṭhamaṃ yāmaṃ rattiyā majjhimaṃ yāmaṃ rattiyā pacchimaṃ yāmaṃ paṭiccasamuppādaṃ anulomaṃ paṭilomaṃ manasikatvā tīṇi udānāni udānesi.

Reply – At that time, venerable sir, the Blessed One, having attended in his mind to dependent origination in forward and reverse order during the first watch of the night, the middle watch of the night, and the last watch of the night, uttered three inspired utterances.

Ajapāla

Ajapāla

Pucchā – tassa [M 2.0004] āvuso sattāhassa accayena bhagavā kattha vihāsi.

Question: Friends, after the passing of that week, where did the Blessed One dwell?

Vissajjanā – tassa bhante sattāhassa accayena bhagavā ajapāla nigrodhamūle vihāsi, sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena vihāsi vimuttisukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedī.

Answer: Venerable sir, after the passing of that week, the Blessed One dwelt at the foot of the Ajapāla Banyan tree, dwelling for a week in a single posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation.

Mucalindā

Mucalinda

Pucchā – tassapi āvuso sattāhassa accayena bhagavā kattha vihāsi.

Question: And friends, after the passing of that week, where did the Blessed One dwell?

Vissajjanā – tassa [M 2.0005] bhante sattāhassa accayena bhagavā mucalindamūle sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisīdi.

Answer: Venerable sir, after the passing of that week, the Blessed One sat at the foot of the Mucalinda tree for a week in a single posture.

Pucchā – tassapi āvuso sattāhassa accayena bhagavā kattha vihāsi.

Question: And friends, after the passing of that week, where did the Blessed One dwell?

Vissajjanā – tassapi bhante sattāhassa accayena bhagavā rājāyatanamūle sattāhaṃ ekapallaṅkena nisīdi vimuttisukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedī.

Answer: Venerable sir, after the passing of that week also, the Blessed One sat at the foot of the Rājāyatana tree for a week in a single posture, experiencing the bliss of liberation.

Pucchā – tassapi [M 2.0006] āvuso sattāhassa accayena bhagavā kattha vihāsi.

Question: And friends, after the passing of that week, where did the Blessed One dwell?

Vissajjanā – tassa bhante sattāhassa accayena bhagavā punadeva ajapālanigrodhamūle vihāsi.

Answer: Venerable sir, after the passing of that week, the Blessed One again dwelt at the foot of the Ajapāla Banyan tree.

Pucchā – tasmiṃ pana āvuso ajapālanigrodhe viharantassa bhagavato kathaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi.

Question: Friends, while the Blessed One was dwelling at the Ajapāla Banyan tree, what reflection arose in his mind?

Vissajjanā – tasmiṃ pana bhante ajapālanigrodhe viharantassa bhagavato rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa dhammagambhīratāpaṭisaṃyutto cetaso parivitakko udapādi.

Answer: Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at the Ajapāla Banyan tree, having gone into seclusion and being withdrawn, a reflection connected with the profundity of the Dhamma arose in his mind.

Adhigato kho myāyaṃ dhammo gambhīro duddaso aranubodho santo paṇīto atakkāvacarā nipuṇo paṇḍitavedanīyo.

This Dhamma that I have attained is profound, difficult to see, difficult to understand, peaceful, sublime, beyond the scope of logic, subtle, to be experienced by the wise.

Ālayarāmākho [M 2.0007] panāyaṃ pajā ālayaratā ālayasammuditā,

But this generation delights in attachment, is fond of attachment, and rejoices in attachment.

Sahampati brahmā

Brahmā Sahampati

Pucchā – kathaṃ āvuso tadā dhammadesanāya brahmayācanā ca bhagavato paṭiññāca ahosi.

Question: Friends, how then did Brahmā's request for the teaching of the Dhamma and the Blessed One's acceptance come to be?

Vissajjanā – desetu bhante bhagavā dhammaṃ, desetu sugato dhammaṃ, santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāroti iti bhante brahmuno dhammadesanāya yācanā ahosi,

Answer: "Venerable sir, let the Blessed One teach the Dhamma, let the Well-farer teach the Dhamma. There are beings with little dust in their eyes; from not hearing the Dhamma, they are declining. There will be those who understand the Dhamma." Thus, venerable sir, was Brahmā's request for the teaching of the Dhamma.

“Apārutā tesaṃ amatassa dvārā,

Ye sotavanto pamuñcantu saddhaṃ;

Vihiṃsasaññī paguṇaṃ nabhāsiṃ,

Dhammaṃ paṇītaṃ manujesu brahme”ti.

"Open for them are the doors to the Deathless, let those with ears release their faith. Perceiving vexation, I did not speak of the proficient, sublime Dhamma among humans, O Brahmā."

Evaṃ kho bhante bhagavato dhammadesanāya paṭiññā ca ahosi.

Thus indeed, venerable sir, was the Blessed One's acceptance to teach the Dhamma.

Nassati [M 2.0008] vata loko…

The world is indeed perishing...

Desetu bhante bhagavā dhammaṃ…

Venerable sir, let the Blessed One teach the Dhamma...

Apārutā tesaṃ amatassa dvārā,

Ye sotavanto pamuñcantu saddhaṃ.

Open for them are the doors to the Deathless, let those with ears release their faith.

Pucchā – evaṃ [M 2.0009] kho āvuso bhagavā dhammadesanāya paṭiññaṃ katvā paṭhamaṃ dhammaṃ desento kattha kīdisaṃ dhammaṃ kassa desesi.

Question: Friends, thus having made his acceptance to teach the Dhamma, when teaching the Dhamma for the first time, where, what kind of Dhamma, and to whom did the Blessed One teach it?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante isipatane migadāye pañcavaggiya bhikkhupamukhānaṃ brahmagaṇānaṃ dhammacakkapavattanasuttaṃ desesi.

Answer: Venerable sir, at Varanasi, in the Deer Park at Isipatana, he taught the Dhammacakkappavattana Sutta to the assembly of Brahmas headed by the group of five monks.

Pucchā – tasmiṃ [M 2.0010] kho pana āvuso dhammacakkapavattanasutte desiyamāne manussaloke kassa dhammābhisamayo ahosi.

Question: Friends, when that Dhammacakkappavattana Sutta was being taught, whose realization of the Dhamma occurred in the human world?

Vissajjanā – āyasmato bhante koṇḍaññassa dhammābhisamayo ahosi.

Answer: Venerable sir, the Venerable Koṇḍañña had the realization of the Dhamma.

Pucchā – kathaṃ nu kho āvuso imasmiṃ buddhasāsane paṭhamaṃ pabbajjā ca upasampadā ca ahosi.

Question: Friends, how indeed did the first going forth and full ordination take place in this dispensation of the Buddha?

Vissajjanā – imasmiṃ bhante buddhasāsane paṭhamaṃ ehibhikkhupasampadā ahosi.

Answer: Venerable sir, in this dispensation of the Buddha, the first ordination was the 'Come, bhikkhu' ordination.

Labheyyāhaṃ [M 2.0011] bhante bhagavato santike pabbajaṃ, labheyyaṃ upasampadaṃ.

"Venerable sir, may I receive the going forth in the Blessed One's presence; may I receive the full ordination."

Ehi bhikkhu svākhāto dhammo, cara brahmacariyaṃ sammā dukkhassa antakiriyāya.

"Come, bhikkhu, well-proclaimed is the Dhamma. Live the holy life for the complete ending of suffering."

Pucchā – itaresaṃ pana āvuso catunnaṃ pañcavaggiyānaṃ kadā dhammābhisamayo ahosi.

Question: But friends, when did the realization of the Dhamma occur for the other four of the group of five?

Vissajjanā – pāṭipade bhante divase āyasmato vappassa, dutiye bhante divase āyasmato bhaddiyassa, tatiye bhante divase āyasmato mahānāmassa, catutthe bhante divase āyasmato assajissa dhammābhisamayo ahosi.

Answer: Venerable sir, on the first day, for the Venerable Vappa; on the second day, for the Venerable Bhaddiya; on the third day, for the Venerable Mahānāma; and on the fourth day, for the Venerable Assaji, the realization of the Dhamma occurred.

Pucchā – sabbesampi [M 2.0012] āvuso tesaṃ pañcavaggiyānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ kadā arahattapatti hoti.

Question: Friends, when did all those monks of the group of five attain arahantship?

Vissajjanā – sabbesampi bhante tesaṃ pañcavaggiyānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pakkhassa pañcamiyaṃ anattalakkhaṇasutte desiyamāne arahattapatti hoti.

Answer: Venerable sir, for all those monks of the group of five, the attainment of arahantship occurred on the fifth day of the fortnight while the Anattalakkhaṇa Sutta was being taught.

Pucchā – kadā āvuso bhagavatā bhikkhū tattha tattha cārikaṃ caritvā dhammadesanatthāya pesitā.

Question: Friends, when were the monks sent forth by the Blessed One to wander here and there for the purpose of teaching the Dhamma?

Vissajjanā – yasappamukhānaṃ bhante catupaññāsa sahāyakānaṃ pabbajitakāle bhagavatā bhikkhū tattha tattha cārikaṃ caritvā dhammadesanatthāya pesitā.

Answer: Venerable sir, at the time of the going forth of the fifty-four companions headed by Yasa, the monks were sent forth by the Blessed One to wander here and there for the purpose of teaching the Dhamma.

Saraṇagamana

Going for Refuge

Pucchā – kadā [M 2.0013] āvuso bhagavatā tīhi saraṇagamanehi pabbajjūpa sampadā anuññāto ahosi.

Question: Friend, when was the going forth and full ordination by means of the three refuges permitted by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – yasappamukhānaṃ bhante catupaññāsāya gihi sahāyakānaṃ pabbajitvā tattha tattha cārikaṃ caritvā dhammadesanatthāya pesitakāle bhagavatā tīhi saraṇagamanehi pabbajjā ca upasampadā ca anuññātā.

Answer: Venerable sir, at the time when the fifty-four lay companions headed by Yasa had gone forth and were sent to wander here and there for the purpose of teaching the Dhamma, the going forth and the full ordination by means of the three refuges were permitted by the Blessed One.

Anujānāmi [M 2.0014] bhikkhave tumheva dāni tāsu tāsu disāsu tesu tesu janapadesu pabbājetha upasampādetha.

“I allow you, monks, now to confer the going forth and full ordination yourselves in those various regions and those various countries.”

Pucchā – bhagavatā āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ ārāmo paṭiggaṇhituṃ kattha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Question: Friend, where and in what instance was it permitted by the Blessed One for monks to accept a monastery?

Vissajjanā – bhagavatā bhante bhikkhūnaṃ ārāmaṃ paṭiggaṇhituṃ rājagahe anuññāto, rājā bhante māgadho seniyo bimbisāro buddhappamukhassa bhikkhusaṅghassa veḷuvanaṃ uyyānaṃ adāsi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was permitted by the Blessed One in Rājagaha for monks to accept a monastery. Venerable sir, King Seniya Bimbisāra of Magadha gave the Veḷuvana park to the Saṅgha of monks headed by the Buddha. In that instance, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Etāhaṃ [M 2.0015] bhante veḷuvanaṃ uyyānaṃ buddhappamukhassa bhikkhusaṅghassa dammi –

“Venerable sir, I give this Veḷuvana park to the community of monks headed by the Buddha.”

Anujānāmi bhikkhave ārāmaṃ.

“I allow a monastery, monks.”

Pucchā – kathañca [M 2.0016] āvuso dvinnaṃ aggasāvakānaṃ pabbajjūpasampadā ahosi.

Question: And how, friend, did the going forth and full ordination of the two chief disciples come about?

Vissajjanā – dvinnaṃ bhante aggasāvakānaṃ ehi bhikkhupasampadā ahosi.

Answer: Venerable sir, the full ordination of the two chief disciples was the “Come, bhikkhu” ordination.

Vippasannāni [M 2.0017] kho te āvuso indriyāni.

“Your faculties, friend, are very clear.”

Kaṃsi tvaṃ āvuso uddissa pabbajito.

“In whose name, friend, have you gone forth?”

Ko vā te satthā.

“Or who is your teacher?”

Kassa vā tvaṃ dhammaṃ rocesi.

“Or whose Dhamma do you approve of?”

Atthāvuso mahāsamaṇo sakyaputto sakyakulā pabbajito.

“Friend, there is the Great Ascetic, a son of the Sakyans, who has gone forth from the Sakyan clan.”

Kiṃ vādī panāyasmato satthā kimakkhāyī.

“But what is your venerable teacher's doctrine? What does he proclaim?”

Ye [M 2.0018] dhammā hetuppabhavā, tesaṃ hetuṃ tathāgato;

Āha tesañca yo nirodho, evaṃ vādī mahāsamaṇo.

“Of those things that arise from a cause,

The Tathāgata has told their cause;

And also their cessation—

Such is the doctrine of the Great Ascetic.”

Alaṃ āvuso mā agamittha.

“Enough, friend, do not go.”

Ete bhikkhave dve sahāyakā āgacchanti kolito upatisso ca etaṃ me sāvakayugaṃ bhavissati aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ.

“Monks, these two companions, Kolita and Upatissa, are coming. This will be my chief pair of disciples, an excellent pair.”

Labheyyāma [M 2.0019] mayaṃ bhante bhagavato santike pabbajjaṃ labheyyāma upasampadaṃ.

“Venerable sir, may we receive the going forth in the Blessed One’s presence; may we receive the full ordination.”

Etha bhikkhavo svākhāto dhammo, caratha brahmacariyaṃ sammā dukkhassa antakiriyāya.

“Come, monks. The Dhamma is well-proclaimed. Live the holy life for the complete ending of suffering.”

Pucchā – upajjhāyo āvuso gaṇhituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññāto, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Question: Friend, where was the taking of a preceptor permitted by the Blessed One? Regarding whom and in what instance was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññāto, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū anupajjhāyakā anācariyakā anovadiyamānā ananusāsiyamānā dunnivatthā duppārutā anokappasampannā piṇḍāya cariṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Answer: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, regarding a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, being without preceptors, without teachers, uninstructed, and unadmonished, were improperly dressed, improperly robed, and lacking in seemliness when they went for alms. In that instance, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – upajjhāyamhi āvuso na sammāvattantassa dukkaṭaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down for one not behaving properly towards a preceptor? Regarding whom, and in what instance, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe [M 2.0021] bhante sambahule saddhivihārike ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante saddhivihārikā upajjhāyamhi na sammāvattiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of co-residents. A number of co-residents, venerable sir, did not behave properly towards their preceptor. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – kadā āvuso bhagavatā tīhi saraṇagamanehi upasampadaṃ paṭikkhipitvā ñāticatutthena kammena upasampadā anuññātā.

Question: When, friend, did the Blessed One, having rejected the higher ordination by the three goings for refuge, permit the higher ordination by a formal act with a motion as the fourth part?

Vissajjanā – rādhabrāhmaṇassa bhante pabbajitakāle bhagavatā tīhi saraṇagamanehi upasampadaṃ paṭikkhipitvā ñātticatutthena kammena upasampadā anuññātā.

Reply: At the time of the going forth of Rādha the brahmin, venerable sir, the Blessed One, having rejected the higher ordination by the three goings for refuge, permitted the higher ordination by a formal act with a motion as the fourth part.

Konu [M 2.0022] kho bhikkhave tassa brāhmaṇassa adhikāraṃ sarati.

Who, monks, remembers a service done by that brahmin?

Ahaṃ kho bhante tassa brāhmaṇassa adhikāraṃ sarāmi.

I, venerable sir, recall a service done by that brahmin.

Sādhu sādhu sāriputta, kataññuno hi sāriputta sappurisā katavedino.

Good, good, Sāriputta! Truly, Sāriputta, good people are grateful and appreciative.

Kathāhaṃ bhante taṃ brāhmaṇaṃ upasampādemi.

How, venerable sir, shall I give the higher ordination to that brahmin?

Yāsā [M 2.0023] bhikkhave mayā tīhi saraṇagamanehi upasampadāanuññātā, taṃ ajjatagge paṭikkhipāmi.

Monks, the higher ordination by the three goings for refuge that I permitted, from this day forward, I reject.

Anujānāmi bhikkhave ñātticatutthena kammena upasampādetuṃ.

I permit, monks, the giving of the higher ordination by a formal act with a motion as the fourth part.

Pucchā – ayācitakaṃ āvuso upasampadāpekkhaṃ upasampādentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who gives the higher ordination to a candidate for higher ordination without being asked? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu upasampannasamanantaraṃ anācāraṃ acari, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, immediately after receiving the higher ordination, engaged in misconduct. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – catunnaṃ [M 2.0024] āvuso nissayānaṃ ācikkhaṇā bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, where was the declaration of the four resources permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha anuññātā, aññataro bhante bhikkhu udarassa kāraṇā pabbaji, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a certain monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, went forth for the sake of his stomach. In that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Ehi [M 2.0025] dāni āvuso piṇḍāya carissāma.

Come now, friend, let us go for alms.

Kathañhi nāma bhikkhu evaṃ svākkhāte dhammavinaye udarassa kāraṇā pabbajissati.

How indeed could a monk go forth for the sake of his stomach in this well-expounded Dhamma and Discipline!

Pucchā – ūnadasavassena [M 2.0026] āvuso upasampādentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one of less than ten years' seniority who gives the higher ordination? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante āyasmantaṃ upasenaṃ vaṅgantaputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante upaseno vaṅgantaputto ekavasso saddhivihārikaṃ upasampādesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the venerable Upasena Vaṅgantaputta. The venerable Upasena Vaṅgantaputta, venerable sir, being of one year's seniority, gave the higher ordination to a co-resident. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – bālena [M 2.0027] āvuso abyattena upasampādentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for a foolish, incompetent one who gives the higher ordination? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū “dasavassamhā dasavassamhā”ti bālā abyattā upasampādesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, being foolish and incompetent, gave the higher ordination, saying, 'We are of ten years' seniority, we are of ten years' seniority.' In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – ācariyo [M 2.0028] āvuso gaṇhituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññāto, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Question: Friend, where was it permitted by the Blessed One to take a teacher? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññāto, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū upajjhāyesu pakkantesupi vibbhantesupi kālaṅkatesupi pakkhasaṅkantesupi anācariyakā anovadiyamānā ananusāsiyamānā dunnivatthā duppārutā anākappasampannā piṇḍāya cariṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññāto.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, whose preceptors had departed, or had left the Order, or had died, or had gone over to another sect, being without a teacher, unadvised, and uninstructed, went for alms improperly dressed, improperly robed, and lacking in proper deportment. In that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – pañcahi [M 2.0029] āvuso ābādhehi phuṭṭhaṃ pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who gives the going-forth to a person afflicted with the five diseases? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū pañcahi ābādhehi phuṭṭhaṃ pabbājesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, gave the going-forth to those afflicted with the five diseases. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Sādhu [M 2.0030] bhante ayyā pañcahi ābādhehi phuṭṭhaṃ na pabbājeyyuṃ…

It is good, venerable sir, if the noble ones would not give the going-forth to one afflicted with the five diseases...

Pucchā – rājabhaṭaṃ āvuso pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who gives the going-forth to a person in the king's service? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū rājabhaṭe pabbājesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, gave the going-forth to those in the king's service. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – dhajabandhaṃ [M 2.0031] āvuso coraṃ pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who gives the going-forth to a notorious thief? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagaheyeva bhante aṅgulimālaṃ coraṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aṅgulimālo bhante coro bhikkhūsu pabbajito hoti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha itself, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the thief Aṅgulimāla. The thief Aṅgulimāla, venerable sir, had gone forth among the monks. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – kārabhedakaṃ āvuso coraṃ pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who gives the going-forth to a thief who has broken out of prison? Concerning whom, in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagaheyeva [M 2.0032] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū kārabhedakacoraṃ pabbājesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha itself, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, gave the going-forth to a thief who had broken out of prison. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – iṇāyikaṃ āvuso pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrongdoing for ordaining a debtor laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom and on what account was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagaheyeva bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū iṇāyikaṃ pabbājesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several monks at Rājagaha. Venerable sir, several monks ordained a debtor; venerable sir, it was laid down concerning that matter.

Pucchā – dāsaṃ [M 2.0033] āvuso pabbajentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrongdoing concerning ordaining a slave laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagaheyeva bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū dāsaṃ pabbājesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several monks right in Rājagaha. Venerable sir, several monks ordained a slave; it was laid down concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – bhaṇḍukammāya āvuso apalokanaṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where was the announcement for craftwork permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagaheyeva bhante aññataraṃ kammārabhaṇḍuputtaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ aññataro bhante kammārabhaṇḍuputto mātāpitūhi saddhiṃ bhaṇḍitvā bhikkhūsu pabbajito hoti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a certain son of a smith right in Rājagaha. Venerable sir, a certain son of a smith, having quarreled with his parents, had gone forth among the monks. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was permitted.

Pucchā – ūnavīsativassassa [M 2.0034] āvuso puggalassa upasampadā bhagavatā kattha paṭikkhittā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Question – Friend, where was the higher ordination of a person under twenty years of age prohibited by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it prohibited?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe [M 2.0035] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paṭikkhittā, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū ūnavīsativassaṃ puggalaṃ upasampādesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prohibited concerning several monks in Rājagaha. Venerable sir, several monks gave higher ordination to a person under twenty years of age. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was prohibited.

Na [M 2.0036] bhikkhave jānaṃ ūnavīsativasso puggalo upasampādetabbo, youpasampādeyya, yathādhammo kāretabbo.

Monks, a person under twenty years of age should not knowingly be given higher ordination. If one should give it, he must be dealt with according to the Dhamma.

Pucchā – mātāpitūhi [M 2.0037] āvuso ananuññātaṃ puttaṃ pabbājentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrongdoing concerning one who ordains a son without the consent of his parents laid down by the Blessed One? In what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sakkesu bhante paññattaṃ, rājā bhante suddhodano bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā varaṃ yāci, sādhu bhante ayyāananuññātaṃ mātāpitūhi puttaṃ pabbājeyyunti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down among the Sakyans. Venerable sir, King Suddhodana approached the Blessed One and requested a boon: 'Venerable sir, may the noble ones not ordain a son without the permission of his parents.' It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was laid down.

Na [M 2.0039] bhikkhave ananuññāto mātāpitūhi putto pabbājetabbo, yo pabbājeyya, āpattidukkaṭassa.

Monks, a son should not be ordained without the permission of his parents. Whoever ordains him incurs an offense of wrongdoing.

Pucchā – theyyasaṃvāsakassa āvuso upasampadā bhagavatā kattha paṭikkhittā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Question – Friend, where was the higher ordination of one who has entered communion by theft prohibited by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it prohibited?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ purāṇakulaputtaṃ ārabbha paṭikkhittā, aññataro bhante purāṇakulaputto khīṇa kolañño sāmaṃ pattacīvaraṃ paṭiyādetvā kesamassuṃ ohāretvā [M 2.0040] kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā ārāmaṃ gantvā bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ saṃvasi, bhante vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prohibited concerning a certain clansman from an old family in Sāvatthī. Venerable sir, a certain clansman from an old family, whose family had decayed, himself prepared a bowl and robe, shaved off his hair and beard, put on saffron robes, went to the monastery, and lived together with the monks. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was prohibited.

Pucchā – tiracchānagatassa [M 2.0041] āvuso upasampadā bhagavatā kattha paṭikkhittā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Question – Friend, where was the higher ordination of an animal prohibited by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it prohibited?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ nāgaṃ ārabbha paṭikkhittā, aññataro bhante nāgo māṇavakavaṇṇena bhikkhū upasaṅkamitvā pabbajjaṃ yāci, taṃ bhikkhū pabbājesuṃ upasampādesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prohibited concerning a certain nāga in Sāvatthī. Venerable sir, a certain nāga, appearing in the guise of a young man, approached the monks and requested ordination. The monks ordained him and gave him higher ordination. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was prohibited.

Pucchā – mātughātakassa [M 2.0042] ca āvuso pitughātakassa ca upasampadā bhagavatā kattha paṭikkhittā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Question – Friend, where was the higher ordination of a matricide and a patricide prohibited by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it prohibited?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃyeva [M 2.0043] bhante aññataraṃ māṇavakaṃ ārabbha paṭikkhittā, aññataro ca bhante māṇavako aññataro ca māṇavako mātaraṃ jīvitā voropesi pitaraṃ jīvitā voropesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paṭikkhittā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prohibited concerning a certain young man right in Sāvatthī. Venerable sir, one young man deprived his mother of life, and another deprived his father of life. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was prohibited.

Mātughātako [M 2.0044] bhikkhave anupasampanno naupasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo, pitughātako bhikkhave anupasampanno naupasampādetabbo, upasampanno nāsetabbo.

Monks, a matricide, if not fully ordained, should not be given higher ordination; if fully ordained, he should be expelled. Monks, a patricide, if not fully ordained, should not be given higher ordination; if fully ordained, he should be expelled.

Pucchā – apattacīvarakaṃ vā āvuso yācitapattacīvarakaṃ vā upasampādentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrongdoing concerning one who gives higher ordination to someone lacking a bowl and robe, or to someone with a begged bowl and robe, laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū apattacīvarakaṃ upasampādesuṃ, yācitakena pattacīvarena upasampādesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several monks in Sāvatthī. Venerable sir, several monks gave higher ordination to someone without a bowl and robe, and gave higher ordination with a begged bowl and robe. It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was laid down.

Pucchā – upasampadaṃ [M 2.0045] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ sauddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

The Question – I will ask about the higher ordination, with its basis and its detailed statement. Friend, how many offenses are there among the offenses enumerated in detail?

Vissajjanā – upasampadaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante dve āpattiyo.

The Reply – I will explain the higher ordination, with its basis and its explanation. Venerable sir, there are two offenses among the offenses enumerated in detail.

2. Uposathakkhandhaka

2. The Uposatha Section

Pucchā – cātuddase [M 2.0046] ca āvuso pannarase ca pakkhassa ca aṭṭhamiyā sannipatitvā dhammaṃ bhāsituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Friend, where and concerning what matter was it permitted by the Blessed One to gather and speak the Dhamma on the fourteenth, fifteenth, and eighth days of the fortnight?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātā, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū cātuddesa pannarase aṭṭhamiyā ca pakkhassa sannipatitvā tuṇhī nisīdiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Rājagaha concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus, having gathered on the fourteenth, fifteenth, and eighth days of the fortnight, sat in silence. Venerable sir, it was permitted concerning that matter.

Anujānāmi [M 2.0047] bhikkhave cātuddase pannarase aṭṭhamiyā ca pakkhassa sannipatituṃ.

“Monks, I allow you to gather on the fourteenth, fifteenth, and eighth days of the fortnight.”

Nanu nāma sannipatitehi dhammo bhāsitabbo.

“Surely, when gathered, the Dhamma should be spoken?”

Pucchā – kadā āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkhuddeso bhagavatā anuññāto.

The Question – Friend, when was the recitation of the Pātimokkha for the bhikkhus permitted by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – yadā bhante bhagavato rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi “yaṃnūnāhaṃ yāni mayā bhikkhūnaṃ paññattāni sikkhāpadāni, tāni nesaṃ pātimokkhuddesaṃ anujāneyyaṃ, so nesaṃ bhavissati uposathakamma”nti [M 2.0048] tadā bhante bhagavatā bhikkhūnaṃ pātimokkhuddeso anuññāto.

The Reply – Venerable sir, when the Blessed One was in seclusion, withdrawn, this train of thought arose in his mind: “Suppose I were to permit the bhikkhus to recite the Pātimokkha of the training rules I have laid down for them; that would serve as their Uposatha observance.” Then, venerable sir, the Blessed One permitted the bhikkhus the recitation of the Pātimokkha.

Pucchā – sīmaṃ āvuso sammanituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kismiñca vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the agreement on a boundary permitted by the Blessed One? And concerning what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante anuññātaṃ, rājagahe bhante bhagavati viharati bhikkhūnaṃ etadahosi “bhagavatā paññattaṃ ettāvatā sāmaggī, yāvatā ekāvāsoti kittāvatānukho ekāvāso hotī”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Rājagaha. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Rājagaha, the bhikkhus had this thought: “The Blessed One has laid down that unity extends as far as a single dwelling. How extensive, then, is a single dwelling?” Venerable sir, it was permitted concerning that matter.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0049] āvuso uposathā divasavasena ca kārakapuggalavasena ca kātabbākāravasena ca.

The Question – Venerable sir, how many Uposathas are there with respect to the day, with respect to the person performing it, and with respect to the manner of performance?

Vissajjanā – divasavasena bhante tayo uposathā, puggalavasena ca bhante tayo uposathā, kātabbākāravasena ca bhante tayo uposathā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, there are three Uposathas with respect to the day; venerable sir, there are three Uposathas with respect to the person; and venerable sir, there are three Uposathas with respect to the manner of performance.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0050] āvuso uposathakammāni tesu ca kīdisaṃ uposathakammaṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ, kīdisaṃ ananuññātaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, how many Uposatha acts are there? And among them, what kind of Uposatha act was permitted by the Blessed One, and what kind was not permitted?

Vissajjanā – cattārimāni bhante uposathakammāni, adhammena vaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, adhammena samaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, dhammena vaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, dhammena samaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ. Tatra bhante yadidaṃ adhammena vaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, adhammena samaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ dhammena vaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, evarūpaṃ bhante uposathakammaṃ ananuññātaṃ, tatra bhante yadidaṃ dhammena samaggaṃ uposathakammaṃ, evarūpaṃ bhante uposathakammaṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, there are these four Uposatha acts: an Uposatha act performed unlawfully by a divided assembly, an Uposatha act performed unlawfully by a united assembly, an Uposatha act performed lawfully by a divided assembly, and an Uposatha act performed lawfully by a united assembly. Among these, venerable sir, that which is an Uposatha act performed unlawfully by a divided assembly, an Uposatha act performed unlawfully by a united assembly, and an Uposatha act performed lawfully by a divided assembly—such an Uposatha act is not permitted. And among these, venerable sir, that which is an Uposatha act performed lawfully by a united assembly—such an Uposatha act is permitted.

Pucchā – uposathaṃ [M 2.0051] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

The Question – I will ask about the Uposatha, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, how many offenses are there in the distinctly formulated rules?

Vissajjanā – uposathaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

The Reply – I will declare about the Uposatha, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there are three offenses in the distinctly formulated rules.

3. Vassupanāyikakkhandhaka

3. The Chapter on the Rains Residence

Pucchā – vassūpanāyikaṃ āvuso bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the Rains Residence prescribed by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and on what account was it prescribed?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahulā bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū hemantampi gimhampi vassampi cārikaṃ cariṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed at Rājagaha concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus went on tour during the winter, the summer, and the rainy season. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – katipanāvuso vassūpanāyikā bhagavatā anuññātā.

The Question – Venerable sir, how many Rains Residences were permitted by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – dvemā bhante vassūpanāyikā bhagavatā anuññātā purimikā pacchimikā, aparajjugatāya bhante āsaḷhiyā purimikā upagantabbā, māsagatāya bhante āsaḷhiyā pacchimikā upagantabbā, imā kho bhante dve vassūpanāyikā bhagavatā anuññātā.

The Reply – Venerable sir, these two Rains Residences were permitted by the Blessed One: the earlier and the later. Venerable sir, the earlier is to be entered on the day after the full moon of Āsāḷha; the later is to be entered one month after the full moon of Āsāḷha. Venerable sir, these are the two Rains Residences permitted by the Blessed One.

Pucchā – vassaṃ [M 2.0054] āvuso upagantvā purimaṃ vā temāsaṃ pacchimaṃ vā temāsaṃ avasitvā cārikaṃ pakkamantassa dukkaṭaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the offense of wrong-doing prescribed for one who, having entered the Rains Residence, departs on tour without completing either the earlier or the later three-month period? Regarding whom, and concerning what matter was it prescribed?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū vassaṃ upagantvā antarā vassaṃ cārikaṃ pakkamiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed at Rājagaha concerning the bhikkhus of the group of six. Venerable sir, the bhikkhus of the group of six, having entered the Rains Residence, departed on tour during the rains. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – sattāha karaṇīyena āvuso pahite gantuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was permission granted by the Blessed One to go when sent on a seven-day task? Concerning whom, and on what matter was it granted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū udenena upāsakena pahite na gacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus did not go when sent by the lay follower Udena. Venerable sir, it was permitted concerning that matter.

Pucchā – sattāhakaraṇīyena [M 2.0056] āvuso sattannaṃ apahitepi gantuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was it permitted by the Blessed One to go on a seven-day task for seven kinds of people, even when not sent? Concerning whom, and on what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññatarassa bhikkhuno mātugilāna vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī in the matter of a certain bhikkhu’s sick mother.

Anujānāmi [M 2.0057] bhikkhave sattannaṃ sattāhakaraṇīyena apahitepi gantuṃ, pageva pahite.

I allow, monks, for seven kinds of people, to go on a seven-day task even when not sent, let alone when sent.

Pucchā – kismiṃci āvuso antarāye sati antovassaṃ pakkamituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, when there is some danger, where has the Blessed One permitted leaving during the Rains Residence? Regarding whom, in what circumstance, has it been permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū kosalesu janapadesu vassaṃ upagacchiṃsu, te vassūpagatā vāḷehi ubbāḷhā ahesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, permission was granted at Sāvatthī concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus went to spend the rains in the Kosala country. Those who had entered the Rains Residence were harassed by wild beasts. Venerable sir, permission was granted concerning that matter.

Pucchā – rukkha [M 2.0058] susire vā āvuso rukkhaviṭabhiyā vā ajjhokāse vā vassaṃ upagacchantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who enters the Rains Residence in a hollow tree, or on a tree branch, or in the open air? With reference to whom was it declared? In what circumstance was it declared?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū rukkhasusirepi rukkhaviṭabhiyāpi ajjhokāsepi vassaṃ upagacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed at Sāvatthī concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus undertook the Rains Residence in tree hollows, on tree branches, and in the open air. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – asenāsanikena [M 2.0059] āvuso vassaṃ upagacchantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who enters the Rains Residence without a lodging? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it declared?

Vissajjanā – tasmiṃyeva bhante sāvatthiyaṃ sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū asenāsanikā vassaṃ upagacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed in that same Sāvatthī concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus without lodgings entered the Rains Residence. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – chavakuṭikāya vā āvuso chatte vā cāṭiyā vā vassaṃ upagacchantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who enters the Rains Residence in a charnel-ground hut, or under a parasol, or in a large pot? Concerning whom, and on what account, was it declared?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃyeva bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū chavakuṭikāyapi chattepi cāṭiyāpi vassaṃ upagacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed in Sāvatthī itself concerning a number of bhikkhus. Venerable sir, a number of bhikkhus undertook the Rains Residence in a charnel-ground hut, under a parasol, and in a large pot. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – paṭissavaṃ [M 2.0060] āvuso visaṃ vādentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Question – Venerable sir, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who breaks a promise? In relation to whom was it declared, and on what account was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ upanandaṃ sakyaputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante upanando sakyaputto rañño pasenadissa kosalassa vassaṃ vāsaṃ paṭissuṇitvā visaṃvādesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed at Sāvatthī concerning the Venerable Upananda the Sakyan. Venerable sir, the Venerable Upananda the Sakyan, having promised King Pasenadi of Kosala to spend the rains, broke his promise. Venerable sir, it was prescribed concerning that matter.

Pucchā – vassūpanāyikaṃ [M 2.0061] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about the Rains Residence, with its origin and exposition. Venerable, how many offenses are there for the rules of the highest degree?

Vissajjanā – vassūpanāyikaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply: I will explain the Rains Residence, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there is one offense for the rules of the highest degree.

4. Pavāraṇākkhandhaka

4. The Chapter on the Pavāraṇā

Pucchā – vassaṃ [M 2.0063] vuṭṭhānaṃ āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ tīhi ṭhānehi pavārituṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One for monks who have completed the rains residence to invite criticism on three grounds? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū sandiṭṭhā sambhattā antovassaṃ neva ālapiṃsu na sallapiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, who were acquaintances and companions, neither spoke nor conversed with one another during the rains residence. In that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Anujānāmi [M 2.0064] bhikkhave vassaṃ vuṭṭhānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ tīhi ṭhānehi pavāretuṃ, diṭṭheva vā sutena vā parisaṅkāya vā.

I allow, monks, for those who have completed the rains residence to invite criticism on three grounds: on the basis of what has been seen, what has been heard, or what is suspected.

Pucchā – kati āvuso pavāraṇā divasavasena ca puggalavasena ca kātabbākāravasena ca.

Question: Venerable, how many kinds of Pavāraṇā are there, with regard to the day, with regard to the person, and with regard to the manner of performance?

Vissajjanā – divasavasena bhante tisso pavāraṇā, tathā puggalavasena kātabbākāravasenaca.

Reply: Venerable sir, with regard to the day, there are three kinds of Pavāraṇā; likewise with regard to the person, and with regard to the manner of performance.

Pucchā – katīnaṃ [M 2.0065] āvuso saṅghe pavāretuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ, katīnaṃ panāvuso aññamaññaṃ pavāretuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Question: Venerable, for how many has the Blessed One permitted the Pavāraṇā to be held in the Sangha? And, venerable, for how many has the Blessed One permitted the Pavāraṇā to be held among one another?

Vissajjanā – pañcannaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ saṅghe pavāretuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ, catunnaṃ vā bhante tiṇṇaṃ vā dvinnaṃ vā aññamaññaṃ pavāretuṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply: For five monks, venerable sir, the Blessed One has permitted the Pavāraṇā to be held in the Sangha. For four, three, or two, venerable sir, it is permitted to hold the Pavāraṇā among one another.

Pucchā – ekena panāvuso vassaṃvuṭṭhena bhikkhunā kathaṃ paṭipajjitabbanti bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Question: Venerable, how has the Blessed One permitted a single monk who has completed the rains residence to proceed?

Vissajjanā – ekena pana bhante bhikkhunā “ajja me pavāraṇā”ti adhiṭṭhātabbanti bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it has been permitted by the Blessed One that a single monk should make a determination, saying, ‘Today is my Pavāraṇā.’”

Pucchā – pavāraṇaṃ [M 2.0066] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about the Pavāraṇā, with its origin and exposition. Venerable, how many offenses are there for the rules of the highest degree?

Vissajjanā – pavāraṇaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Reply: I will explain the Pavāraṇā, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there are three offenses for the rules of the highest degree.

5. Cammakkhandhaka

5. The Chapter on Leather

Soṇamathera vatthu

The Story of the Elder Soṇa

Pucchā – upāhanaṃ [M 2.0067] āvuso bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was footwear permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante āyasmantaṃ soṇaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, āyasmato bhante soṇassa accāraddhavīriyassa caṅkamato pādā bhijjiṃsu, caṅkamo lohitena phuṭo ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning the Venerable Soṇa. Venerable sir, as the Venerable Soṇa was walking with excessive energy, his feet split, and the walking path became stained with blood. In that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – sabbanīlikā [M 2.0070] vā āvuso sabbapītikā vā sabbalohitikā vā sabbamañjiṭṭhikā vā sabbakaṇhā vā sabbamahāraṅgarattā vā sabbamahānāmarattā [M 2.0071] vā upāhanāyo dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One as an offense of wrong conduct for one to wear footwear that is entirely blue, entirely yellow, entirely red, entirely madder-colored, entirely black, entirely bright red, or entirely dark red? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū sabbanīlikāyo upāhanāyo dhāresuṃ, sabbapītikāyo sabbalohitikāyo sabbamañjiṭṭhikāyo sabbakaṇhāyo sabbamahāraṅgarattā sabbamahānāmarattāyopi upāhanāyo dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, wore footwear that was entirely blue, entirely yellow, entirely red, entirely madder-colored, entirely black, entirely bright red, and entirely dark red. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – khallakabandhā [M 2.0072] vā āvuso puṭabandhā vā pāliguṇṭhimā vā tūlapuṇṇikā vā tittirapattikā vā meṇḍavisāṇavaddhikā vā ajavisāṇavaddhikā vā vicchikāḷikā vā morapiñcha parisibbikā vā citrā vā upāhanāyo dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One as an offense of wrong conduct for one to wear footwear with cross-straps, with wraparound straps, with ankle-guards, stuffed with cotton, shaped like a partridge’s wing, adorned with ram’s horns, adorned with goat’s horns, with scorpion-tail points, decorated with peacock feathers, or embroidered? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū khallakabandhā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, puṭabandhā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, pāliguṇṭhimā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, tūlapuṇṇikā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, tittirapattikā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, meṇḍavisāṇavaddhikā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, ajavisāṇavaddhikā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, vicchikāḷikā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, morapiñcha parisibbitā upāhanāyo dhāresuṃ, citrā upāhanāyopi dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, wore footwear with cross-straps, with wraparound straps, with ankle-guards, stuffed with cotton, shaped like a partridge’s wing, adorned with ram’s horns, adorned with goat’s horns, with scorpion-tail points, decorated with peacock feathers, and embroidered footwear. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – kaṭṭhapādukāyo [M 2.0073] āvuso dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One as an offense of wrong conduct for one to wear wooden clogs? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggīyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū rattiyā paccūsasamayaṃ paccuṭṭhāya kaṭṭhapādukāyo abhiruhitvā ajjhokāse caṅkamanti uccāsaddā mahāsaddā khaṭakhaṭasaddā anekavihitaṃ tiracchānakathaṃ kathentā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, having risen in the early morning, put on wooden clogs and walked in the open, making loud noises, great noises, and clattering sounds, while engaging in various kinds of worldly talk. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – yānena [M 2.0074] āvuso yāyantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One as an offense of wrong conduct for one to travel by vehicle? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū yānena yāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, travelled by vehicle. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – gilānassa [M 2.0075] āvuso yānaṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was a vehicle for the sick permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ gilānaṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu kosalesu janapade sāvatthiṃ gacchanto bhagavantaṃ dassanāya antarāmagge gilāno kukkuccāyanto yānaṃ nābhiruhi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a certain sick monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, while traveling through the country of the Kosalans to Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One, fell ill on the way. Being scrupulous, he did not mount a vehicle. In that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – uccāsayanamahāsayanāni [M 2.0076] āvuso dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Venerable, where was it laid down by the Blessed One as an offense of wrong conduct for one to use high and large beds? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū uccāsayanamahāsayanāni dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, used high and large beds. In that matter, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – gocammāni āvuso dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing for wearing cowhides laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom and in what instance was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ pāpabhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu pāṇātipāte samādapesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain evil monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, encouraged the killing of living beings. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – paccantimesu [M 2.0078] āvuso janapadesu vinayadharapañcamena gaṇena upasampadā ca guṇaṅguṇūpahanañca dhuvanahānañca cammāni attharaṇāni ca nissīmagatānaṃ cīvaradānañcāti imāni pañca bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question: Friend, these five things—ordination by a chapter with a Vinaya expert as the fifth, foot-enclosing footwear, frequent bathing, leather floor-coverings, and the acceptance of robes by those who have gone outside the boundary—where were these allowed by the Blessed One in the border regions? Concerning whom and in what instance were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ soṇaṃ kuṭikaṇṇaṃ ārabbha anuññātā, āyasmā bhante soṇo kuṭikaṇṇo etāni pañcavatthūni āyasmato mahākaccānassa vacanena bhagavantaṃ yāci, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, they were allowed concerning the Venerable Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa. Venerable sir, the Venerable Soṇa Kuṭikaṇṇa requested these five things from the Blessed One at the word of the Venerable Mahākaccāna. In that instance, venerable sir, they were allowed.

Pucchā – cammasaññuttaṃ [M 2.0081] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about the section on leather, with its origin story and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there for the most serious cases?

Vissajjanā – cammasaññuttaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Answer: I will answer regarding the section on leather, with its origin story and explanation. Venerable sir, there are three offenses for the most serious cases.

6. Bhesajjakkhandhaka

6. The Section on Medicines

Pucchā – pañca [M 2.0082] āvuso bhesajjāni paṭiggahetvā kālepi vikālepi paribhuñjituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was it allowed by the Blessed One to accept and consume the five medicines both in and out of the proper time? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulānaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ sāradikena ābādhena puṭṭhānaṃ yāgupi na sammā pariṇāmaṃ gacchi, bhattampi bhuttaṃ na sammā pariṇāmaṃ gacchi, te tena kisā ahesuṃ lūkhā dubbaṇṇā uppaṇḍuppaṇḍukajātā dhamanisanthatagattā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was allowed in Sāvatthī concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, for a number of monks afflicted by an autumnal illness, neither the rice-gruel they consumed was properly digested, nor was the meal they had eaten properly digested. Because of that, they became thin, rough-skinned, discolored, pale and jaundiced, and with veins standing out on their limbs. In that instance, venerable sir, it was allowed.

Pucchā – mūlādibhesajjāni [M 2.0083] āvuso bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question: Friend, where were medicines such as roots allowed by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule gilāne bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātāni, sambahulānaṃ gilānānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ mūlehi bhesajjehi attho ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.
