Answer: Venerable sir, they were allowed in Sāvatthī concerning a number of sick monks. Venerable sir, a number of sick monks had need of root medicines. In that instance, venerable sir, they were allowed.

Pucchā – antovuṭṭhaṃvā āvuso antopakkaṃ vā sāmaṃpakkaṃ vā paribhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who consumes what has been stored inside, cooked inside, or cooked by oneself? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante ānando sāmaṃ tilampi taṇḍulampi muggampi viññāpetvā anto vāsetvā anto sāmaṃ paci, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Rājagaha concerning the Venerable Ānanda. Venerable sir, the Venerable Ānanda, having himself requested sesame seeds, rice, and beans, stored them inside and cooked them inside himself. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Suppiyā vatthu

The Story of Suppiyā

Pucchā – manussamaṃsaṃ [M 2.0084] āvuso paribhuñjantassa thullaccayañca appaṭivekkhitvā maṃsaṃ paribhuñjantassa dukkaṭañca bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the grave offense for one who consumes human flesh, and the offense of wrong-doing for one who consumes meat without having investigated it, laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, were they laid down?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu appaṭivekkhitvā manussamaṃsaṃ bhuñji, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Bārāṇasī concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk ate human flesh without having investigated it. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Na [M 2.0087] bhikkhave manussamaṃsaṃ paribhuñjitabbaṃ, yo paribhuñjeyya, āpatti thullaccayassa, na ca bhikkhave appaṭivekkhitvā maṃsaṃ paribhuñjitabbaṃ, yo paribhuñjeyya āpatti dukkaṭassa.

Monks, human flesh is not to be consumed. Whoever should consume it commits a grave offense. And, monks, meat is not to be consumed without having investigated it. Whoever should consume it commits an offense of wrong-doing.

Pucchā – hatthimaṃsaṃ vā āvuso assamaṃsaṃ vā paribhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing for one who consumes elephant meat or horse meat laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū hatthimaṃsampi assamaṃsampi paribhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Bārāṇasī concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks consumed elephant meat and horse meat. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – sunakkhamaṃsaṃ [M 2.0088] vā āvuso ahimaṃsaṃ vā bhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing for one who consumes dog meat or snake meat laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū sunakkhamaṃsampi ahimaṃsampi paribhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Bārāṇasī concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks consumed dog meat and snake meat. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – sīhamaṃsaṃ [M 2.0089] vā āvuso byagghamaṃsaṃ vā dīpimaṃsaṃ vā acchamaṃsaṃ vā taracchamaṃsaṃ vā bhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing for one who consumes lion meat, tiger meat, leopard meat, bear meat, or hyena meat laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃyeva bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū sīhamaṃsampi byagghamaṃsampi dīpimaṃsampi acchamaṃsampi taracchamaṃsampi paribhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was laid down in Bārāṇasī itself concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks consumed lion meat, tiger meat, leopard meat, bear meat, and hyena meat. In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – gilānassa [M 2.0090] āvuso guḷaṃ vā agilānassa guḷodakaṃ vā bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where were molasses for the sick and molasses water for the healthy allowed by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū gilānasseva bhagavatā guḷo anuññāto no agilānassāti kukkuccāyantā guḷaṃ na bhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was allowed in Rājagaha concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks, being scrupulous, did not consume molasses, thinking, 'Molasses has been allowed by the Blessed One only for the sick, not for the healthy.' In that instance, venerable sir, it was allowed.

Pucchā – jānaṃ [M 2.0091] āvuso uddissa kataṃ maṃsaṃ paribhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ paññapetvā tikoṭiparisuddhaṃ maṃsaṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, having laid down an offense of wrong-doing for one who knowingly consumes meat prepared specifically for them, where did the Blessed One allow meat that is pure in three respects?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante sīhassa seṭāpatino vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was allowed in Vesālī in the case of the general Sīha.

Meṇḍaka vatthu

The Story of Meṇḍaka

Pucchā – pātheyyaṃ [M 2.0094] āvuso pariyesituñca kappiyakārakānaṃ hatthe upanikkhittahiraññato nibbattaṃ kappiyapaccayaṃ sādituñca bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was it allowed by the Blessed One to seek provisions for a journey, and to accept allowable requisites produced from gold and silver deposited in the hands of a steward? Concerning whom, and in what instance, was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – antarā [M 2.0095] ca bhante bhaddiyaṃ antarā ca aṅguttarāpaṃ meṇḍakaṃ gahapatiṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, meṇḍako bhante gahapati bhagavantaṃ yāci “santi bhante maggā kantārā appodakā appabhakkhā na sukarā apātheyyena gantuṃ, sādhu bhante bhagavā bhikkhūnaṃ pātheyyaṃ anujānātū”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: Venerable sir, it was allowed concerning the householder Meṇḍaka on the road between Bhaddiya and Aṅguttarāpa. Venerable sir, the householder Meṇḍaka requested of the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, there are wilderness roads with little water and little food; it is not easy to travel without provisions for the journey. It would be good, venerable sir, if the Blessed One would allow provisions for the monks.' In that instance, venerable sir, it was allowed.

Keṇiya vatthu

The Story of Keṇiya

Pucchā – aṭṭha [M 2.0096] āvuso pānāni bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question: Friend, where were the eight kinds of drinks permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom were they permitted? In what context were they permitted?

Vissajjanā – āpaṇe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātāni, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū kukkuccāyantā pānāni na paṭiggaṇhiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Answer: Venerable sir, they were permitted in Āpaṇa concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks, being scrupulous, did not accept the drinks. Venerable sir, they were permitted in that context.

Rojamallā vatthu

The Story of Rojamallā

Pucchā – sabbañca [M 2.0097] āvuso ḍākaṃ sabbañca piṭṭhakhādanīyaṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One permit all kinds of broth and all kinds of flour-based foods? Concerning whom was it permitted? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – kusinārāyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū kukkuccāyantā ḍākañca piṭṭhakhādanīyañca na paṭiggahesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: At Kusinārā, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks, being scrupulous, did not accept broth and flour-based foods. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that context.

Pucchā – akappiyaṃ [M 2.0098] āvuso samādapentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One lay down the offense of wrong-doing for one who instigates what is unallowable? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – ātumāyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ vuḍḍhapabbajitaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante vuḍḍhapabbajito akappiye samādapeti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: At Ātumā, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain monk ordained in old age. Venerable sir, a certain monk ordained in old age instigated what was unallowable. Venerable sir, it was laid down in that context.

Pucchā – saṅghikāni [M 2.0099] vā āvuso puggalikāni vā bījāni puggalikāya vā saṅghikāya vā bhūmiyā ropitāni bhāgaṃ datvā paribhuñjituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One permit the use of seeds belonging to the Sangha or to an individual, planted in land belonging to an individual or the Sangha, after giving a share? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante anuññātaṃ, sāvatthiyaṃ bhante bhagavati viharati saṅghikānipi bījāni puggalikāya bhūmiyā ropiyiṃsu, puggalikānipi bījāni saṅghikāya bhūmiyā ropiyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, seeds belonging to the Sangha were planted in land belonging to an individual, and seeds belonging to an individual were planted in land belonging to the Sangha. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that context.

Pucchā – vinayamahāpadesasaṅkhātāni [M 2.0100] āvuso cattāri kappiyākappiyaanulomāni bhagavatā kattha paññattāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattāni.

Question: Friend, where were the four standards, called the Great References of the Vinaya, which accord with what is allowable and unallowable, laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom were they laid down? In what context were they laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattāni, sambahulānaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ kismiñci kismiñciṭhāne kukkuccaṃ uppajji, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattāni.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, they were laid down concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, for a number of monks scruples arose in various instances. Venerable sir, they were laid down in that context.

Pucchā – yāvakālikādīhi [M 2.0101] saṃsaṭṭhānaṃ āvuso yāmakālikādīnaṃ paribhogakālamariyādaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One lay down the time limit for the use of what is allowable for a watch of the night, etc., when mixed with what is allowable until noon, etc.? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulānaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ etadahosi “kappati nukho yāvakālikena yāmakālikaṃ sattāhakālikaṃ yāvajīvikaṃ, na nukho kappati. Kappatinukho yāmakālikena sattāhakālikaṃ yāvajīvikaṃ, na nukho kappati. Kappati nukho sattāhakālikena yāvajīvikaṃ, na nukho kappatī”ti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, this occurred to a number of monks: 'Is it allowable to use what is permitted for a watch of the night, for seven days, or for a lifetime, together with what is permitted until noon? Or is it not allowable? Is it allowable to use what is permitted for seven days or for a lifetime, together with what is permitted for a watch of the night? Or is it not allowable? Is it allowable to use what is permitted for a lifetime, together with what is permitted for seven days? Or is it not allowable?' Venerable sir, it was laid down in that context.

Pucchā – bhesajjakaṃ [M 2.0102] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about medicines, with their origin and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the most serious cases?

Vissajjanā – bhesajjakaṃ vissajjissaṃ sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Answer: I will answer about medicines, with their origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there are three offenses in the most serious cases.

7. Kathinakkhandhaka

7. The Chapter on the Kaṭhina

Pucchā – vassaṃvuṭṭhānaṃ [M 2.0104] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ kathinaṃ attharituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One permit the monks who had completed the rains residence to spread the kaṭhina cloth? Concerning whom was it permitted? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante tiṃsamatte pāveyyake bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, tiṃsamattā bhante pāveyyakā bhikkhū sāvatthiṃ āgacchantā bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upakaṭṭhāya vassūpanāyikāya nāsakkhiṃsu sāvatthiyaṃ vassūpanāyikaṃ sambhāvetuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning about thirty monks from Pāvā. Venerable sir, about thirty monks from Pāvā, while coming to Sāvatthī to see the Blessed One, were unable to reach Sāvatthī for the rains residence as the time for the rains residence was approaching. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that context.

Pucchā – kathinakaṃ [M 2.0105] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about the kaṭhina, with its origin and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the most serious cases?

Vissajjanā – kathinakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante na katamā āpatti.

Answer: I will answer about the kaṭhina, with its origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there is no offense whatsoever in the most serious cases.

8. Cīvarakkhandhaka

8. The Chapter on Robes

Jīvakavatthu

The Story of Jīvaka

Pucchā – gahapaticīvaraṃ [M 2.0106] āvuso sādiyituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was accepting a householder's robe permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom was it permitted? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante jīvakaṃ komārabhaccaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, jīvako bhante komārabhacco bhikkhūnaṃ gahapaticīvaraṃ anujānituṃ bhagavantaṃ yāci, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Answer: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning Jīvaka Komārabhacca. Venerable sir, Jīvaka Komārabhacca requested the Blessed One to permit a householder's robe for the monks. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that context.

Pucchā – cha [M 2.0107] āvuso rajanāni bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question: Friend, where were the six kinds of dyes permitted by the Blessed One? In what context were they permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātāni, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū chakaṇenapi paṇḍumattikāyapi cīvaraṃ rajiṃsu, cīvaraṃ dubbaṇṇaṃ hoti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Answer: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, they were permitted concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks dyed robes with cow dung and with yellow clay, and the robes became discolored. Venerable sir, they were permitted in that context.

Pucchā – acchinnakāni [M 2.0108] āvuso cīvarāni dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One lay down the offense of wrong-doing for one who wears uncut robes? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū acchinnakāni cīvarāni dhāresuṃ dantakasāvāni, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: At Rājagaha, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks wore uncut robes dyed the color of a tooth-stick. Venerable sir, it was laid down in that context.

Pucchā – kadā āvuso bhagavā anuññāsi chinnakaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ chinnakaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ chinnakaṃ antaravāsakaṃ.

Question: When, friend, did the Blessed One permit a cut outer robe, a cut upper robe, and a cut inner robe?

Vissajjanā – dakkhiṇāgirito bhante pacchāgatakāle bhagavā anuññāsi chinnakaṃ saṅghāṭiṃ chinnakaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ chinnakaṃ antaravāsakaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, the Blessed One, upon returning from Dakkhiṇāgiri, permitted a cut-up outer robe, a cut-up upper robe, and a cut-up lower robe.

Pucchā – aggaḷaṃ [M 2.0110] ca āvuso tunnañca ovaṭṭikañca kaṇḍusakañca daḷhīkammañca bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where were a bolt, a needle-key, a keyhole, a bolt-pin, and reinforcing permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom was it permitted? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – bārāṇasiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu aggaḷaṃ acchupesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply: At Bārāṇasī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk touched a bolt. In that context, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – vassikasāṭikā [M 2.0111] ca āvuso āgantukabhattañca gamikabhattañca gilānabhattañca gilānupaṭṭhākabhattañca gilānabhesajjañca dhuvayāgu ca bhikkhunisaṅghassa udakasāṭikācāti imā aṭṭha bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, where were these eight things permitted by the Blessed One: a rains-bathing cloth, food for incoming monks, food for departing monks, food for the sick, food for those attending the sick, medicine for the sick, constant rice-gruel, and a bathing cloth for the community of nuns? Concerning whom were they permitted? In what context were they permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante visākhaṃ migāramātaraṃ ārabbha anuññātā, visākhā bhante migāramātā bhagavantaṃ aṭṭhavarāni yāci, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, they were permitted concerning Visākhā, Migāra’s mother. Venerable sir, Visākhā, Migāra’s mother, requested eight boons from the Blessed One. In that context, venerable sir, they were permitted.

Pucchā – bhagavatā [M 2.0114] āvuso mātāpitūnaṃ dānaṃ anujānitvā saddhādeyyaṃ vinipātentassa dukkaṭaṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who squanders what was given in faith, after the giving to parents was permitted? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññatarassa bhante bhikkhuno bahuṃ cīvaraṃ uppannaṃ ahosi, so ca taṃcīvaraṃ mātāpitūnaṃ dātukāmo ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, much robe-cloth had arisen for a certain monk, and he was desirous of giving that robe-cloth to his parents. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Mātāpitaroti [M 2.0115] kho bhikkhave dadamāne kiṃ vadeyyāma, anujānāmi bhikkhave mātāpitūnaṃ dātuṃ, na ca bhikkhave saddhādeyyaṃ vinipātetabbaṃ, yo vinipāteyya āpatti dukkaṭassa –

‘Monks, regarding parents, when giving, what should we say?’ Monks, I permit giving to parents. But, monks, what is given in faith should not be squandered. Whoever should squander it, commits an offense of wrong-doing.

Pucchā – aññatra vassaṃ vuṭṭhena āvuso aññatra cīvarabhāgaṃ sādiyantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who, having spent the rains residence in one place, accepts a share of robes in another? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ upanandaṃ sakyaputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante upanando sakyaputto aññatra vassaṃ vuṭṭho aññatra cīvarabhāgaṃ sādiyi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the Venerable Upananda the Sakyan. Venerable sir, the Venerable Upananda the Sakyan, having spent the rains residence in one place, accepted a share of robes in another. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – anāthakaṃ [M 2.0117] āvuso bhikkhuṃ gilānaṃ na upaṭṭhahantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who does not attend to a sick, helpless monk? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, bhagavati bhante sāvatthiyaṃ viharati aññatarassa bhikkhuno kucchivikārābādho ahosi, so sake muttakarīse palipanno sayi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, a certain monk had an affliction of a stomach disorder, and he lay fallen in his own urine and excrement. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – naggiyaṃ [M 2.0119] āvuso titthiyasamādānaṃ samādiyantassa thullaccayaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the grave offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who undertakes the sectarian observance of nakedness? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu naggiyaṃ titthiyasamādānaṃ samādiyi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk undertook the sectarian observance of nakedness. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – sabbanīlakāni [M 2.0120] vā āvuso cīvarāni sabbapītakāni vā sabbalohitakāni vā sabbamañjiṭṭhakāni vā sabbakaṇhānivāti evarūpāni cīvarāni dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one wearing robes that are entirely blue, or entirely yellow, or entirely red, or entirely crimson, or entirely black? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 2.0121] bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū sabbanīlakādīni cīvarāni dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks wore robes that were entirely blue and so forth. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – kañcukaṃ [M 2.0122] vā āvuso veṭhanaṃ vā dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one wearing a jacket or a wrap? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū kañcukampi dhāresuṃ veṭhanampi dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks wore jackets and they wore wraps. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – cīvarasaññuttaṃ pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about what is connected with robes, with its origin and exposition. Friend, how many offenses are there for the highest category?

Vissajjanā – cīvarasaññuttaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Reply: I will answer about what is connected with robes, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there are three offenses for the highest category.

6. Campeyyakkhandhaka

6. The Chapter Concerning Campā

Pucchā – suddhaṃ [M 2.0124] āvuso bhikkhuṃ anāpattikaṃ avatthusmiṃ akāraṇe ukkhipantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who suspends a pure monk, who is without offense, on baseless grounds and without reason? Concerning whom was it laid down? In what context was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – campāyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, bhagavati bhante campāyaṃ viharati sambahulā bhikkhū kāsīsu vāsabhagāme suddhaṃ kassapagottaṃ bhikkhuṃ anāpattikaṃ avatthusmiṃ akāraṇe ukkhipiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: At Campā, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several monks. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Campā, several monks in the Kāsī country, at Vāsabhagāma, suspended the pure monk Kassapagotta, who was without offense, without grounds, and without reason. In that context, venerable sir, it was laid down.

Pucchā – campeyyakaṃ [M 2.0125] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about the section concerning Campā, with its origin and exposition. Friend, how many offenses are there for the highest category?

Vissajjanā – campeyyakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply: I will answer about the section concerning Campā, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there is one offense for the highest category.

10. Kosambakakkhandhaka

10. The Chapter Concerning Kosambī

Pucchā – bhedagarukehi [M 2.0127] bhikkhave bhikkhūhi na so bhikkhu āpattiyā adassane ukkhipitabboti ca, bhedagarukena bhikkhave bhikkhunā paresampi saddhāya āpattidesetabbāti ca āvuso ayaṃ sāmaggirasaovādo bhagavatā kattha dinno, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ dinno.

Question: Friend, where was this exhortation on the taste of harmony given by the Blessed One: ‘Monks, that monk should not be suspended by monks who hold schism to be a grave matter for not seeing an offense,’ and ‘Monks, an offense should be confessed by a monk who holds schism to be a grave matter, even to others, out of faith’? Concerning whom was it given? In what context was it given?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha dinno, bhagavati bhante kosambiyaṃ viharati aññataro bhikkhu āpattiṃ āpanno ahosi, so tassā āpattiyā āpatti diṭṭhi ahosi, aññe bhikkhū tassā āpattiyā anāpatti diṭṭhino ahesuṃ. So aparena samayena tassā āpattiyā anāpattidiṭṭhi ahosi, aññe bhikkhū tassā āpattiyā āpattidiṭṭhino ahesuṃ. Atha kho te bhante bhikkhū sāmaggiṃ labhitvā taṃ bhikkhuṃ āpattiyā adassane ukkhipiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ dinno.

Reply: At Kosambī, venerable sir, it was given concerning several monks. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Kosambī, a certain monk had committed an offense. He had the view that it was an offense, while other monks had the view that it was not an offense. At a later time, he came to have the view that it was not an offense, while the other monks came to have the view that it was an offense. Then, venerable sir, those monks, having achieved unanimity, suspended that monk for not seeing the offense. In that context, venerable sir, it was given.

Pucchā – saṅghabhedassa [M 2.0130] āvuso mūlabhūte bhikkhumhi ukkhittake osārite tassa vatthussa vūpasamāya saṅghasāmaggiñca kātuṃ sāmaggī uposathañca kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was it permitted by the Blessed One, for the monk who was the root of the schism of the Sangha, having been suspended and then reinstated, that for the settling of that matter, harmony of the Sangha should be made and the Uposatha of harmony should be performed? Concerning whom was it permitted? In what context was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante teyeva ukkhittakānuvattake bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, te bhante ukkhittakānuvattakā bhikkhū taṃ ukkhittakaṃ bhikkhuṃ osāretvā yena ukkhepakā bhikkhū tenupasaṅkamiṃsu upasaṅkamitvā te ukkhepake bhikkhū ekadavocuṃ “yasmiṃ āvuso vatthusmiṃ ahosi saṅghassa [M 2.0131] bhaṇḍanaṃ kalaho viggaho vivādo saṅghabhedo saṅgharāji saṅghavavatthānaṃ saṅghanānākaraṇaṃ, so eso bhikkhuṃ āpanno ca ukkhitto ca passi ca osārito ca, handa mayaṃ āvuso tassa vatthussa vūpasamāya saṅghasāmaggiṃ karomā”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, in Sāvatthī, concerning those very monks who sided with the suspended monk, it was permitted. Venerable sir, those monks who sided with the suspended monk, having reinstated that suspended monk, approached the monks who had suspended him. Having approached, they said to those suspending monks: "Friends, regarding the matter in which there was for the Sangha contention, quarrel, dispute, disagreement, schism, factionalism, division, and alienation—that monk has fallen into it, was suspended, has seen his fault, and has been reinstated. Come, friends, let us create concord in the Sangha for the resolution of that matter." Venerable sir, concerning that matter, it was permitted.

Pucchā – kosambakaṃ [M 2.0132] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – I will ask about the Kosambaka, with its origin and exposition. Friend, how many offenses are there in the highest category?

Vissajjanā – kosambakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply – I will answer regarding the Kosambaka, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the highest category.

Cūḷavaggapāḷi

The Lesser Division

1. Kambakkhandhaka

1. The Chapter on Formal Acts

Pucchā – bhaṇḍanādikārakassa [M 2.0133] āvuso bhikkhuno tajjanīyakammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where has the Blessed One permitted the act of censure to be performed against a monk who causes quarrels and disputes? Concerning whom was it permitted, and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante paṇḍukalohitake bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, paṇḍukalohitakā bhante bhikkhū attanā bhaṇḍanakārakā kalahakārakā vivādakārakā bhassakārakā saṅghe adhikaraṇakārakā, yepi caññe bhikkhū bhaṇḍanakārakā kalahakārakā vivādakārakā bhassakārakā saṅghe adhikaraṇakārakā te upasaṅkamitvā evamāhaṃsu “mā kho tumhe āyasmanto eso ajesi, balavābalavaṃ paṭimantetha, tumhe tena paṇḍitatarā ca byattatarā ca bahussutatarā ca alamattatarā ca, mā cassa bhāyittha, mayampi tumhākaṃ pakkhā bhavissāmā”ti, tena anuppannāni ceva bhaṇḍanāni uppajjanti, uppannāni ca bhaṇḍanāni bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya saṃvattanti. Tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning the Paṇḍukalohitaka monks. Venerable sir, the Paṇḍukalohitaka monks were themselves makers of quarrels, makers of disputes, makers of contentions, makers of worthless talk, and makers of legal issues in the Sangha. And they approached other monks who were makers of quarrels, makers of disputes, makers of contentions, makers of worthless talk, and makers of legal issues in the Sangha, and said this: "Venerables, do not let this one win. Counter him strongly. You are wiser, more competent, more learned, and more capable than he is. Do not be afraid of him. We too will be on your side." Thus, quarrels that had not arisen came to arise, and quarrels that had arisen increased and grew. Concerning this matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – tajjanīyakammakatassa [M 2.0134] āvuso bhikkhuno katisu vattesu sammā vattantassa taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, for a monk subjected to an act of censure who is behaving properly in how many duties has the Blessed One permitted the rescinding of that act? Where, concerning whom, and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhārasasu [M 2.0135] bhante vattesu sammā vattantassa tajjanīyakammakatassa bhikkhuno taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā sāvatthiyaṃ anuññātaṃ, teyeva paṇḍukalohitakā bhikkhū saṅghena tajjanīyakammakatā sammā vattitvā saṅghaṃ upasaṅkamitvā yāciṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, at Sāvatthī, the Blessed One permitted that the act be rescinded for a monk subjected to the act of censure who behaves properly in eighteen duties. Those very Paṇḍukalohitaka monks, on whom the act of censure had been performed by the Sangha, behaved properly, approached the Sangha, and made a request. Concerning that matter, venerable sir, it was permitted.

Pucchā – bālassa [M 2.0136] āvuso abyattassa āpattibahulassa anapadānassa gihisaṃsaṭṭhassa ananulomikehi gihisaṃsaggehi niyassakammaṃ kātuṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the act of subordination prescribed for a foolish, incompetent monk, one who commits many offenses, does not acknowledge them, associates with householders, and engages in improper association with householders? Concerning whom and in what matter was it prescribed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ seyyasakaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante seyyasako bālo ahosi abyatto āpattibahulo anapadāno gihisaṃsaṭṭho vihāsi ananulomikehi gihisaṃsaggehi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was prescribed at Sāvatthī concerning the venerable Seyyasaka. Venerable sir, the venerable Seyyasaka was foolish, incompetent, committed many offenses, did not acknowledge them, and lived associating with householders through improper associations with householders. Concerning this matter, venerable sir, it was prescribed.

Pucchā – niyassakammakatassa [M 2.0137] āvuso bhikkhuno katisu vattesu sammā vattantassa taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, for a monk subjected to an act of subordination who is behaving properly in how many duties has the Blessed One permitted the rescinding of that act?

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhārasasu bhante vattesu sammā vattantassa niyassa kammakatassa bhikkhuno taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ, na upasampādetabbaṃ, na nissayo dātabbo, na sāmaṇero upaṭṭhāpetabbo, na bhikkhunovādakasammuti sāditabbā, sammatenapi bhikkhuniyo na ovaditabbā, evamādīsu bhante aṭṭhārasasu vattesu sammā vattantassa niyassakammakatassa bhikkhuno taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, the Blessed One has permitted the rescinding of that act for a monk subjected to an act of subordination who behaves properly in eighteen duties. He should not give higher ordination, he should not give dependence, he should not have a novice attend on him, he should not consent to be an instructor of nuns, and even if he has consented, he should not instruct nuns, and so forth. Venerable sir, the Blessed One has permitted the rescinding of that act for a monk subjected to an act of subordination who behaves properly in these eighteen duties.

Pucchā – kuladūsakassa [M 2.0138] āvuso pāpasamācārassa bhikkhuno pabbājanīyakammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One permit the act of banishment for a monk who is a corrupter of families and of evil conduct? Concerning whom was it permitted, and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante assajipunabbasuke bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, assajipunabbasukā bhante bhikkhū kīṭāgirismiṃ kuladūsakā ahesuṃ pāpasamācārā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī concerning the monks Assaji and Punabbasu. Venerable sir, the monks Assaji and Punabbasu were corrupters of families at Kīṭāgiri, of evil conduct. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Pucchā – paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ [M 2.0140] āvuso pucchāmi, saddhaṃ āvuso pasannaṃ gahapatiṃ dāyakaṃ kārakaṃ saṅghupaṭṭhākaṃ hīnena khuṃsentassa bhikkhuno [M 2.0141] paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, I ask about the act of reconciliation. Friend, where was the act of reconciliation permitted by the Blessed One for a monk who disparages with base speech a faithful, devoted householder, a donor, a benefactor, an attendant of the Sangha? Concerning whom was it permitted, and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ sudhammaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, bhagavati bhante sāvatthiyaṃ viharati āyasmā sudhammo macchikāsaṇḍe cittaṃ gahapatiṃ saddhaṃ pasannaṃ dāyakaṃ kārakaṃ saṅghupaṭṭhākaṃ hīnena khuṃsesi hīnena vambhesi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī concerning Venerable Sudhamma. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, Venerable Sudhamma disparaged and reviled with base speech the householder Citta of Macchikāsaṇḍa, who was faithful, devoted, a donor, a benefactor, and an attendant of the Sangha. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Tenahi [M 2.0142] bhikkhave saṅgho sudhammassa bhikkhuno paṭisāraṇīyakammaṃ karotu “citto te gahapati khamāpetabbo”ti –

Therefore, monks, let the Sangha perform the act of reconciliation on the monk Sudhamma, saying: "You must ask forgiveness of the householder Citta."

Pucchā – paṭisāraṇīyakammakatassa [M 2.0143] āvuso bhikkhuno anudūtaṃ dātuñca tena anudūtena saddhiṃ gantvā yathākhuṃsitaṃ gahapatiṃ khamāpetuṃ [M 2.0144] ca bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One permit that a monk who has been subjected to an act of reconciliation be given a companion, and going with that companion, ask for forgiveness from the householder he had disparaged? Concerning whom was it permitted, and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃyeva sudhammaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, āyasmā bhante sudhammo macchikāsaṇḍaṃ gantvā maṅkubhūto nāsakkhi cittaṃ gahapatiṃ khamāpetuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī concerning the Venerable Sudhamma himself. Venerable sir, the Venerable Sudhamma went to Macchikāsaṇḍa, but being ashamed, was unable to ask the householder Citta for forgiveness. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Pucchā – paṭisāraṇīyakammakatassa [M 2.0145] āvuso bhikkhuno katisu vattesu sammā vattantassa taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, for a monk subjected to an act of reconciliation who is behaving properly in how many duties has the Blessed One permitted the rescinding of that act?

Vissajjanā – aṭṭhārasasu bhante vattesu sammā vattantassa paṭisāraṇīya kammakatassa bhikkhuno taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, the Blessed One has permitted the rescinding of that act for a monk subjected to an act of reconciliation who behaves properly in eighteen duties.

Pucchā – āpattiṃ [M 2.0146] āvuso āpajjitvā taṃ āpattiṃ passituṃ vā paṭikātuṃ vā na icchantassa bhikkhuno āpattiyā adassane vā appaṭikamme vā ukkhepanīyakammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One prescribe the act of suspension for not seeing an offense or for not atoning for it, for a monk who, having committed an offense, does not wish to see it or atone for it? Concerning whom and in what matter was it prescribed?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ channattheraṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante channo āpattiṃ āpajjitvā na icchi taṃ āpattiṃ passituṃ vā paṭikātuṃ vā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the Venerable Channa in Kosambī. Venerable sir, the Venerable Channa incurred an offense, but did not want to see that offense or to atone for it. It was in that situation, venerable sir, that it was laid down.

Pucchā – āpattiyā [M 2.0147] āvuso adassanena vā appaṭikamme vā ukkhepanīya kammakatena bhikkhunā katisu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Question – In how many duties, friend, must a monk who is subject to an act of suspension for not acknowledging an offense or not making amends, properly conduct himself?

Vissajjanā – tecattālīsāya bhante vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, one must properly conduct oneself in forty-three duties.

Pucchā – ukkhepanīyakammakatassa [M 2.0148] āvuso bhikkhuno sammā vattantassa taṃ kammaṃ paṭippassambhetuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One grant permission to rescind the act of suspension for a monk who has properly conducted himself? Concerning whom and in what matter was it granted?

Vissajjanā – kosambiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃyeva channaṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, āyasmā bhante channo saṅghena āpattiyā adassane vā appaṭikamme vā ukkhepanīyakammakato sammā vattesi lomaṃpātesi netthāraṃ vattesi saṅghaṃ upasaṅkamitvā tassa kammassa paṭippassaddhiyā yāci, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was granted concerning the same Venerable Channa at Kosambī. Venerable sir, Channa, having been subjected by the Sangha to the act of suspension for not seeing an offense or not making amends, properly conducted himself, humbled himself, behaved so as to lead to the annulment of the act, approached the Sangha, and requested the lifting of that act. It was in this context, venerable sir, that permission was granted.

Pucchā – pāpikaṃ [M 2.0149] āvuso diṭṭhiṃ gahetvā taṃ diṭṭhiṃ na paṭinissajjantassa pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissagge ukkhepanīyakammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the act of suspension laid down by the Blessed One for one who, having adopted a harmful view, does not relinquish that view? Concerning whom and in what circumstance was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante ariṭṭhaṃ bhikkhuṃ gaddhabādhipubbaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, bhagavati bhante sāvatthiyaṃ viharati ariṭṭhassa nāma bhikkhuno gaddhabādhipubbassa evarūpaṃ pāpakaṃ diṭṭhigataṃ uppannaṃ ahosi “tathāhaṃ bhagavatā dhammaṃ desitaṃ ājānāmi, yathāyeme antarāyikā dhammā vuttā bhagavatā, te paṭisevato [M 2.0150] nālaṃ antarāyāyā”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī concerning the monk Ariṭṭha, formerly a vulture trapper. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, the monk Ariṭṭha, formerly a vulture trapper, conceived such a harmful view: "As I understand the Dhamma taught by the Blessed One, those things called obstructive by the Blessed One are not obstructive for one who indulges in them." It was in this context, venerable sir, that it was laid down.

Pucchā – pāpikāya āvuso diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissagge ukkhepanīyakammakatena bhikkhunā katisu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Question – In how many duties, friend, must a monk who has been subjected to an act of suspension for not relinquishing a wrong view, properly conduct himself?

Vissajjanā – tecattālīsāya [M 2.0151] bhante vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, one must properly conduct oneself in forty-three duties.

Pucchā – kammakkhandhakaṃ pucchissaṃ, sanidāni saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – I will ask about the Chapter on Formal Acts, with its causes and explanations. Friend, how many offenses are there in the principal items?

Vissajjanā – kammakkhandhakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply – I will explain the Chapter on Formal Acts, with its basis and explanation. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the principal items.

2. Pārivāsikakkhandhaka

2. The Chapter on Probation

Pucchā – pārivāsikassa [M 2.0152] āvuso pakatattānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ abhivādanādīni sādiyantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One concerning a monk on probation who approves of greetings and other courtesies from monks of regular standing? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it declared?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante pārivāsike bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, pārivāsikā bhante bhikkhū sādiyiṃsu pakatattānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ abhivādanādīni, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was declared at Sāvatthī concerning monks on probation. The monks on probation, venerable sir, approved of greetings and other courtesies from monks of regular standing. It was declared concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – pārivāsikena [M 2.0153] āvuso bhikkhunā katisu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Question – Friend, in how many duties should a monk on probation properly conduct himself?

Vissajjanā – pārivāsikena bhante bhikkhunā catunavutiyā vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, a monk on probation should properly conduct himself in ninety-four duties.

Pucchā – pārivāsikaṃ pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – I will ask about probation, with its basis and detailed explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the principal items?

Vissajjanā – pārivāsikaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply – I will explain probation, with its basis and detailed explanation. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the principal items.

3. Samuccayakkhandhaka

3. The Chapter on Compilation

Pucchā – samuccayaṃ [M 2.0154] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – I will ask about compilation, with its basis and detailed explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the principal items?

Vissajjanā – samuccayaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

Reply – I will explain compilation, with its basis and detailed explanation. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the principal items.

4. Samathakkhandhaka

4. The Chapter on the Settling of Disputes

Pucchā – asammukhībhūtānaṃ [M 2.0155] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ tajjanīyaṃ vā niyassaṃ vā pabbājanīyaṃ vā paṭisāraṇīyaṃ vā ukkhepanīyaṃ vā kammaṃ karontassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who, in the absence of monks, performs an act—whether censure, subjection, expulsion, requiring apology, or suspension? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it declared?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū asammukhībhūtānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ kammaṃ akaṃsu tajjanīyampi niyassampi pabbājanīyampi paṭisāraṇīyampi ukkhepanīyampi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was declared at Sāvatthī concerning the group of six monks. The group of six monks, venerable sir, performed acts—censure, subjection, expulsion, requiring apology, and suspension—against monks who were not present. It was declared concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – sativepullapattassa [M 2.0156] āvuso bhikkhuno sativinayaṃ dānaṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the granting of the verdict of mindfulness permitted by the Blessed One to a monk who has attained fullness of mindfulness? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante mettiyabhūmajake bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ mettiyabhūmajakā bhante bhikkhū āyasmantaṃ dabbaṃ mallaputtaṃ amūlikāya sīlavipattiyā anuddhaṃsesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Rājagaha concerning the monks Mettiya and Bhūmajaka. The monks Mettiya and Bhūmajaka, venerable sir, falsely accused Venerable Dabba Mallaputta of a groundless moral transgression. It was permitted concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – katipanāvuso [M 2.0161] ettha dhammikāni sativinayadānāni.

Question – How many, friend, are the lawful grants of the verdict of mindfulness here?

Vissajjanā – pañcimāni bhante dhammikāni sativinayassa dānāni, suddho hoti bhikkhu anāpattiko, anuvadanti ca naṃ, yācati ca, tassa saṅgho sativinayaṃ deti dhammena samaggena, imāni kho bhante pañca dhammikāni sati vinayassa dānāni.

Reply – There are these five, venerable sir, lawful grants of the verdict of mindfulness: a monk is pure and without offense; they accuse him, and he requests it; and the Sangha grants him the verdict of mindfulness lawfully and unanimously. These, venerable sir, are the five lawful grants of the verdict of mindfulness.

Pucchā – amūḷhassa [M 2.0162] āvuso bhikkhuno amūḷhavinayaṃ dātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the granting of the verdict of past insanity permitted by the Blessed One to a monk who is no longer deluded? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū gaggaṃ bhikkhuṃ ummattakena cittavipariyāsakatena [M 2.0163] ajjhāciṇṇena āpattiyā codesuṃ “saratā yasmā evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti, so evaṃ vadeti “ahaṃ kho āvuso ummattako ahosi cittavipariyāsakato, tena me ummattakena cittavipariyāsakatena bahuṃ assāmaṇakaṃ ajjhāciṇṇaṃ bhāsitaparikkantaṃ, nāhaṃ taṃ sarāmi, mūḷhena me etaṃ kata”nti. Evampi naṃ vuccamānā codenteva “saratā yasmā evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Rājagaha concerning several monks. Several monks, venerable sir, accused the monk Gagga of an offense repeatedly committed while he was deranged and mentally unbalanced, saying, “Do you remember, venerable sir, having committed such an offense?” He replied, “Friends, I was deranged and mentally unbalanced. Because of that derangement and mental imbalance, I repeatedly committed much that was unbecoming an ascetic, in speech and action. I do not remember it. This was done by me in my delusion.” Even when he was spoken to thus, they kept accusing him, saying, “Do you remember, venerable sir, having committed such an offense?” It was concerning that matter, venerable sir, that it was permitted.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0164] āvuso adhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni, vibhajitvā kathehi.

Question – How many, friend, are the unlawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity? Explain them in detail.

Vissajjanā – tīṇi bhante adhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni, idha bhante bhikkhu āpattiṃ āpanno hoti, tamenaṃ codeti saṅgho vā sambahulā vā ekapuggalo vā “saratāyasmā evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti, so saramānova evaṃ vadeti “na kho ahaṃ āvuso sarāmi evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti. Añño saramānova evaṃ vadeti “sarāmi kho ahaṃ āvuso yathā supinantenā”ti. Añño anummattakova ummattakālayaṃ karoti “ahampi kho evaṃ karomi, tumhepi evaṃ karotha, mayhampi etaṃ kappati, tumhākampetaṃ kappatī”ti. Esaṃ saṅgho amūḷhavinayaṃ deti, adhammikaṃ bhante amūḷhavinayassa dānaṃ. Imāni kho bhante tīṇi adhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni.

Reply – There are these three, venerable sir, unlawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity. Here, venerable sir, a monk has committed an offense. The Sangha, a group, or an individual accuses him, saying, “Do you remember, venerable sir, having committed such an offense?” Though remembering, he says, “Friends, I do not remember having committed such an offense.” Another, though remembering, says, “Friends, I remember it as if in a dream.” Another, though not insane, acts as if insane, saying, “I do this, you do this. This is allowable for me, and this is allowable for you.” The Sangha grants him the settlement for non-insanity. This, venerable sir, is an unlawful grant of the settlement for non-insanity. These, venerable sir, are the three unlawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0166] āvuso dhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni, vibhajitvā kathehi.

Question – How many, friend, are the lawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity? Explain them in detail.

Vissajjanā – tīṇi bhante dhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni, idha bhante bhikkhu ummattako hoti cittavipariyāsakato tena ummattakena cittavipariyāsakatena bahuṃ assāmaṇakaṃ ajjhāciṇṇaṃ hoti bhāsitaparikkantaṃ, tamenaṃ codeti saṅgho vā sambahulā vā ekapuggalo vā “saratāyasmā evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti, so assaramānova evaṃ vadeti “na kho ahaṃ āvuso sarāmi evarūpiṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitā”ti. Aññopi assaramānova evaṃ vadeti “sarāmi kho ahaṃ āvuso yathā supinantenā”ti. Aññopi ummattakova ummattakālayaṃ karoti “ahampi evaṃ karomi, tumhepi evaṃ karotha, mayhampi etaṃ kappati, tumhākampetaṃ kappatī”ti. Esaṃ bhante tiṇṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ saṅgho amūḷhavinayaṃ deti, dhammikaṃ bhante amūḷhavinayassa dānaṃ. Imāni kho bhante tīṇi dhammikāni amūḷhavinayassa dānāni.

Reply – There are these three, venerable sir, lawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity. Here, venerable sir, a monk is insane, his mind is deranged. Due to that insanity and mental derangement, he has repeatedly committed much that is unbecoming of a recluse, both in speech and in action. The Sangha, a group, or an individual then accuses him, saying, “Do you remember, venerable sir, having committed such an offense?” Not remembering, he replies, “No, friends, I do not remember having committed such an offense.” Another, also not remembering, says, “Friends, I remember as if it were a dream.” Another, being insane, acts as if insane, saying, “I do this, you do this. This is allowable for me, and this is allowable for you.” Venerable sir, the Sangha grants the settlement for non-insanity to these three monks. This, venerable sir, is a lawful grant of the settlement for non-insanity. These, venerable sir, are the three lawful grants of the settlement for non-insanity.

Pucchā – appaṭiññāya [M 2.0167] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ tajjanīyādīni kammāni karontassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the offense of wrong-doing declared by the Blessed One for one who, without acknowledgement, performs acts such as censure against monks? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it declared?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe [M 2.0168] bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū appaṭiññāya bhikkhūnaṃ kammāni akaṃsu tajjanīyādīni, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was declared in Rājagaha concerning the group of six monks. The group of six monks, venerable sir, performed acts such as censure against monks without acknowledgement. It was declared concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kathaṃ āvuso adhammikaṃ hoti paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ.

Question – Friend, how is a procedure in accordance with confession unlawful?

Vissajjanā – aññathā bhante āpattiṃ āpajjantassa aññathā paṭijānantassa yathā so paṭijānāti, tathā saṅgho kāreti, evaṃ kho bhante adhammikaṃ hoti paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, when one commits one offense but confesses another, and the Sangha acts according to what he confesses, then that, venerable sir, is an unlawful procedure in accordance with confession.

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 2.0169] āvuso dhammikaṃ hoti paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ.

Question – Friend, how is a procedure in accordance with confession lawful?

Vissajjanā – yaṃ bhante āpattiṃ āpajjantassa tameva paṭijānantassa teneva saṅgho kāreti, evaṃ kho bhante dhammikaṃ paṭiññātakaraṇaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, when one commits an offense and confesses to that very offense, and the Sangha acts accordingly, then that, venerable sir, is a lawful procedure in accordance with confession.

Pucchā – yebhuyyasikāya [M 2.0170] āvuso adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasametuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was settling a dispute by majority vote permitted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe [M 2.0171] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū bhaṇḍanajātā kalahajātā vivādāpannā aññamaññaṃ mukhasattīhi vitudantā vihariṃsu, na sakkonti taṃ adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasametuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was permitted in Rājagaha concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, were prone to quarrels, disputes, and contentions, attacking each other with verbal weapons. They were unable to settle that dispute. It was permitted concerning that matter, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kathaṃ āvuso adhikaraṇaṃ yebhuyyasikāya vūpasametabbaṃ.

Question – Friend, how should a dispute be settled by majority vote?

Vissajjanā – pañcahi bhante aṅgehi samannāgato bhikkhu salākaggāhāpako sammannitabbo, tena bhante salākaggāhāpakena [M 2.0172] salākā gāhātabbā yathā bahutarā bhikkhū dhammavādino vadanti tathā taṃ adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasametabbaṃ, evaṃ kho bhante yebhuyyasikāya adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasametabbaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, a monk endowed with five qualities should be appointed as the ballot distributor. Venerable sir, the ballot distributor should distribute the ballots so that the dispute may be settled in accordance with what the majority of monks who advocate the Dhamma say. Thus, venerable sir, a dispute should be settled by majority vote.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0173] āvuso adhammikā salākaggāhā.

Question – Friend, how many unlawful ways are there of taking votes?

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante adhammikā salākaggāhā, oramattakañca adhikaraṇaṃ hoti, na ca gatigataṃ hoti, na ca saritasāritaṃ hoti, jānāti ‘adhammavādī bahutarā’ti, appeva nāma adhammavādī bahutarā assūti, jānāti ‘saṅgho bhijjissatī’ti, appeva nāma saṅgho bhijjeyyāti, adhammena gaṇhanti, vaggā gaṇhanti, na ca yathādiṭṭhiyā gaṇhanti, ime kho bhante dasa adhammikā kalākaggāhā.

Reply – Venerable sir, there are ten unlawful ways of taking votes: when the matter is trifling, and the case is not gone into thoroughly, nor properly examined; one knows, ‘The advocates of unrighteousness are in the majority,’ and thinks, ‘May the advocates of unrighteousness be in the majority!’; one knows, ‘The Saṅgha will be divided,’ and thinks, ‘May the Saṅgha be divided!’; they take votes unlawfully; they take votes by party; and they do not take votes according to their own view. These, venerable sir, are the ten unlawful ways of taking votes.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0175] āvuso dhammikā salākaggāhā.

Question – Friend, how many lawful ways of taking votes are there?

Vissajjanā – dasa bhante dhammikā salākaggāhā vuttavipariyāyena.

Reply – Venerable sir, there are ten lawful ways of taking votes, by the reverse of what has been said.

Pucchā – pāpussannassa āvuso bhikkhussa tassa pāpiyasikā kammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the act for further depravity for a monk overwhelmed by evil laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and in what incident, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante upavāḷaṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, upavāḷo bhante bhikkhu saṅghamajjhe āpattiyā anuyuñjiyamāno avajānitvā paṭijānāti, paṭijānitvā avajānāti, aññenaññaṃ paṭicarati, sampajānamusā bhāsati, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the monk Upavāḷa. Venerable sir, the monk Upavāḷa, being questioned in the midst of the Saṅgha about an offense, denied it and then acknowledged it, acknowledged it and then denied it, equivocated, and knowingly spoke falsely. It was laid down concerning that incident, venerable sir.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0176] āvuso dhammikāni tassapāpiyasikā kammassa karaṇāni.

Question – Friend, how many lawful grounds are there for the act for further depravity?

Vissajjanā – pañcimāni bhante dhammikāni tassapāpiyasikā kammassa karaṇāni. Asuci ca hoti, alajjī ca, sānuvādo ca, tassa saṅgho tassapāpiyasikā kammaṃ karoti dhammena samaggena, imāni kho bhante pañca dhammikāni tassa pāpiyasikā kammassa karaṇāni.

Reply – Venerable sir, these are the five lawful grounds for the act for further depravity: he is impure, shameless, and blameworthy; and the Saṅgha performs the act for further depravity against him lawfully and in concord. These, venerable sir, are the five lawful grounds for the act for further depravity.

Pucchā – tassa [M 2.0177] pāpiyasikākammakatena āvuso bhikkhunā katisu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Question – Friend, in how many observances should a monk against whom the act for further depravity has been performed conduct himself properly?

Vissajjanā – tassa pāpiyasikākammakatena bhante bhikkhunā aṭṭhārasasu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ. Na upasampādetabbaṃ, na nissayo dātabbo, na sāmaṇero upaṭṭhāpetabbo, evamādīsu bhante aṭṭhārasasu vattesu sammā vattitabbaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, a monk against whom the act for further depravity has been performed should conduct himself properly in eighteen observances. He should not give ordination, he should not give dependence, he should not have a novice attend on him, and so on. Venerable sir, he should conduct himself properly in these eighteen observances.

Pucchā – kati [M 2.0178] āvuso adhikaraṇāni samathehi vūpasametabbāni.

Question – Friend, how many legal issues are to be settled by means of settlement procedures?

Vissajjanā – cattārimāni bhante adhikaraṇāni samathehi vūpasametabbāni, vivādādhikaraṇaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇaṃ āpattādhikaraṇaṃ kiccādhikaraṇaṃ, imāni bhante cattāri adhikaraṇāni samathehi vūpasametabbāni.

Reply – Venerable sir, these four legal issues are to be settled by means of settlement procedures: legal issues concerning disputes, legal issues concerning accusations, legal issues concerning offenses, and legal issues concerning duties. These, venerable sir, are the four legal issues to be settled by means of settlement procedures.

Pucchā – kiṃ [M 2.0179] āvuso vivādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Question – Friend, what is the root of a legal issue concerning disputes?

Vissajjanā – cha bhante vivādamūlāni vivādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, tīṇipi akusalamūlāni vivādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, tīṇipi kusalamūlāni vivādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, idaṃ bhante vivādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, the six roots of disputes are the root of a legal issue concerning disputes; also, the three unwholesome roots are the root of a legal issue concerning disputes, and the three wholesome roots are the root of a legal issue concerning disputes. This, venerable sir, is the root of a legal issue concerning disputes.

Pucchā – kiṃ [M 2.0180] panāvuso anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Question – Now what, friend, is the root of a legal issue concerning accusations?

Vissajjanā – cha bhante anuvādamūlāni anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, tīṇipi akusalamūlāni anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, tīṇipi kusalamūlāni anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, kāyopi anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, vācāpi anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, idaṃ kho bhante anuvādādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, the six roots of accusations are the root of a legal issue concerning accusations; also, the three unwholesome roots are the root of a legal issue concerning accusations, and the three wholesome roots are the root of a legal issue concerning accusations; the body is also a root of a legal issue concerning accusations, and speech is also a root of a legal issue concerning accusations. This, venerable sir, is the root of a legal issue concerning accusations.

Pucchā – kiṃ [M 2.0181] panāvuso āpattādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Question – But what, friend, is the root of a legal issue concerning offenses?

Vissajjanā – cha bhante āpattisamuṭṭhānā āpattādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ, atti bhante āpatti kāyato samuṭṭhāti na vācato na cittato, atthi bhante āpatti vācato samuṭṭhāti na kāyato na cittato, atthi bhante āpatti kāyato [M 2.0182] ca vācato ca samuṭṭhāti na cittato, atthi bhante āpatti kāyato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhāti na vācato, atthi bhante āpatti vācato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhāti na kāyato, atthi bhante āpatti kāyato ca vācato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhāti, imāni bhante cha āpatti samuṭṭhānāni āpattādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, the six origins of offenses are the root of a legal issue concerning offenses. Venerable sir, there is an offense that arises from the body, not from speech or mind; there is an offense that arises from speech, not from the body or mind; there is an offense that arises from both the body and speech, not from mind; there is an offense that arises from both the body and mind, not from speech; there is an offense that arises from both speech and mind, not from the body; there is an offense that arises from the body, speech, and mind. These, venerable sir, are the six origins of offenses, which are the root of a legal issue concerning offenses.

Pucchā – kiṃ [M 2.0183] panāvuso kiccādhikaraṇassa mūlaṃ.

Question – But what, friend, is the root of a legal issue concerning duties?

Vissajjanā – kiccādhikaraṇassa bhante ekaṃ mūlaṃ saṅgho.

Reply – Venerable sir, the Saṅgha is the sole root of a legal issue concerning duties.

Pucchā – samathaṃ āvuso pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – Friend, I will ask about settlement, with its basis and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there concerning the aggravated penalty?

Vissajjanā – samathaṃ bhante vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante dve āpattiyo.

Reply – Venerable sir, I will reply about settlement, with its basis and explanation. Venerable sir, there are two offenses concerning the aggravated penalty.

5. Khuddakavatthukkhandhaka

5. Chapter on Minor Matters

Pucchā – nahāyantena [M 2.0184] āvuso bhikkhunā rukkhe vā thambhe vā kuṭṭe vā kāyaṃ ugghaṃsentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for a monk who, while bathing, rubs his body against a tree, a pillar, or a wall? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū nahāyamānā rukkhepi thambhepi kuṭṭepi kāyaṃ ugghaṃsesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks, while bathing, rubbed their bodies against trees, pillars, and walls. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – gandhabbahatthakena vā āvuso kuruvindakasuttiyā vā mallakena vā nahāyantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one bathing with a gandhabbahatthaka, or with a kuruvindakasutti, or with a mallaka? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū gandhabbahatthakenapi kuruvindakasuttiyāpi [M 2.0185] mallakenapi nahāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks bathed with gandhabbahatthakas, kuruvindakasuttis, and mallakas. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – pāṇinā āvuso parikammaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One allow performing massage with the hand? Concerning whom was it allowed, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū piṭṭhiparikammaṃ kātuṃ kukkuccāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was allowed at Rājagaha concerning several monks. Venerable sir, several monks were scrupulous about performing back massage. Venerable sir, it was allowed on that occasion.

Pucchā – vallikaṃ [M 2.0186] vā āvuso pāmaṅgaṃ vā kaṇṭhasuttakaṃ vā kaṭisuttakaṃ vā ovaṭṭikaṃ vā kāyuraṃ vā hatthābharaṇaṃ vā aṅgulimuddikaṃ vā dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one wearing a vallika, or a pāmaṅga, or a kaṇṭhasuttaka, or a kaṭisuttaka, or an ovaṭṭika, or a kāyura, or a hatthābharaṇa, or an aṅgulimuddika? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū vallikādīni dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks wore vallikas and so forth. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – dīghe [M 2.0187] āvuso kese dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one keeping long hair? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū dīghe kese dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks kept long hair. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – kocchenavā [M 2.0188] āvuso phaṇakenavā hatthaphaṇakenavā kese osaṇṭhentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one smoothing the hair with a koccha, or a phaṇaka, or a hatthaphaṇaka? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū kocchādīhi kese osaṇṭhesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks smoothed their hair with kocchas and so forth. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – ādāse [M 2.0189] vā āvuso udakapatte vā mukhanimittaṃ olokentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one looking at the reflection of their face, either in a mirror or in a water bowl? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū ādāsepi udakapattepi mukhanimittaṃ olokesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks looked at their reflections in mirrors and water bowls. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – ābādhapaccayā [M 2.0190] panāvuso ādāse vā udakapatte vā mukhanimittaṃ oloketuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – But, friend, where was it allowed by the Blessed One to look at a reflection of the face in a mirror or a water bowl on account of illness? Concerning whom was it allowed, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, bhagavati bhante rājagahe viharati aññatarassa bhikkhuno mukhe vaṇo ahosi, so bhikkhū etadavoca “kīdiso me āvuso vaṇo”ti, bhikkhū evamāhaṃsu “ediso te āvuso vaṇo”ti, so na saddahati, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was allowed at Rājagaha concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, when the Blessed One was staying at Rājagaha, a certain monk had a wound on his face. That monk said, “Friends, what kind of wound do I have?” The monks said, “Friend, you have such and such a wound.” He did not believe them. Venerable sir, it was allowed on that occasion.

Pucchā – mukhālepanādīni [M 2.0191] āvuso karontassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who uses mukhālepanas and so forth? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū mukhālepanādīni akaṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks applied mukhālepanas and so forth. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – naccaṃ [M 2.0192] vā āvuso gītaṃ vā vāditaṃ vā dassanāya gacchantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who goes to see dancing, singing, or instrumental music? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante naccampi gītampi vāditampi dassanāya gacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks went to see dancing, singing, and instrumental music. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – kathaṃ āvuso naccādīni passantassa āpatti hoti, kathaṃ pana anāpatti.

Question: Friend, how does an offense occur for one who watches dancing and so forth? And how does no offense occur?

Vissajjanā – naccaṃ vā bhante gītaṃ vā vāditaṃ vā dassanāya gacchati āpatti dukkaṭassa, yattha ṭhito passati vā suṇāti vā āpatti dukkaṭassa[M 2.0193], dassanūpacāraṃ vijahitvā punappunaṃ passati āpatti dukkaṭassa, ārāme ṭhitassa passato anāpatti, vihārato pana vihāraṃ passissāmīti gacchato āpattiyeva, yattha ṭhito passati vā suṇāti vā āpatti dukkaṭassa, āsanasālāya nisinno passati anāpatti, passissāmīti vuṭṭhahitvā gacchati āpatti dukkaṭassa, yattha ṭhito passati vā suṇāti vā āpatti dukkaṭassa, patipathaṃ gacchanto passati anāpatti, gīvaṃ parivattetvā passato pana āpatti bhante.

Reply: Venerable sir, if one goes to see dancing, singing, or instrumental music, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If one stands where one sees or hears it, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If, having left the range of sight, one sees it again and again, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If one sees it while standing in the monastery grounds, there is no offense. But if one goes from one dwelling to another thinking, “I will see,” there is an offense. If one stands where one sees or hears it, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If one sees it while seated in the assembly hall, there is no offense. But if one rises thinking, “I will see,” and goes, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If one stands where one sees or hears it, there is a dukkaṭa offense. If one sees it while walking along the road, there is no offense. But if one turns one’s neck to see, then, venerable sir, there is an offense.

Pucchā – āyatakena [M 2.0194] āvuso gītassarena dhammaṃ gāyantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who recites the Dhamma in a drawn-out, musical voice? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū āyatakena gītassarena dhammaṃ gāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks recited the Dhamma with a drawn-out, musical voice. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – dārupattaṃ [M 2.0196] āvuso dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for carrying a wooden bowl? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante āyasmantaṃ piṇḍolabhāradvājaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante piṇḍolabhāradvājo chavassa dārupattassa kāraṇā gihīnaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ iddhipāṭihāriyaṃ dasseti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning the Venerable Piṇḍola Bhāradvāja. Venerable sir, the Venerable Piṇḍola Bhāradvāja displayed a superhuman feat of psychic power to householders on account of a wooden bowl. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Na [M 2.0197] ca bhikkhave dārupatto dhāretabbo, yo dhāreyya āpatti dukkaṭassa –

Monks, a wooden bowl is not to be carried. Whoever should carry one, there is a dukkaṭa offense.

Pucchā – bhagavatā āvuso uccāvace patte paṭikkhipitvā ayo patto bhūmipattoti imeyeva dve pattā kattha anuññātā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, where were these two bowls—an iron bowl and an earthenware bowl—allowed by the Blessed One, after he had rejected bowls of various kinds? Concerning whom were they allowed, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātā, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū uccāvace patte dhāresuṃ sovaṇṇamayā [M 2.0198] rūpiyamayā, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Reply: Venerable sir, they were allowed at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks carried bowls of various kinds, made of gold and made of silver. Venerable sir, they were allowed on that occasion.

Pucchā – sodakaṃ [M 2.0199] āvuso pattaṃ paṭisāmentassa ca otāpentassaca uṇhe pattaṃ nidahantassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who puts away a bowl with water in it, for one who warms it in the sun, and for one who places a bowl in the heat? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū sodakaṃ pattaṃ paṭisāmesuṃ, otāpesuṃ, uṇhe pattaṃ nidahiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks put away their bowls with water in them, warmed them in the sun, and placed their bowls in the heat. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – chavasīsapattaṃ [M 2.0200] āvuso dhārentassa ca sabbapaṃsukūlikassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who carries a bowl made from a corpse’s skull and for one who is entirely a rag-robe wearer? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?
