Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu sabbapaṃsukūliko ahosi, so chavasīsassa pattaṃ dhāresi, aññatarā itthī passitvā bhītā [M 2.0201] vissaramakāsi “abhuṃ me pisāco vatāya”nti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk was one who used only rag-robes. He carried a bowl made from a corpse’s skull. A certain woman saw him and, frightened, cried out, “Alas, a demon is upon me!” Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – calakāni vā āvuso aṭṭhikāni vā ucchiṭṭhodakaṃ vā pattena nīharantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one who removes shellfish, bones, or leftover water with a bowl? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū calakānipi aṭṭhikānipi ucchiṭṭho dakampi pattena nīhariṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning a number of monks. Venerable sir, a number of monks removed shellfish, bones, and leftover water with their bowls. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – upāhanatthavikā [M 2.0202] āvuso bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, where were shoe-bags allowed by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and on what occasion, were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – antarā ca bhante rājagahaṃ antarā ca vesāliṃ aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha anuññātā, aññataro bhante bhikkhu upāhanāyo kāyabandhanena bandhitvā gāmaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi, aññataro upāsako taṃ bhikkhuṃ abhivādento upāhanāyo sīsena ghaṭṭeti, so bhikkhu maṅku ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Reply: Venerable sir, they were allowed between Rājagaha and Vesālī concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk, having tied his sandals with his waistband, entered a village for alms. A certain lay follower, while paying respects to that monk, struck his head against the sandals. That monk became embarrassed. Venerable sir, they were allowed on that occasion.

Pucchā – addhānamaggappaṭipannena [M 2.0203] āvuso parissāvanaṃ yāciyamānena na dadantassa ca appaṭissāvanakena addhānaṃ paṭipajjantassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one traveling on a journey who, being asked for a water-strainer, does not give it, and for one who sets out on a journey without a water-strainer? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – antarā ca bhante rājagahaṃ antarā ca vesāliṃ aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu parissāvanaṃ yāciyamāno na adāsi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down between Rājagaha and Vesālī concerning a certain monk. Venerable sir, a certain monk, when asked for a water-strainer, did not give it. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – pupphābhikiṇṇe [M 2.0205] āvuso sayane sayantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for one lying on a bed strewn with flowers? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū pupphābhikiṇṇesu sayanesu sayiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Vesālī concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks lay on beds strewn with flowers. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – ekabhājane vā āvuso bhuñjantānaṃ ekathālake vā pivantānaṃ ekattharaṇapāvuraṇānaṃ vā tuvaṭṭānaṃ dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for those eating from a single vessel, drinking from a single cup, or lying down together with a single sheet and cover? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante chabbaggiyeva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū ekabhājanepi bhuñjiṃsu, ekathālakepi [M 2.0206] piviṃsu, ekamañcakepi tuvaṭṭesuṃ, ekattharaṇāpi tuvaṭṭesuṃ, ekapāvuraṇāpi tuvaṭṭesuṃ, ekattharaṇapāvuraṇāpi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply: Venerable sir, it was laid down at Vesālī concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks ate from a single vessel, drank from a single cup, lay down on a single bed, lay down with a single sheet, lay down with a single cover, and lay down with a single sheet and cover. Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pucchā – cāmaribījaniṃ āvuso paṭikkhipitvā tisso bījaniyo bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kismiñca vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, where were three kinds of fans allowed by the Blessed One, after he had rejected the yak-tail fan? And on what occasion were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante anuññātā, sāvatthiyaṃ bhante bhagavati viharati saṅghassa cāmaribījanī uppannā ahosi, bhagavato etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Reply: Venerable sir, they were allowed at Sāvatthī. Venerable sir, while the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī, a yak-tail fan had been acquired by the Saṅgha. They informed the Blessed One of this matter. Venerable sir, they were allowed on that occasion.

Na [M 2.0207] bhikkhave cāmaribījanī dhāretabbā, yo dhāreyya, āpatti dukkaṭassa. Anujānāmi bhikkhave tisso bījaniyo vākamayaṃ usīramayaṃ morapiñchā mayaṃ.

Monks, a yak-tail fan is not to be used. Whoever should use one, there is a dukkaṭa offense. I allow, monks, three kinds of fans: one made of bark, one made of vetiver grass, and one made of peacock feathers.

Pucchā – dīghe āvuso nakhe dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the dukkaṭa offense laid down by the Blessed One for keeping long nails? Concerning whom was it laid down, and on what occasion?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 2.0208] bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu dīghe nakhe dhāresi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning a certain monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, kept long nails. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – kattarikāya [M 2.0209] āvuso kese chedāpentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who has their hair cut with scissors? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū kattarikāya kese chindiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, cut their hair with scissors. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – dīghe [M 2.0210] āvuso nāsikālome dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, did the Blessed One lay down an offense of wrong-doing for one who keeps long nose hairs? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū dīghāni nāsikālomāni dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning a number of monks. A number of monks, venerable sir, kept long nose hairs. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – uccāvacā āvuso kaṇṇamalaharaṇiyo paṭikkhipitvā dasa kaṇṇamalaharaṇiyo bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question: Friend, having prohibited the various ear-cleaning instruments, where were ten ear-cleaning instruments allowed by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, on what account, were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātā, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū uccāvacā kaṇṇamalaharaṇiyo dhāresuṃ [M 2.0211] sovaṇṇamayaṃ rūpiyamayaṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Answer: They were allowed, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, kept various ear-cleaning instruments made of gold and silver. They were allowed, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – saṅghāṭipallatthikāya nisīdantassa āvuso dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one sitting with the outer robe used as a cushion? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū saṅghāṭipallatthikāya nisīdiṃsu, saṅghāṭiyā [M 2.0212] pattā lujjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, sat with the outer robe used as a cushion, and the bowls were damaged by the outer robe. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – akāyabandhanena āvuso gāmaṃ pavisantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, did the Blessed One lay down the offense of wrong-doing for entering a village without a waistband? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ bhikkhuṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante bhikkhu akāyabandhano gāmaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning a certain monk. A certain monk, venerable sir, entered a village for alms without a waistband. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – gihinivatthaṃ [M 2.0213] āvuso nivāsentassa ca, gihipārutaṃ pārupantassa ca, saṃvelliyaṃ nivāsentassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for wearing clothing like householders, putting on attire like householders, or wearing a head covering? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū gihinivatthaṃ nivāsiṃsu, gihipārutampi pārupiṃsu, saṃvelliyampi nivāsiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, wore clothing like householders, put on outer garments like householders, and wore head coverings. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – ubhato [M 2.0214] kājaṃ āvuso dhārentassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who wears a double waistband? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū ubhato kājaṃ dhāresuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, wore a double waistband. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – lokāyataṃ [M 2.0215] āvuso pariyāpuṇantassa ca vācentassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, did the Blessed One lay down an offense of wrong-doing for one who learns or teaches worldly lore? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū lokāyataṃ pariyāpuṇiṃsupi vācesumpi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, learned and taught worldly lore. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – tiracchānavijjaṃ [M 2.0217] āvuso pariyāpuṇantassa ca vācentassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who learns and teaches low arts? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū tiracchānavijjaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃsupi vācesumpi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, learned and taught low arts. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – khipite āvuso “jīvā”tivadantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, was the offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who says, “May you live!” when one sneezes? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 2.0218] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, bhagavā bhante mahatiyā parisāya parivuto dhammaṃ desento khipi, bhikkhū “jīvatu bhante bhagavā, jīvatu sugatoti” uccāsaddaṃ mahāsaddaṃ akaṃsu, tena saddena dhammakathā antarā ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning a number of monks. The Blessed One, venerable sir, surrounded by a large assembly, was teaching the Dhamma when he sneezed. The monks made a loud and great noise, saying, “May the Blessed One live, venerable sir! May the Well-farer live!” Because of that noise, the Dhamma talk was interrupted. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Pucchā – ārāme āvuso passāvavaccānaṃ tahaṃ tahaṃ karaṇaṃ paṭikkhipitvā ekamantaṃ kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Where, friend, having prohibited urinating and defecating here and there in the monastery, did the Blessed One lay down that it should be done in a secluded place? Concerning whom, on what account, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū ārāme tahaṃ tahaṃ passāvaṃ akaṃsu, tahaṃ tahaṃ vaccaṃ akaṃsu, ārāmo dussi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Answer: It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, urinated and defecated here and there in the monastery. The monastery became defiled. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that account.

Khuddakavatthukkhandhaka

The Chapter on Minor Matters

Pucchā – khuddakavatthuṃ [M 2.0220] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I shall ask about the minor matters, with their origin and exposition. Friend, how many offenses are there in the principal sections?

Vissajjanā – khuddakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Answer: I shall answer regarding the minor matters, with their origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there are three offenses in the principal sections.

6. Senāsanakkhandhaka

6. The Chapter on Lodgings

Pucchā – pañca [M 2.0221] āvuso leṇāni bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question: Friend, where did the Blessed One allow the five kinds of dwellings? Concerning whom and on what occasion were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātāni, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā etadavocuṃ “rājagahako bhante seṭṭhī vihāre kārāpetukāmo, kathaṃ nu kho bhante paṭipajjitabba”nti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Answer: They were allowed, venerable sir, at Rājagaha concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, approached the Blessed One and said, “Venerable sir, the wealthy merchant of Rājagaha wishes to have dwellings built. How indeed should we proceed?” They were allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 2.0222] āvuso vihārā paṭhamaṃ uppannā, kathañca te patiṭṭhāpi tā.

Question: Friend, how did dwellings first arise, and how were they established?

Vissajjanā – ekāheneva [M 2.0223] bhante rājagahakena seṭṭhinā chaṭṭhivihārā patiṭṭhāpitā, te idha bhante saṭṭhivihārā buddhappamukhassa āgatānāgatassa cātuddisassa saṅghassa patiṭṭhāpitā.

Reply – In a single day, venerable sir, the wealthy merchant of Rājagaha established sixty dwellings. These sixty dwellings, venerable sir, were established here for the Sangha of the four quarters, present and future, with the Buddha at its head.

Pucchā – vihāre [M 2.0224] āvuso paṭibhānakammaṃ paṭikkhipitvā mālākammādīni bhagavatā kattha anuññātāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One, having prohibited decorative work in dwellings, allow garland-making and so forth? Concerning whom and on what occasion were they allowed?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātāni. Chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū vihāre paṭibhānacittaṃ kārāpesuṃ itthirūpakaṃ purisarūpakaṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātāni.

Reply – They were allowed, venerable sir, at Rājagaha concerning the group-of-six monks. The group-of-six monks, venerable sir, had decorative paintings made in the dwellings, figures of women and figures of men. They were allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – yathāvuḍḍhaṃ [M 2.0225] āvuso abhivādanādīni ca aggāsanādīni ca anujānitvā saṅghikaṃ yathāvuḍḍhaṃ paṭibāhantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who obstructs the Sangha's arrangements according to seniority, when salutations and so forth and chief seats and so forth have been permitted according to seniority? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – antarā ca bhante vesāliṃ antarā ca sāvatthiṃ chabbaggiyānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antevāsike bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyānaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ antevāsikā bhikkhū buddhappamukhassa saṅghassa purato purato gantvā vihāre paṭiggahesuṃ, seyyāyo paṭiggahesuṃ “idaṃ amhākaṃ upajjhāyānaṃ bhavissati, idaṃ amhākaṃ ācariyānaṃ bhavissati, idaṃ amhākaṃ bhavissatī”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, between Vesālī and Sāvatthī concerning the resident pupils of the group-of-six monks. The resident pupils of the group-of-six monks, venerable sir, went ahead of the Sangha with the Buddha at its head and took possession of dwellings and beds, saying, "This will be for our preceptors, this will be for our teachers, this will be for us." It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – ekena [M 2.0227] āvuso dve paṭibāhantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one obstructing two by himself? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante āyasmantaṃ upanandaṃ sakyaputtaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante upanando sakyaputto eko dve paṭibāhi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning the Venerable Upananda the Sakyan. The Venerable Upananda the Sakyan, venerable sir, alone obstructed two. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – navakena [M 2.0228] āvuso uddisantena samake vā āsane nisīdituṃ uccatare vā dhammagāravena, therena pana bhikkhunā uddisāpentena samake vā āsane nisīdituṃ nīcatare vā dhammagāravena bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was it allowed by the Blessed One for a junior monk, when reciting, to sit on an equal or higher seat out of respect for the Dhamma, and for an elder monk, when instructing, to sit on an equal or lower seat out of respect for the Dhamma? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū āyasmato upālissa santike vinayaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃsu, āyasmā bhante upāli ṭhitakova uddisati therānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ gāravena, tattha bhante therā ceva bhikkhū kilamiṃsu āyasmā ca upāli kilami, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – It was allowed, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, were learning the Vinaya in the presence of the Venerable Upāli. The Venerable Upāli, venerable sir, recited while standing, out of respect for the elder monks. There, venerable sir, both the elder monks and the Venerable Upāli became weary. It was allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – tivassantarena [M 2.0230] āvuso saha nisīdituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was it allowed by the Blessed One to sit together with a three-year interval? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulānaṃ bhante bhikkhūnaṃ etadahosi “kittāvatānu kho samānāsaniko hotī”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – It was allowed, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many monks. It occurred to many monks, venerable sir: "By what measure, indeed, is one a co-seater?" It was allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – mañce [M 2.0231] ca āvuso pīṭhe ca dvinnaṃyeva nisīdituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where did the Blessed One allow only two to sit on a couch or a stool? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule samānāsanike ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū samānāsanikā mañce nisīditvā mañcaṃ bhindiṃsu, pīṭhe nisīditvā pīṭhaṃ bhindiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many co-seaters. Many monks, venerable sir, who were co-seaters, sat on a couch and broke the couch, and sat on a stool and broke the stool. It was allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – dīghāsane [M 2.0232] panāvuso asamānāsanikehipi nisīdituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – But on a long seat, friend, where did the Blessed One allow even non-co-seaters to sit? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it allowed?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū dīghāsane asamānāsanikehi saha nisīdituṃ kukkuccāyiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Reply – It was allowed, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, felt scruples about sitting together with non-co-seaters on a long seat. It was allowed, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – pañca [M 2.0233] āvuso avissajjiyāni bhagavatā kattha paññattāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattāni.

Question – Friend, where were the five things not to be relinquished laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom and on what occasion were they laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattāni, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū saṅghikaṃ senāsanaṃ vissajjesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – They were laid down, venerable sir, at Sāvatthī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, relinquished Sangha lodgings. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – pañca [M 2.0234] āvuso avebhaṅgiyāni bhagavatā kattha paññattāni, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattāni.

Question – Friend, where were the five things not to be divided laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom and on what occasion were they laid down?

Vissajjanā – kīṭāgirismiṃ bhante assajipunabbasuke bhikkhū ārabbha paññattāni, assajipunabbasukā bhante bhikkhū saṅghikaṃ senāsanaṃ vibhajiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattāni.

Reply – They were laid down, venerable sir, at Kīṭāgiri concerning the monks Assaji and Punabbasu. The monks Assaji and Punabbasu, venerable sir, divided the Sangha's lodgings. They were laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – aññatra [M 2.0236] paribhogaṃ āvuso aññatra paribhuñjantassa dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one who uses for a different purpose that which is for another use? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āḷaviyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū aññatarassa upāsakassa vihāraparibhogaṃ senāsanaṃ aññatra paribhuñjiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, at Āḷavī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, used for a different purpose the lodging intended for the dwelling of a certain lay follower. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – adhotehi ca āvuso allehi ca pādehi senāsanaṃ akkamantassa ca saupāhanena senāsanaṃ akkamantassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one treading on lodgings with unwashed and wet feet, and for one treading on lodgings with sandals on? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āḷaviyaṃ [M 2.0237] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū adhotehi pādehi senāsanaṃ akkamiṃsu, allehi ca pādehi senāsanaṃ akkamiṃsu, saupāhanāpi senāsanaṃ akkamiṃsu, senāsanaṃ dussi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, at Āḷavī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, trod on the lodgings with unwashed feet, trod on the lodgings with wet feet, and also trod on the lodgings with sandals on. The lodgings became soiled. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – parikammakatāya āvuso bhūmiyā niṭṭhubhantassa ca parikammakataṃ bhittiṃ apassayantassa ca dukkaṭaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was an offense of wrong-doing laid down by the Blessed One for one spitting on prepared ground and for one leaning against a prepared wall? Concerning whom and on what occasion was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – āḷaviyaṃ [M 2.0238] bhante sambahuleva bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū parikammakatāya bhūmiyā niṭṭhubhiṃsu, parikammakataṃ bhittiṃ apassayiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Reply – It was laid down, venerable sir, at Āḷavī concerning many monks. Many monks, venerable sir, spat on prepared ground and leaned against a prepared wall. It was laid down, venerable sir, on that occasion.

Pucchā – senāsanakkhandhakaṃ pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – I shall ask about the Chapter on Lodgings, with its origin and exposition. Friend, how many offenses are there in the principal terms?

Vissajjanā – senāsanakkhandhakaṃ [M 2.0239] vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante tisso āpattiyo.

Reply – I shall answer regarding the Chapter on Lodgings, with its origin and exposition. Venerable sir, there are three offenses in the principal terms.

7. Saṅghatedakakkhandhaka

7. The Chapter on Schism in the Sangha

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 2.0240] āvuso channaṃ sakyakumārānaṃ saha upālikappakena pabbajjā ahosi, kathañca nesaṃ viseso udapādi.

Question – Friend, how did the going forth of the six Sakyan princes together with Upāli the barber come about, and how did their distinction arise?

Vissajjanā – bhagavā bhante upāliṃ kappakaṃ paṭhamaṃ pabbājesi, pacchā te sakyakumāre, atha bhante āyasmā bhaddiyo teneva antaravassena tisso vijjā sacchākāsi, āyasmā anuruddho dibbacakkhuṃ uppādesi, āyasmā ānando sotāpattiphalaṃ sacchākāsi, devadatto pothujjanikaṃ iddhiṃ abhinipphādesi. Evaṃ kho bhante channaṃ sakyakumārānaṃ upālikappakena saha pabbajjā ahosi, evañca pana bhante tesaṃ visesādhigamo ahosi.

The reply – The Blessed One, venerable sir, first ordained Upāli the barber, and afterwards those Sakyan princes. Then, venerable sir, the Venerable Bhaddiya realized the three knowledges in that very same rains retreat. The Venerable Anuruddha developed the divine eye. The Venerable Ānanda realized the fruit of stream-entry. Devadatta perfected ordinary mundane psychic power. Thus, venerable sir, the going forth of the six Sakyan princes together with Upāli the barber took place. And in this manner, venerable sir, was their attainment of special qualities.

Pakāsanīya

The Proclamation

Pucchā – pakāsanīyakammaṃ [M 2.0241] āvuso kātuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The question – Friend, where, concerning whom, and on what matter was the act of proclamation laid down by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante devadattaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, devadatto bhante “sarājikāya maṃ bhagavā parisāya kheḷāsakavādena apasādeti, sāriputtamoggallāneva ukkaṃsatī”ti kupito anattamano bhagavati āghātaṃ bandhi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Rājagaha concerning Devadatta. Venerable sir, Devadatta, being angry and displeased, harbored resentment towards the Blessed One, saying, 'The Blessed One disparages me before the royal assembly by calling me a "spittle-eater," and only praises Sāriputta and Moggallāna.' Venerable sir, it was laid down on that occasion.

Pakāsanīya

The Proclamation

Pucchā – devadattassa [M 2.0243] āvuso pakāsanīyakammaṃ kārāpetvā kathaṃ bhagavā pakāsetuṃ āṇāpesi.

The question – Friend, after having the act of proclamation performed regarding Devadatta, how did the Blessed One order it to be proclaimed?

Vissajjanā – bhagavā bhante devadattassa pakāsanīyakammaṃ kātuṃ paññapetvā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ āmantesi “tena hi tvaṃ sāriputta devadattaṃ rājagahe pakāsehī”ti, evaṃ kho bhante devadattassa pakāsanīyakammaṃ katvā devadattaṃ rājagahe pakāsetuṃ bhagavā āṇāpesi.

The reply – The Blessed One, venerable sir, having laid down the act of proclamation concerning Devadatta, addressed the Venerable Sāriputta: 'Well then, Sāriputta, you should proclaim Devadatta in Rājagaha.' Thus, venerable sir, having had the act of proclamation carried out concerning Devadatta, the Blessed One ordered Devadatta to be proclaimed in Rājagaha.

Devadatta

Devadatta

Pucchā – kathaṃ [M 2.0244] āvuso devadattena duṭṭhacittena vadhakacittena tathā gatassa ruhiraṃ uppādetvā paṭhamaṃ ānantariyaṃ kammaṃ upacitaṃ.

The question – Friend, how was it that Devadatta, with a wicked mind and murderous intent, having caused the Tathāgata's blood to flow, accumulated the first deed of immediate retribution?

Vissajjanā – bhagavā bhante gijjhakūṭassa pabbatassa chāyāyaṃ caṅkami, atha bhante devadatto gijjhakūṭaṃ pabbataṃ āruhitvā mahatiṃ silaṃ pavijjhi “imāya samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ jīvitā voropessāmī”ti, atha kho bhante dve pabbatakūṭāni samāgantvā taṃ silaṃ sampaṭicchiṃsu, tato papatikā uppatitvā bhagavato pāde ruhiraṃ uppādesi. Evaṃ kho bhante devadattena duṭṭhena vadhakacittena tathāgatassa ruhiraṃ uppādetvā paṭhamaṃ ānantariyakammaṃ upacitaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, the Blessed One was walking in the shade of Vulture Peak Mountain. Then, venerable sir, Devadatta climbed Vulture Peak and hurled a massive rock, thinking, 'With this, I will deprive the ascetic Gotama of life.' Then, venerable sir, two mountain peaks came together and intercepted that rock. A splinter flew off and caused blood to flow from the Blessed One’s foot. Thus, venerable sir, Devadatta, with his wicked and murderous intent, having caused the Tathāgata's blood to flow, accumulated the first deed of immediate retribution.

Pucchā – kulesu [M 2.0245] āvuso tikabhojanaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The question – Friend, where was the threefold meal among families laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and on what matter was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante devadattaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, devadatto bhante parihīnalābhasakkāro sapariso kulesu viññāpetvā viññāpetvā bhuñji, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down in Rājagaha concerning Devadatta. Venerable sir, Devadatta, having lost gains and honor, together with his following, repeatedly made known his needs and ate in families. It was in this context, venerable sir, that the rule was laid down.

Pucchā – alaṃ [M 2.0246] devadatta, mā te rucci saṅghabhedo, garuko kho devadatta saṅghabhedotiādiko āvuso ovādo bhagavatā kattha dinno, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ dinno.

The question – 'Enough, Devadatta! Do not delight in schism in the Sangha. For schism in the Sangha is grave, Devadatta.' Friend, where was the admonition beginning with this given by the Blessed One, and in what connection was it given?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante devadattaṃ ārabbha dinno, devadatto bhante saṅghabhedāya parakkami cakkabhedāya, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ dinno.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was given in Rājagaha concerning Devadatta. Venerable sir, Devadatta strove for schism in the Sangha and for disruption of the wheel. It was in this context, venerable sir, that it was given.

Alaṃ [M 2.0247] devadatta, mā te rucci saṅghabhedo, garuko kho devadatta saṅghabhedo –

Enough, Devadatta. Let not schism in the Sangha please you. Grave, indeed, is schism in the Sangha, Devadatta.

Pucchā – “sukaraṃ sādhunā sādhuṃ, sādhuṃ pāpena dukkaraṃ. Pāpaṃ pāpena sukaraṃ, pāpamariyehi dukkara”nti- āvuso [M 2.0248] idaṃ udānaṃ bhagavatā kattha udānitaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ udānitaṃ.

The question – 'Good is easy for the good to do; good is difficult for the evil to do. Evil is easy for the evil to do; evil is difficult for the noble to do.' Friend, where was this inspired utterance uttered by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and on what matter was it uttered?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante devadattaṃyeva ārabbha udānitaṃ, āyasmā bhante ānando pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya rājagahaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi, atha kho bhante devadatto yenāyasmā ānando tenupasaṅkami, upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ etadavoca “ajjatagge dānāhaṃ āvuso ānanda aññatreva bhagavatā aññatreva bhikkhusaṅghā uposathaṃ karissāmi saṅghakammaṃ karissāmī”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ udānitaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was uttered in Rājagaha concerning Devadatta. Venerable sir, the Venerable Ānanda, having dressed in the morning and taken his bowl and robe, entered Rājagaha for alms. Then, venerable sir, Devadatta approached the Venerable Ānanda and said, 'From today onward, friend Ānanda, I will perform the Uposatha and Sangha acts independently of the Blessed One and independently of the community of monks.' It was in this context, venerable sir, that the inspired utterance was spoken.

Sukaraṃ sādhunā sādhuṃ, sādhuṃ pāpena dukkaraṃ;

Pāpaṃ pāpena sukaraṃ, pāpamariyehi dukkaraṃ.

Good is easy for the good to do; good is difficult for the evil to do. Evil is easy for the evil to do; evil is difficult for the noble to do.

Pucchā – bhedānuvattakānaṃ [M 2.0249] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ thullaccayaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The question – Friend, where was the grave offense for monks who follow schismatics laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom was it laid down, and in what circumstance?

Vissajjanā – rājagahe bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, āyasmā bhante sāriputto bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā etadavoca “sādhu bhante bhedānuvattakā bhikkhū puna upasampajjeyyu”nti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down in Rājagaha concerning a number of monks. Venerable Sāriputta approached the Blessed One and said, 'Venerable sir, it would be good if monks who follow a schismatic were to be reordained.' In that instance, venerable sir, it was laid down.

“Sādhu [M 2.0252] bhante bhedānuvattakā bhikkhū puna upasampajjeyyu”nti –

'Venerable sir, it would be good if the monks who follow a schismatic were reordained.'

Pucchā – kittāvatā [M 2.0253] nu kho āvuso saṅgharāji hoti no ca saṅghabhedo, kittāvatā ca pana saṅgharāji ceva hoti saṅghabhedo ca.

The question – Friend, to what extent is there a faction in the Sangha but no schism, and to what extent is there both a faction in the Sangha and a schism?

Vissajjanā – ekato bhante eko hoti, ekato dve catuttho anussāveti, salākaṃ gāheti “ayaṃ dhammo ayaṃ vinayo idaṃ satthusāsanaṃ imaṃ gaṇhatha imaṃ rocethāti”, eteneva bhante nayena catunnaṃ vā pañcannaṃ vā channaṃ vā sattannaṃ vā aṭṭhannaṃ vā saṅgharāji hoti, no ca saṅghabhedo, ekato bhante cattāro honti, ekato cattāro, navamo anussāveti, salākaṃ gāheti “ayaṃ dhammo ayaṃ vinayo idaṃ satthusāsanaṃ imaṃ gaṇhatha imaṃ rocethā”ti, evaṃ kho bhante saṅgharāji ceva hoti saṅghabhedo ca. Evaṃ kho bhante saṅgharāji hoti, no ca saṅghabhedo, evañca pana bhante saṅgharāji ceva hoti saṅghabhedo ca.

The reply – Venerable sir, when there is one monk on one side and two on another, and a fourth announces, taking up the voting ticket, 'This is the Dhamma, this is the Vinaya, this is the Teacher’s instruction—accept this, approve of this,' then, venerable sir, in this way, among a group of four, five, six, seven, or eight, there is a faction in the Sangha, but not a schism. When there are four monks on one side and four on another, and a ninth announces, taking up the voting ticket, 'This is the Dhamma, this is the Vinaya, this is the Teacher’s instruction—accept this, approve of this,' then, venerable sir, in this way, there is both a faction in the Sangha and a schism. Thus, venerable sir, there is a faction in the Sangha, but not a schism, and thus, venerable sir, there is both a faction in the Sangha and a schism.

Pucchā – kittāvatā [M 2.0254] nu kho āvuso saṅgho bhinno hoti.

The question – Friend, to what extent is the Sangha split?

Vissajjanā – idha bhante bhikkhū adhammādiṃ dhammādīnīti dīpenti, te imehi aṭṭhārasahi vatthūhi apakassanti, avapakassanti, āveniṃ [M 2.0255] uposathaṃ karonti, āveniṃ pavāraṇaṃ karonti, āveniṃ saṅghakammaṃ karonti, evaṃ kho bhante saṅgho bhinno hoti.

The reply – Here, venerable sir, monks declare what is not Dhamma as Dhamma, what is Dhamma as not Dhamma, and so forth. Based on these eighteen grounds, they secede and draw others away. They perform the Uposatha separately, the Pavāraṇā separately, and Sangha acts separately. In this way, venerable sir, the Sangha becomes divided.

Pucchā – kittāvatā nu kho āvuso saṅgho samaggo hoti.

The question – Friend, to what extent is the Sangha united?

Vissajjanā – idha bhante bhikkhū adhammaṃ adhammoti dīpenti, dhammaṃ dhammoti dīpenti, avinayaṃ avinayoti dīpenti…pe… duṭṭhullaṃ āpattiṃ duṭṭhullā āpattīti dīpenti, aduṭṭhullaṃ āpattiṃ aduṭṭhullā āpattīti dīpenti, te imehi aṭṭhārasahi vatthūhi na apakassanti, na avapakassanti, na āveniṃ uposathaṃ karonti, na āveniṃ pavāraṇaṃ karonti, na āveniṃ saṅghakammaṃ karonti, ettāvatā kho bhante saṅgho samaggo hoti.

The reply – Here, venerable sir, monks declare what is not Dhamma as not Dhamma, and Dhamma as Dhamma; they declare what is not Vinaya as not Vinaya... they declare a gross offense as a gross offense, and a non-gross offense as a non-gross offense. Based on these eighteen grounds, they neither secede nor draw others away; they do not perform the Uposatha separately, nor the Pavāraṇā separately, nor Sangha acts separately. To this extent, venerable sir, the Sangha is in concord.

Pucchā – samaggaṃ [M 2.0256] āvuso saṅghaṃ bhinditvā kiṃ so pasavati.

The question – Friend, what does one generate by dividing a united Sangha?

Vissajjanā – samaggaṃ kho bhante saṅghaṃ bhinditvā kappaṭṭhitikaṃ kibbisaṃ pasavati, kappaṃ nirayamhi paccati.

The reply – Venerable sir, one who divides a united Saṅgha generates demerit lasting for an eon. For an eon, he is boiled in hell.

Pucchā – bhinnaṃ [M 2.0257] kho āvuso saṅghaṃ samaggaṃ katvā kiṃ so pasavati.

The question – Friend, having reunited a divided Saṅgha, what does one generate?

Vissajjanā – bhinnaṃ kho bhante saṅghaṃ samaggaṃ katvā brahmaṃ puññaṃ pasavati, kappaṃ saggamhi modati.

The reply – Venerable sir, one who unites a divided Saṅgha generates supreme merit, and rejoices in heaven for an eon.

Pucchā – saṅghabhedaṃ [M 2.0258] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

The question – I will ask about schism in the Saṅgha, with its basis and full explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there of the highest degree?

Vissajjanā – saṅghabhedaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante dve āpattiyo.

The reply – I will answer regarding schism in the Saṅgha, with its basis and full explanation. Venerable sir, concerning offenses of the highest degree, there are two.

8. Vattakkhandhaka

8. The Chapter on Observances

Pucchā – āgantukānaṃ [M 2.0259] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the observance for visiting monks laid down by the Blessed One? With reference to whom, and on what occasion, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule āgantuke bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, āgantukā bhante bhikkhū saupāhanāpi ārāmaṃ pavisiṃsu, chattapaggahitāpi ārāmaṃ pavisiṃsu, oguṇṭhitāpi ārāmaṃ pavisiṃsu, sīsepi cīvaraṃ karitvā ārāmaṃ pavisiṃsu, pānīyenapi pāde doviṃsu, vuḍḍhatarepi āvāsike bhikkhū nābhivādesuṃ, napi senāsanaṃ pucchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “tena hi bhikkhave āgantukānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ paññapessāmi, yathā āgantukehi bhikkhūhi sammā vattitabbaṃ, āgantukena bhikkhave bhikkhunā idāni ārāmaṃ pavisissāmīti upāhanā omuñcitvā nīcaṃ katvā papphoṭetvā gahetvā chattaṃ apanāmetvā sīsaṃ vivaritvā sīse cīvaraṃ khandhe katvā sādhukaṃ ataramānena ārāmo pavisitabbo” evamādinā bhante āgantukānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several visiting monks. Venerable sir, the visiting monks entered the monastery wearing sandals, holding umbrellas, with their heads covered, and with robes placed on their heads; they also washed their feet with drinking water. They did not pay respects to the senior resident monks, nor did they ask for lodgings. Regarding this matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One said: "Well then, monks, I will lay down the observance for visiting monks, so that visiting monks may behave properly. Monks, a visiting monk, when about to enter a monastery, should take off his sandals, put them down, shake them out, and pick them up; he should put away his umbrella, uncover his head, move the robe from his head to his shoulder, and enter the monastery carefully, without hurrying." In this way, venerable sir, the observance for visiting monks was laid down.

Pucchā – āvāsikānaṃ [M 2.0261] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ bhagavatā kattha kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ, kathañca pana paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where, concerning whom, and regarding what matter was the observance for resident monks laid down by the Blessed One? And how was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule āvāsike bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante āvāsikā bhikkhū āgantuke bhikkhū disvā neva āsanaṃ paññapesuṃ, na pādodakaṃ pādapīṭhaṃ pādakathalikaṃ upanikkhipiṃsu, na paccuggantvā pattacīvaraṃ paṭiggahesuṃ, na senāsanaṃ paññapesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ –

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several resident monks. Venerable sir, several resident monks, upon seeing visiting monks, neither prepared a seat, nor set out water for the feet, a footstool, or a foot towel. They did not go out to meet them, receive their bowls and robes, nor prepare lodging for them. Venerable sir, regarding that matter, it was laid down. Venerable sir, regarding that matter—

“Āvāsikena bhikkhave bhikkhunā āgantukaṃ bhikkhuṃ vuḍḍhataraṃ disvā āsanaṃ paññapetabbaṃ, pādodakaṃ pādapīṭhaṃ pādakathalikaṃ upanikkhipitabbaṃ, paccuggantvā pattacīvaraṃ paṭiggahetabbaṃ [M 2.0262] pānīyena pucchitabbo” evamādinā bhante āvāsikānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ bhagavatā paññattaṃ.

"Monks, when a resident monk sees a visiting monk who is his senior, he should prepare a seat, set out water for the feet, a footstool, and a foot towel, go out to meet him, receive his bowl and robe, and ask if he needs drinking water." In this way, venerable sir, the observance for resident monks was laid down by the Blessed One.

Pucchā – gamikānaṃ [M 2.0263] āvuso bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ, kathañcapana paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the observance for departing monks laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, on what occasion, and how was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule gamike bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, sambahulā bhante gamikā bhikkhū dārubhaṇḍaṃ mattikābhaṇḍaṃ appaṭisāmetvā dvāravātapānaṃ vivaritvā senāsanaṃ anāpucchā pakkamiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “gamikena bhikkhave bhikkhunā dārubhaṇḍaṃ paṭisāmetvā dvāravātapānaṃ thaketvā senāsanaṃ āpucchā pakkamitabbaṃ” evamādinā bhante ākārena bhagavatā gamikānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ vattaṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning several departing monks. Venerable sir, several departing monks left without putting away the wooden and clay utensils, leaving doors and windows open, and without asking leave regarding their lodging. In that matter, venerable sir, the Blessed One laid down the observance for departing monks in this way: "A departing monk, monks, should put away the wooden and clay utensils, close the doors and windows, and ask leave regarding his lodging before departing."

Pucchā – bhattagge [M 2.0264] āvuso anumodituṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was permission to give thanks in the refectory granted by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, and on what occasion, was it granted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ [M 2.0265] bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū bhattagge nānumodiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was permitted concerning several monks. Venerable sir, several monks did not give thanks in the refectory. In that matter, venerable sir, permission was granted.

Pucchā – bhikkhūnaṃ āvuso bhattaggavattaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the observance for monks in the refectory laid down by the Blessed One? With reference to whom, and on what occasion, was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū dunnivatthā duppārutā anākappasampannā bhattaggaṃ gacchiṃsu, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “sace ārāme kālo ārocito hoti, timaṇḍalaṃ paṭicchādentena parimaṇḍalaṃ nivāsetvā kāyabandhanaṃ bandhitvā saguṇaṃ katvā saṅghāṭiyo pārupitvā gaṇṭhikaṃ paṭimuñcitvā dhovitvā [M 2.0266] pattaṃ gahetvā sādhukaṃ ataramānena gāmo pavisitabbo”ti evamādinā bhante bhattaggavattaṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, the group-of-six monks went to the refectory poorly dressed, improperly covered, and lacking decorum. In that matter, venerable sir, the observance for the refectory was laid down thus: "If the time has been announced in the monastery, one should cover the three circles, wear the lower robe evenly, fasten the waistband, put on the outer robe properly folded, fasten the toggle, wash and take the bowl, and carefully enter the village without haste."

Pucchā – bhikkhūnaṃ [M 2.0267] āvuso senāsanavattaṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ, kathañca taṃ paññattaṃ.

Question: Friend, where was the observance for monks concerning dwellings laid down by the Blessed One? Concerning whom, on what occasion, and how was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante chabbaggiye bhikkhū ārabbha paññattaṃ, chabbaggiyā bhante bhikkhū bhikkhūsu ajjhokāse cīvaraṃ karontesu paṭivāte aṅgaṇe senāsanaṃ papphoṭesuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ “yasmiṃ vihāre viharati, sace so vihāro uklāpo hoti, sace ussahati sodhetabbo”ti evamādinā bhante bhikkhūnaṃ senāsanavattaṃ bhagavatā paññattaṃ.

The reply – At Sāvatthī, venerable sir, it was laid down concerning the group-of-six monks. Venerable sir, while some monks were making robes in an open space, the group-of-six monks shook out their bedding in the courtyard upwind of them. In that matter, venerable sir, the observance for monks concerning dwellings was laid down by the Blessed One thus: "In whatever monastery one resides, if that monastery is untidy, it should be cleaned if one is able."

Pucchā – samācāraṃ pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question: I will ask about proper conduct, with its basis and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the gravest category?

Vissajjanā – samācāraṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

The reply – I will answer concerning proper conduct, with its basis and explanation. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the gravest category.

9. Pātimokkhaṭṭhapanakkhandhaka

9. The Chapter on the Suspension of the Pātimokkha

Pucchā – sāpattikena [M 2.0270] āvuso pātimokkhaṃ suṇantassa pātimokkhaṃ ṭhapetuṃ bhagavatā kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was it laid down by the Blessed One that the Pātimokkha recitation may be suspended on account of an offender who is listening? Concerning whom and in what circumstance was it laid down?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante aññataraṃ puggalaṃ dussīlaṃ pāpadhammaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, aññataro bhante puggalo dussīlo pāpadhammo tadahuposathe saṅghamajjhe nisinno ahosi, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ.

The reply – Venerable sir, it was laid down at Sāvatthī concerning a certain unethical person of bad character. Venerable sir, on that Uposatha day, a certain unethical person of bad character was seated in the midst of the Saṅgha. It was laid down regarding that incident.

Pucchā – ṭhapanaṃ [M 2.0272] āvuso pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – Friend, I will ask about suspension, with its origin and explanation. Friend, how many offenses are there in the gravest category?

Vissajjanā – ṭhapanaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante ekā āpatti.

The reply – I will answer regarding suspension, with its origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there is one offense in the gravest category.

10. Bhikkhunikkhandhaka

10. The Chapter on Nuns

Pucchā – aṭṭhahi [M 2.0273] āvuso garudhammehi upasampadā bhagavatā kattha anuññātā, kassa anuññātā, kismiñca vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Question – Friend, where, for whom, and in what context was ordination by the eight important rules permitted by the Blessed One?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante mahāpajāpatiyā gotamiyā anuññātā, mahāpajāpati bhante gotamī sūnehi pādehi rajokiṇṇena gattena dukkhī dummanā assumukhī rudamānā bahidvārakoṭṭhake aṭṭhāsi, atha kho bhante āyasmā ānando mahāpajāpatiṃ gotamiṃ taṃ kāraṇaṃ pucchitvā bhagavantaṃ catukkhattuṃ yāci mātugāmassa tathāgatappavedite dhammavinaye āgārasmā anāgāriyaṃ pabbajjaṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātā.

Response – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Vesālī for Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī. Venerable sir, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī stood outside the gatehouse with swollen feet, her body covered with dust, miserable and distressed, with a tearful face, weeping. Then, venerable sir, the Venerable Ānanda, having questioned Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī about the reason, requested the Blessed One four times that women might go forth from the household life into homelessness in the Dhamma-Vinaya proclaimed by the Tathāgata. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Pucchā – bhikkhūhi [M 2.0275] āvuso bhikkhuniyo upasampādetuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was it permitted by the Blessed One for monks to give full ordination to nuns? Concerning whom and in what matter was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – vesāliyaṃ bhante mahāpajāpatiṃ gotamiṃ ārabbha anuññātaṃ, mahāpajāpati bhante gotamī bhagavantaṃ etadavoca “kathāhaṃ bhante imāsu sākiyānīsu paṭipajjāmī”ti, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Vesālī in connection with Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī. Venerable sir, Mahāpajāpatī Gotamī said this to the Blessed One: “How, venerable sir, should I conduct myself with regard to these Sakyan women?” Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Pucchā – ekato [M 2.0276] upasampannāya āvuso bhikkhunisaṅghe visuddhāya bhikkhusaṅghe upasampādetuṃ bhagavatā kattha anuññātaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Question – Friend, where was it permitted by the Blessed One for one who has been ordained on one side in the community of nuns and is purified, to be given full ordination in the community of monks? Concerning whom, and in what matter, was it permitted?

Vissajjanā – sāvatthiyaṃ bhante sambahule bhikkhū ārabbha anuññātaṃ, sambahulā bhante bhikkhū bhikkhunīnaṃ antarāyike dhamme pucchiṃsu, upasampadāpekkhāyo vitthāyiṃsu, maṅkū ahesuṃ, na sakkhiṃsu vissajjetuṃ, tasmiṃ bhante vatthusmiṃ anuññātaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, it was permitted at Sāvatthī in connection with several monks. Venerable sir, several monks questioned the candidates for ordination about the obstructing conditions. The candidates became embarrassed, the monks became discomfited, and they were unable to answer. Venerable sir, it was permitted in that matter.

Pucchā – bhikkhunikkhandhakaṃ [M 2.0277] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – Friend, I will ask regarding the Bhikkhunikkhandhaka, with its origin and explanation. Friend, how many principal offenses are there?

Vissajjanā – bhikkhunikkhandhakaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante dve āpattiyo.

Response – I will answer regarding the Bhikkhunikkhandhaka, with its origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there are two principal offenses.

11. Pañcasatikakkhandhaka

11. The Chapter of the Five Hundred

Paṭhama saṃgāyanā

The First Council

Pucchā – pañcasatikaṃ [M 2.0279] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – Friend, I will ask about the Five Hundred, with its origin and explanation. Friend, how many principal offenses are there?

Vissajjanā – pañcasatikaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante na katamā āpatti.

Response – I will answer regarding the Five Hundred, with its origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there is no principal offense.

12. Sattasatikakkhandhaka

12. The Chapter of the Seven Hundred

Dutiya saṃgāyanā

The Second Council

Pucchā – sattasatikaṃ [M 2.0280] pucchissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ āvuso kati āpattiyo.

Question – Friend, I will ask about the Seven Hundred, with its origin and explanation. Friend, how many principal offenses are there?

Vissajjanā – sattasatikaṃ vissajjissaṃ, sanidānaṃ saniddesaṃ. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ bhante na katamā āpatti.

Response – I will answer regarding the Seven Hundred, with its origin and explanation. Venerable sir, there is no principal offense.

Pucchā – kassa [M 2.0281] āvuso vacanaṃ.

Question – Friend, whose utterance is this?

Vissajjanā – bhagavato bhante vacanaṃ arahato sammāsambuddhassa.

Response – Venerable sir, it is the utterance of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.

Pucchā – ke āvuso sikkhanti.

Question – Friend, who train?

Vissajjanā – sekkhā ca bhante puthujjanakalyāṇakā ca sikkhanti.

Response – Venerable sir, both trainees and virtuous ordinary people train.

Pucchā – ke [M 2.0282] āvuso sikkhitasikkhā.

Question – Friend, who are those whose training is complete?

Vissajjanā – arahanto bhante sikkhitasikkhā.

Response – Venerable sir, Arahants are those whose training is complete.

Pucchā – kattha āvuso ṭhitaṃ.

Question – Friend, where is it established?

Vissajjanā – sikkhākāmesu bhante ṭhitaṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, it is established in those who desire training.

Pucchā – kenāvuso ābhataṃ.

Question – Friend, by whom was it brought?

Vissajjanā – paramparāya bhante ābhataṃ.

Response – Venerable sir, it has been handed down in succession.
